- **Endpoint:** Every authenticated product request, as #100 lists them, and appends for the queued-bytes axis; every product route for a reserved project's tokens. `bootstrap::run` installs `CellCeiling::of_config` (`src/auth/ceiling.rs`) through `AuthService::install_cell_ceiling` (`src/auth/publication.rs`, no longer test-only) right after `AuthService::new`, before the feed refresher publishes the first snapshot. Four settings (`src/config/cli.rs`): `PROJECT_SHARE_K` (default 1, at least 1, refused by the parser below that), `CELL_ENVELOPE_REQUESTS_PER_SEC`, `CELL_ENVELOPE_APPEND_BYTES_PER_SEC` and `CELL_ENVELOPE_READ_BYTES_PER_SEC` (default 0, no shared bound).
- **Condition:** A server started with `PROJECT_SHARE_K` > 1; the bounds are the three envelope settings, `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT`, the effective `SSE_MAX_CONNECTIONS`, the 65,536-entry stream maps (`REGISTRY_CACHE_MAX`, which `usage::MAX_TRACKED` is asserted to equal) and `MAX_UNABSORBED_BYTES_PER_INSTANCE`. On every server, k = 1 included: a feed policy naming the deployment's `PROJECT_ID`.
- **Before:** A production binary published every policy through the dedicated ceiling (k = 1, no bounds, only the system project reserved), so #100's ceilings and #101's `PROJECT_ID` and `ACCOUNT_ID` drops existed only in the test rigs, and the shared-cell profile's four names were read by `cell-admin` alone. Queued append bytes had no shared bound on any cell: a 0 quota meant no project limit up to the instance's 512 MiB unabsorbed line, past which every project's appends were shed, attributed to none.
- **After:**
  - With k > 1 each project's effective quota on each of seven bounded axes is min(feed, ⌊bound ÷ k⌋), never below 1, and a 0 or missing quota is exactly that: on the shared-cell profile 176 requests/s, 257,500 append B/s, 3,750,000 read B/s, 64 inflight, 150 subscriptions, 8,192 streams and 67,108,864 queued append bytes (`429 queued_bytes` past it). Append records/s keep the feed value.
  - A policy naming the deployment's `PROJECT_ID` is dropped on every cell, and one in a workspace equal to its `ACCOUNT_ID` on a cell with k > 1 (a dedicated cell's `ACCOUNT_ID` may be its customer's own workspace); their tokens get `421 wrong_cell`, and `GET /v1/debug/auth` `feeds.policies.reservedDropped` counts them.
  - k = 1, the default, keeps every quota as fed.
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged per code: the 429s keep their `Retry-After` behaviour (`queued_bytes` clears as the project's appends are decided); `421 wrong_cell` is not retryable at this cell.
- **Who is affected:** Projects on a cell started with `PROJECT_SHARE_K` > 1 (only the shared-cell profile sets it) whose feed sets 0, nothing or more than the share; `cell-admin` publishes every bounded quota explicitly at or below its ceiling, queued append bytes now included. A dedicated deployment whose feed serves a project under its own `PROJECT_ID`, the raw surface's tenant: that project's tokens now get `421 wrong_cell` (no feed in the tree does).
- **Given up:** As #100: a project cannot use more than its share while its neighbours are idle, and envelope values set too low cap honest tenants (the envelope stays pending re-measurement on the one-shard shape, README "Shared cells Q5").
- **Deployment comparison:** Four new settings, each defaulting to the old behaviour. The shared-cell profile sets them, as it did, and now states `MAX_UNABSORBED_BYTES_PER_INSTANCE=536870912`; the effective-configuration tool declares the four as added under this record (133 -> 137 leaves).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/auth/ceiling.rs::tests::boot_installs_the_ceiling_of_the_configured_cell (the profile's settings give the seven ceilings and drop `PROJECT_ID` and `ACCOUNT_ID`; a server with no settings keeps every quota)
  - src/auth/ceiling.rs::tests::a_shared_cell_holds_every_bounded_axis_to_its_share (the seventh axis) and ::reserved_identities_are_dropped_and_counted (k = 1 drops `PROJECT_ID`, not the account)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned; deploy/cell-admin/profile.test.ts, "every name it sets is one the server reads" (red on the unfixed tree: the four names unread)
  - Controls: the queued axis passed through, or the account reserved at k = 1: the named unit tests fail.
- **Risk reason:** medium: it makes #100's 429s and #101's 421s live in production, at values the operator sets through k and the bounds, and adds a seventh bounded axis. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written in this push's records commit and checked against 75d28313 (the installation before the first publication, both `#[cfg(test)]` gates gone, `reserving`'s k > 1 rule, the seventh axis, the settings and their pins) and 536558f3 (the six `crate::run` rows only).
- **Amendment (27caa95a, awaiting ratification):** "Who is affected" said no feed in the tree serves a project under its deployment's own `PROJECT_ID`. Two in-tree rigs did, through the platform emulator's fixture feed: CI's LiveFeed fleet certification (`bench/fleet/livefeed-cert.mjs`, job `livefeed-fleet-cert`) and the LiveFeed canary (`bench/canary/livefeed-canary.mjs`, an input of `scripts/rc-certify.sh`), each with `PROJECT_ID=proj-lc`, the project it certifies, so that `/v1/segments` (which resolves a name in the deployment's project only) reached its product streams. From 75d28313 that project's tokens get `421 wrong_cell`, and CI's certification failed its split leg on f53a39cc. 27caa95a gives the certification a deployment project of its own (`proj-lc-deploy`) and reads its split stream's segment map from the stream's stored descriptor; the canary keeps the old setting (not run here) and needs the same change. No server behaviour changes.

### #131 0638741e — The deploy wrapper keeps a running cell's auth feeds current, so a publish needs no restart, and deletes them after 120 s of awake time without a successful poll, so a cell that cannot see its feed bundle stops serving

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-08 (README, "Shared cells Q6" (a), option 4(b)): the wrapper polls before the first cell; the in-binary object source (4(a)) waits for the multi-server generation, and A9 stays red for it. It re-opens the owner's deployment gate "Compute validation of the wrapper -> binary -> platform lifecycle" (README "Shared cells Q7"). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Surface:** both (every request to a cell whose wrapper was deployed with `FEEDS_S3_KEY`: shared cells and the MT campaigns); process for the wrapper.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `deploy/app-server/feeds.ts` (`FeedPoller`, `s3BundleFetcher`), started by `deploy/app-server/index.ts` after the boot download; the server reads `STREAMS_AUTH_{KEYS,POLICY,GRANTS}_FILE` every `STREAMS_AUTH_REFRESH_SECS` (30 s), unchanged.
- **Condition:** A cell deployed with `FEEDS_S3_KEY`; the deletion, when no poll succeeds for 120 s of awake time, because the bundle's bucket is unreachable or because the bundle published there is not JSON or lacks a feed.
- **Before:** The wrapper downloaded the bundle once, at boot. A republished bundle (an admission, a revocation, an offboarding step) reached the cell only when it restarted, so every `cell-admin` change restarted the cell for every tenant; and a cell whose bundle became unreachable went on serving the feeds it booted with.
- **After:**
  - Every 15 s the wrapper GETs the bundle with `If-None-Match` (a presigned 60 s URL); a changed bundle is parsed whole and its three feeds rewritten atomically (tmp + rename), so a publish takes effect within about 45 s (the poll, then the server's refresh) without a restart. A bundle that is not JSON or lacks a feed is a failed poll: it replaces nothing, and every later poll fetches it again and fails again until a good bundle is published, so it counts towards the 120 s below exactly as an unreachable bucket does.
  - After 120 s of awake time without a successful poll the wrapper deletes the three files. The server keeps its last snapshots until their staleness window (300 s for policies and grants), then answers every product request `503 policy_stale` or `grants_stale` (`Retry-After: 1`, #102) until a poll succeeds and rewrites the files. The 120 s count awake time on a monotonic clock and start again at a wake (a gap of more than 30 s between polls), and only a poll's own failure deletes, so the first act after a sleep is a poll.
- **Retry semantics:** The refusals are the existing retryable 503s; they end within about 45 s of the bundle becoming reachable again, or of a good bundle replacing a malformed one.
- **Who is affected:** Every client of such a cell, when its bundle's bucket is unreachable, or a malformed bundle stays published, for more than about 7 minutes (120 s, then the 300 s window); operators, who no longer restart a cell to publish, and who must republish a good bundle at once after a bad one.
- **Given up:** Availability through a long outage of the bundle's bucket, or a malformed bundle left published: the cell fails closed rather than serve feeds it can no longer see revoked. Keeping the last good files on a malformed bundle (counting only an unreachable bucket) is the alternative left to the owner at ratification.
- **Deployment comparison:** No setting changes; the wrapper's behaviour changes for every cell deployed with `FEEDS_S3_KEY`. CI's Bun step runs `./deploy/feeds.test.ts`.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - deploy/feeds.test.ts: a changed bundle rewritten whole with its ETag sent next; deletion at the first failed poll 120 s after the last success and restoration by the next success; a ten-minute sleep restarts the count; a broken or partial bundle replaces nothing and, left published, deletes the feeds at the first failed poll 120 s after the last success, the next good bundle restoring them; the S3 fetcher sends `If-None-Match` and reads 304 (red before the module: `Cannot find module './app-server/feeds'`)
  - Controls: without the wake rule the sleep test fails; counting from the last poll instead of the last success fails the deletion test; counting a fetched but malformed bundle as a success fails the malformed-bundle test.
- **Risk reason:** medium: a new mode in which a whole cell refuses every request (its bundle's bucket unreachable, or a malformed bundle left published, for about 7 minutes), deliberately, and the wrapper's Compute lifecycle is not yet validated. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written in this push's records commit and checked against 0638741e.

### #125 c5da3a4e — Usage and retention count stored page bytes: frame_bytes, the storage gauge and the stored-byte totals measure layout 5 pages, after compression

- **Program item:** layout 5 (spec §7; the owner's decision of 2026-10-07: retention is billed on the bytes the server stores, after its compression; produce and consume stay on the bytes the customer sees).
- **Surface:** both (product usage answers, the usage ledger and rollup); operator-debug for `/v1/debug/usage` and `/v1/debug/load`.
- **Endpoint:** product `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage`, `/usage/current` and `GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage` (`ownedStoredBytesNow`, `storageByteSeconds`, `averageStoredBytes`, `gbMonth`, all from `owned_frame_bytes_current`); the usage ledger and rollup; debug `GET /v1/debug/usage` (`frame_bytes`, `compression_ratio`) and `GET /v1/debug/load` (`ingest_frame_bytes_total`, `absorbed_frame_bytes_total`, `unabsorbed_frame_bytes`); the read counters `read_frames_scanned` and `read_frames_matched`, which now count pages.
- **Condition:** any append.
- **Before:** the counters summed per-record frame bytes: 55 B plus the routing key per record, plus the record, zstd-1 only with `FRAME_COMPRESS=1`.
- **After:** they sum stored page bytes: one header per page (56 B plus the routing key), the request's records compressed together when that pays. For 1 KiB JSON in batches this is about 3 to 4 times smaller; for one-record requests and incompressible data about the same (a single-record page is 3 to 4 B larger than the frame was). Ingest (`ingestPayloadBytes`, `ingestRecords`) and read metering stay on plaintext payload bytes. The admission thresholds that count unabsorbed stored bytes (the 4 MiB absorb trigger, the 256/512 MiB backlog lines) now cover about 3 times more records of compressible data.
- **Retry semantics:** none.
- **Who is affected:** every customer's storage bill (lower for compressible batched data, about 1.04x for single-record requests), and anyone reading the debug counters as record bytes. The field names still say "frame" (`owned_frame_bytes_current`, debug `frame_bytes`, `read_frames_*`); renaming them is a further, separate edge change the owner has not decided.
- **Pinning tests:** `shard::billing_read_tests::the_retention_gauge_is_the_sum_of_the_stored_page_bytes` (the gauge is the sum of the four stored pages, ingest the payload bytes; the planted control "retention billed on payload bytes" turns it red); `shard::page_log_tests::a_request_is_stored_as_pages_keyed_by_their_last_offset` (`frame_bytes` equals the page bytes).
- **Risk reason:** medium. No request fails or changes its answer, but the meaning of every stored-byte counter, and so the storage line of every bill, changes: for compressible batched data it falls to about a third.
- **Check against commit:** written at landing from the layout 5 drafts (analysis/layout5-proto/edge-layout5.md, #NN-b); not checked by an independent pass. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call L1), as drafted, before landing.

### #123 412a560a — A consumer pull leases consecutive records of a routing key up to max, each under its own lease token, and the key stays blocked for every other pull while any of them is leased

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-07 (README, "Owner decisions", row "Consumer groups": "A pull may lease up to its `max` consecutive records of one routing key to one consumer while the key stays blocked for the group's other consumers; acks stay per record, in any order; unacked records return after their visibility window from the lowest unacked offset"; NEXT-WORK §14.2 item 1). A consumer says how many records it takes per pull (`max`), acks them one by one (`:settle` with per-record lease tokens), and may stop part-way. Until this change one pull took at most one record per routing key, so a producer that appends 1,000 records under one key was consumed one record per pull.
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:pull` (`product_consumer_pull`, `src/product/consumer_pull.rs`, then `application::consumer::delivery::pull`). The behaviour lives in the committer's Receive walk, `CommitTransaction::receive` (`src/shard/transaction/queue/receive.rs`). `POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:settle` is unchanged in code, but it now settles runs of a key instead of single records.
- **Condition:** All of these hold:
  - The pull's effective `max` is above 1. The effective `max` is the body's `max`, or the consumer's `maxBatchRecords` when the body has none (default 10, at most 1,000). The SDK iterator sends no `max`, so it pulls `maxBatchRecords`.
  - The segment holds two or more unsettled records of one routing key at or after the consumer's cursor, inside the pull's pre-read window (4 MiB of records from the cursor).
  - No earlier pull holds a live lease on any record of that key.

  Records appended without a routing key share one key, `stream_hash("")`, so an un-keyed queue meets the condition whenever two or more of its records are pending.
- **Before:** A pull leased at most one record per routing key: each lease blocked its key for the rest of the walk. Example: `max` 10 over records `a0 a1 b0` returned `[a0, b0]`, and over 1,000 records under one key it returned one record. An un-keyed queue delivered one record per pull, so a consumer group had at most one un-keyed record in flight. An expired record at `maxAttempts` was reported as poisoned in every walk that reached it.
- **After:**
  - **Leasing:** The walk goes from the cursor in offset order and leases every unsettled record whose key holds no live lease from an earlier pull, until `max`. One answer therefore holds runs: each key's records are consecutive in that key's order (acked records are skipped), and the runs of different keys interleave by offset. Example: `max` 10 over `a0 a1 b0` returns `[a0, a1, b0]`.
  - **Leases and tokens:** Each record keeps its own lease row (lease generation, attempts) and its own `leaseToken`, as before.
  - **Blocking:** While any record of a key holds a live lease, every other pull skips that key. The rule is unchanged, but it now covers the whole run.
  - **Settling:** Acks, retries and extends are per record and may come in any order (unchanged).
  - **Redelivery:** When the leases of a run expire, the next pull delivers the key's unacked records again from the key's lowest unacked offset, with `attempts` + 1 each, and the key's next fresh records follow, up to `max`. A record whose lease expired waits while any other record of its key is leased (for example one the consumer extended).
  - **Poison:** Poison is judged per record, on that record's own count, and only while its key is open (no live lease of the key remains). Within one Receive only the lowest poisoned record of a key is reported, and that report blocks the key for the rest of the Receive, so the pull's dead-letter handoff settles a key's records in offset order, one per Receive. A pull that leases nothing loops its handoff (`continue 'outer`) and so drains a poisoned run into the dead-letter stream within the same request; a pull that leases records of other keys returns them, and the next pull continues the drain. The settle's own handoff (a retry of a record at `maxAttempts`) is not ordered this way: it dead-letters the retried records in the order the request lists them (see "Who is affected").
  - **Isolation:** A record that has already been delivered twice is leased alone: the Receive blocks its key after leasing it, so that answer holds no later record of the key, and the key's later records wait while it is leased. A first redelivery (one crash, one expiry or one retry) keeps the run; a second one sends the key's lowest unacked record alone, and each of the run's records that was delivered with it twice follows alone once it is settled, before the key's fresh records form runs again. A failed record's successors therefore lose at most two attempts to its failure. (Option 2 of the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, item T4, in the variant that waits for a second delivery; the same push.)
  - **Unchanged:** `max` 1 behaves exactly as before. The stored format is unchanged: one lease row per offset, the same codecs, the same `LAYOUT_VERSION`.
  - **Code change:** In `CommitTransaction::receive` the line that blocked a key after each lease is gone, and the blocked-key check moves ahead of the lease lookup. That check skips every record of a key with a live lease, so the per-record in-flight check after it could no longer fire and is removed, and a poison report no longer advances the walk itself: it blocks the key, and the check skips the record on the next step (a poisoned record now costs the walk two of its `max` × 8 + 4,096 steps, where it cost one). The function's three exception contracts shrink: `scope_lines` 118 to 113, `syntax_facts` 220 to 213, `nested_items` 1, and no `expect_used` fingerprint grows (147 to 142 counted sites). Option 2 adds one check beside the poison check (block the key after leasing a record delivered twice): with it the contracts stand at `scope_lines` 115 and `syntax_facts` 217 against slate's 118 and 220.
- **Retry semantics:** No new error or status. A consumer that acks *i* of *n* run records and stops leaves the other *n* − *i* leased until their visibility expires (by default 30 s, the consumer's `visibilityTimeoutMs`); until then every pull skips the key, and afterwards the records are redelivered with `attempts` + 1. Tokens from the earlier delivery count as `stale` and never cause errors. A crash before a batch is settled costs every unacked record of that batch one attempt, as before; with runs those records are the key's successors instead of other keys' heads.
- **Who is affected:** Every product consumer that pulls with `max` above 1, including the SDK's `for await (const msg of consumer)` iterator.
  - **Throughput:** Hot-key and un-keyed queues deliver up to `max` records per pull instead of 1. `meter_pull` still counts delivered payload bytes and records, and the read quota still debits the framed body; both grow per pull, not per record. The usage counter `queue_operations` counts one per pull, so it counts fewer operations for the same records.
  - **Ordering, concurrent processing:** Within a consumer group one consumer at a time still holds a key, in offset order. A client that processes one answer's messages concurrently (for example `Promise.all` over `batch.messages`) now processes records of one key concurrently and loses per-key order; before, one answer never held two records of a key.
  - **Ordering, retries:** A retried record returns after its delay from the key's lowest unacked offset, once no record of its key is leased. A consumer that retries a record and then acks the key's later records of the same answer has processed them before the retried record: the retried record comes back after them. The SDK iterator does exactly that when a loop body calls `msg.retry()` and continues: it yields the next message, which may now be the same key's successor. Before, an answer held at most one record of a key, so a retry kept per-key order. Measured: a settle that acks `p1`, `p2` and retries `p0` of the run `p0 p1 p2` answers `acked` 2, `retried` 1, and the next pull returns `p0` at attempts 2.
  - **Ordering against poison isolation:** A consumer that keeps per-key order stops processing a key at a failed record and leaves (or retries) the key's later records of the answer. They come back with the failed record, each with `attempts` + 1, so when the failed record exhausts `maxAttempts`, its successors that were delivered with it every time have exhausted theirs too, and are dead-lettered (or dropped when no dead-letter stream is set) without having been processed. Before, a failed record never took a successor with it. Since "Isolation" above (option 2) a successor follows a failed record at most twice and is then leased alone after it, so with `maxAttempts` 3 or more (the default is 5) no record is dead-lettered for another record's failure; with `maxAttempts` 2 or 1 the trade-off remains, and a consumer can keep isolation by pulling with `max` 1, or by acking the successors it processed, which gives up per-key order. The cost of the isolation: a key whose lowest unacked record has been delivered twice is served one record per pull until its failed run is settled.
  - **SDK:** The iterator settles a batch once, after its last message, so a crash mid-batch loses every recorded ack of the batch; that was already true for a batch of different keys, and now covers a key's run.
  - **Visibility:** A run's leases share the pull's deadline. A consumer must finish all of its records within the visibility window or extend them; a slow consumer should pull fewer records or extend.
  - **Dead-letter drain:** A poisoned run of *n* records is dead-lettered one record per Receive. A pull that leases nothing loops until the run is drained, and each pass costs a pre-read of up to 4 MiB from the cursor, a Receive, one dead-letter append and one settle, inside one request whose `waitMs` does not bound this loop (#3 bounded it by the poisoned count of a Receive, which is now one per key). Measured on the in-memory DST rig (dev profile): one `waitMs` 0 pull dead-lettered 40 records in 0.71 s and 160 in 3.0 s. A run of `maxBatchRecords` 1,000 at the 100 ms write tier, where each dead-letter append and each settle waits for a durable commit, would keep one request busy for an estimated 100 to 200 s (not measured). Option 3 below removes it.
  - **Settle-path dead letters:** A settle that retries records at `maxAttempts` dead-letters them in the order the request lists them, and a failed copy does not stop that key's later records in the same settle. Before, the request could hold at most one leased record per key, so the order question did not arise. Measured: a settle that retries `p2`, `p0`, `p1` of one run at `maxAttempts` 1 writes the dead-letter copies `p2`, `p0`, `p1`.
  - **Specification:** The product surface spec already allows the shape: in `handover/prisma_streams_surface_spec_prelaunch_hard_cutover/02-CONSUMERS-AND-WATCHES.md` §2.3, "at most one record or contiguous batch for that key may be actively leased". The implementation was stricter.
  - **Documents describing the old rule:** not edited here, because parallel work touches them: `bench/k2cost/README.md` ("A consumer pull leases at most one message per routing key"), `bench/k2cost/k2gen.ts` (the `group` help line), and the header comments of `bench/k2cost/points/L5-full.env` and `bench/k2cost/points/L5-tiny.env`. K2 field plans with `--pull` N over K keys now deliver up to N per pull whatever K is (for example `bench/k2cost/field/plans/f5.plan`, `--pull 1000 --routing-keys 64`).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - New, committer level: `src/shard/transaction/queue/receive/run_lease_tests.rs`, seven tests on a raw `ShardEngine` with an in-memory store. They assert the exact leased and poisoned `(offset, lease generation, attempts)`, the stored lease rows, ack rows and the cursor row. Red on slate (1d93d3e0, and again on 44637490, whose `receive.rs` is the same), each at its first multi-record pull:
    - `a_pull_leases_a_run_of_one_key_up_to_max_under_one_lease_per_record`: left `[(0, 1, 1)]`, right `[(0, 1, 1), (1, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1), (3, 1, 1)]`
    - `a_leased_run_blocks_its_key_for_every_other_pull_until_its_last_ack`: left `[(0, 1, 1), (3, 1, 1)]`, right `[(0, 1, 1), (1, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1)]`
    - `a_crash_after_a_partial_ack_redelivers_from_the_lowest_unacked_offset`: left `[(0, 1, 1)]`, right `[(0, 1, 1) … (5, 1, 1)]`
    - `mixed_keys_lease_every_open_key_run_in_offset_order`: left `[(1, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1)]`, right `[(1, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1), (3, 1, 1), (5, 1, 1), (6, 1, 1)]`
    - `max_deliveries_poison_is_judged_per_record_inside_a_run`: left `[(0, 1, 1)]`, right `[(0, 1, 1), (1, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1)]`
    - `an_expired_record_waits_while_a_record_of_its_run_is_still_leased`: left `[(0, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1)]`, right `[(0, 1, 1), (1, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1)]`
    - `max_one_leases_one_record_per_pull_and_blocks_its_key_as_before`: green on both trees; it pins that `max` 1 is unchanged.
    - `a_record_delivered_twice_goes_alone_until_settled_and_its_run_resumes_after_it` (option 2, the same push): a run of four at `maxAttempts` 5 expires twice; the second pull redelivers the whole run (`(0..4, 2, 2)`: one failure keeps the run), the third leases only the head (`[(0, 3, 3)]`), each ack then releases exactly the next record alone at attempts 3, and after the last one the fresh records 4 and 5 come as a run; the lease rows say so. Red on the run-lease commit before option 2: left `[(0, 3, 3), (1, 3, 3), (2, 3, 3), (3, 3, 3)]`, right `[(0, 3, 3)]`.

    The two poison tests are also red on an intermediate tree with only the block-on-lease line removed and the check not moved: `max_deliveries_poison_is_judged_per_record_inside_a_run`, left `([], [(1, 2, 2), (2, 2, 2)])`, right `([], [(1, 2, 2)])`; `an_expired_record_waits_while_a_record_of_its_run_is_still_leased`, left `([], [(1, 1, 1), (2, 1, 1)])`, right `([], [(2, 1, 1)])`.
  - New, HTTP: `src/dst/tests/consumer_runs.rs`.
    - `a_pull_delivers_a_run_of_one_routing_key_with_one_lease_token_per_record`: four distinct tokens, a blocked second pull, per-record acks in any order, and the exact `acked`/`stale`/`backlog` of each settle. Red on slate: left `[("o", 0, 1), ("p", 0, 1)]`, right `[("o", 0, 1), ("o", 1, 1), ("p", 0, 1), ("o", 2, 1)]`.
    - `a_consumer_that_stops_part_way_gets_the_unacked_rest_again_in_offset_order`: acks 3 of 5 out of order; after expiry it receives exactly `o/2` and `o/4` at attempts 2, and the first tokens are `stale`. Red on slate: left `[("o", 0, 1)]`, right five records.
    - `a_run_that_reaches_max_attempts_is_dead_lettered_in_offset_order`: the dead-letter stream receives `p/0`, `p/1`, `p/2` in order, one per Receive. Red on slate: left `[("p", 0, 1), ("q", 0, 1)]`, right the three `p` records. Red on the intermediate tree: left `["p/0@1", "p/1@1", "p/2@1"]`, right `["p/0@1"]` (all three dead-lettered in one Receive while `q0` was returned).
  - Changed: `src/dst/tests/consumer_product.rs::product_consumer_per_key_fifo` (QUE-001; its name is kept for the scenario map and the relocation ledger). Its first pull asks `max` 1, and a second pull asks `max` 10 and must return only `b/0`, so "a/1 must be blocked behind a/0's active lease" now pins blocking across pulls. Unchanged, it fails on the fixed tree with left `[("a", 0), ("a", 1), ("b", 0)]`, right `[("a", 0), ("b", 0)]`.
  - Unchanged and green (dev profile): every `consumer_*` DST and every `shard::` test, and the whole lib suite once at the end (see the commit).
- **Risk reason:** medium. The server change is a reordering of one walk, with no stored-format change, and `max` 1 is unchanged. But every pull with `max` above 1 returns a different shape for hot keys and un-keyed queues, and three client assumptions break silently: concurrent processing of one answer loses per-key order; a retry followed by acks of the same answer's later records reorders the key (the SDK iterator's default); and a consumer that keeps order instead loses poison isolation when `maxAttempts` is 2 or 1 (from 3 up, option 2's isolation keeps a failed record's successors out of the dead-letter stream). A poisoned run drains one record per Receive inside one request. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the `queue_receive` mutation leg (CI selects it: on the diffed lines the function-body replacement and `>=` to `<` on the poison count, which the run-lease tests kill by reading (not run), and `&&` to `||` in the `let` chain, which does not compile), the SDK suite, the platform e2e and conformance.
- **Options for the owner** (decided 2026-10-08, "all recommended", item T4: options 2 and 4 now, option 3 later; option 1 stays as implemented):
  1. **Interleaving, as implemented:** runs of different keys interleave by offset. The alternative is the SQS FIFO style, which fills the batch from one message group first. Interleaving serves the stream head first and keeps the walk one pass; filling from one group gives a consumer longer single-key batches.
  2. **Poison isolation (implemented, the variant: "Isolation" under "After"):** lease a key one record at a time while its lowest unacked record is a redelivery, until that record is acked or dead-lettered; fresh records keep their runs. A successor then loses at most one attempt to its predecessor's failure, so with `maxAttempts` 2 or more no record is dead-lettered for another record's failure, which ends the trade-off between order and isolation above. The cost: after a consumer crash or restart, the unacked records of every run in flight are redelivered one per pull until the key reaches a fresh record. A variant waits for a second failure (single-step only a head whose delivery count is at least 2) and keeps runs after a single crash, but then a successor can lose two attempts, so isolation needs `maxAttempts` 3 or more (the default is 5). In the walk it is one check beside the poison check (block the key for the rest of the Receive when the record about to be leased was delivered before, or at least twice in the variant). The variant is prepared outside the tree as a patch with a red-first committer test (red: left `[(0, 3, 3), (1, 3, 3), (2, 3, 3), (3, 3, 3)]`, right `[(0, 3, 3)]`; every `shard::` and `consumer_*` test green with it); in the same push as this record the function's contracts stay at or below slate's (115 lines, 217 facts), while as a later push they grow against this record's (113, 213) and need a growth row.
  3. **Dead-letter drain:** report a key's consecutive poisoned records in one Receive, and stop the handoff for a key at its first failed copy, so a poisoned run drains in one pass in offset order; apply the same per-key rule to the settle's handoff. This changes `dlq_and_settle` (`src/application/consumer/delivery.rs`), whose two exception contracts are frozen by approved growth rows (`scope_lines` 134, `syntax_facts` 194), so the owner updates those rows. A smaller step is to bound the pull's handoff loop by its `waitMs` deadline; that changes `pull`, an `#[expect]` scope at 195 of its 200 architecture lines, and needs a growth row too, and on its own it makes a `waitMs` 0 pull answer empty after the first dead letter.
  4. **SDK (documented in the same push; the iterator change is not made):** document in `sdk/README.md` ("Consumer batches and per-key order") and `PullOptions.max` that one batch can hold a key's run, so a batch must be processed in order; and make the iterator skip the rest of a key's messages in the batch after `msg.retry()` (retrying them too), which keeps per-key order for SDK users and needs option 2 to keep isolation. Optionally settle each message as it completes instead of once per batch.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change; amended by the option-2 commit of the same push ("Isolation", the ordering bullet, the new pinning test, "Risk reason" and the options' status) and by its SDK documentation commit (option 4's status). An adversarial self-review of 2026-10-08 (notes outside the tree) corrected the draft before landing: the draft said the SDK iterator's order was unaffected (a retry followed by its successors reorders the key), called the crash cost new (its size is unchanged; its kind changed), claimed offset-ordered dead letters for the settle's handoff too, and did not state the drain's cost or the trade-off between order and poison isolation. The review also removed the in-flight check that the moved blocked-key check had made unreachable (its mutants were equivalent) and the poison branch's own step, which the check already makes. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.

### #120 137dfee7 — A fleet that does not set INITIAL_SHARDS starts a fresh topology with the largest power of two at most FLEET_MAX shards, not next_pow2(4 × FLEET_MAX), and a fleet that sets more shards than FLEET_MAX is warned at boot

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-07 (README, "Owner decisions", row "Shards and WAL writers": as few shards as possible; NEXT-WORK §14.4: the fleet-mode default "becomes the largest power of two at most `FLEET_MAX`, and the `CoarseInitialShards` notice inverts"). The routing proposal's K1 bundles this with the `home-v1` placement; `home-v1` is not part of this record, so placement is still the rendezvous draw. Performance acceptance stays with the owner (AGENTS.md §1).
- **Surface:** process: a startup setting's default and one boot warning (operator-debug for the log line). A client observes it only through placement: which server holds how much of a fresh fleet cell's keyspace, and how much of it one shard move or failover reopens at once.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `ServerConfig::validate_topology_and_ceilings` (`src/config/validation.rs`) resolves the effective initial shard count through the new `fleet_shard_default`; `load_or_init_topology` (`src/registry.rs`) writes it into `topology.json` only when the data namespace has no stored topology. The notice is `ConfigNotice::ShardsExceedFleetMax` (`src/config/notice.rs`, which replaces `CoarseInitialShards`); bootstrap logs it at WARN once the whole configuration is accepted.
- **Condition:** For the default: fleet mode (`FLEET_PREFIX` set and `FLEET_MAX` above 1), `INITIAL_SHARDS` unset, and a data namespace (`PATH_PREFIX`) with no stored topology. For the warning: fleet mode and an explicit `INITIAL_SHARDS` above `FLEET_MAX`. A server out of fleet mode is unchanged (default 1, never a notice), and a stored topology wins over either value, as before.
- **Before:** Unset: `next_pow2(4 × FLEET_MAX)`, so 8 shards for `FLEET_MAX=2`, 16 for 3 and for 4 (the default `FLEET_MAX`), 32 for 5-8 and 16,384 for 4,096. An explicit value below `4 × FLEET_MAX` was warned: `INITIAL_SHARDS=N < 4×FLEET_MAX=M: a fresh topology this coarse draws unevenly under rendezvous and the rebalancer flaps against return-home; use >= S`.
- **After:** Unset: the largest power of two at most `FLEET_MAX`, so 2 for 2 and 3, 4 for 4-7, 8 for 8-15 and 4,096 for 4,096. An explicit value is used as before and is warned only when it exceeds `FLEET_MAX`: `INITIAL_SHARDS=N > FLEET_MAX=M: a fresh topology this fine runs more WAL writers than servers, and every busy writer pays its own WAL PUTs whatever it carries; use <= S, the largest power of two at most FLEET_MAX (the fleet default)`. A value at most `FLEET_MAX`, however small, is not warned.
  - Placement is still the rendezvous draw (`ring_pick`, `src/ownership.rs`). Computed with its formula over `streams-1..n` and the topology's prefixes (a scratch computation, not a test), with every server active: the default draws `[0, 2]` for `FLEET_MAX=2` (was 8 shards, `[4, 4]`), `[0, 1, 1]` for 3 (was 16, `[8, 4, 4]`) and `[1, 1, 0, 2]` for 4 (was 16, `[4, 3, 1, 8]`); 4 shards over 5 servers draw `[1, 1, 0, 1, 1]`. So a two-server fleet's whole keyspace is on `streams-2`, and in a four-server fleet `streams-3` holds nothing and `streams-4` half, as `streams-4` held half of 16 before. The rebalancer moves a shard off a server only once that server's absorber lags more than `REBALANCE_LAG_SECS` (60 s) for two ticks; the load-aware return-home then keeps the move. `home-v1` is what makes the draw one shard per server.
  - The draws while an autoscaled fleet grows, computed the same way over each active ring `streams-1..k` (`FLEET_MIN` defaults to 1, so a fleet starts with one active server and the autoscaler adds the next ordinal under load). With `FLEET_MAX` 2 or 3 (2 shards) the step from one server to two moves both shards to `streams-2`: the whole keyspace reopens at once on the new, cold server, `streams-1` holds nothing, and the step adds no write capacity; the old default's 8 shards drew `[4, 4]` there and moved half. A third server then draws `[0, 1, 1]`. With `FLEET_MAX=4` (4 shards) the steps draw `[2, 2]`, `[2, 1, 1]` and `[1, 1, 0, 2]`, moving 2, 1 and 2 of the 4 shards: the step from three servers to four moves `streams-3`'s one shard and one of `streams-1`'s to `streams-4`, so the fourth server leaves `streams-3` idle (the old 16 shards drew `[8, 8]`, `[8, 4, 4]` and `[4, 3, 1, 8]`). An autoscaler that then sees an idle server may scale in again; plausible, not verified.
  - One shard is a larger share of the keyspace (a quarter of a four-server cell instead of a sixteenth): a move or a failover reopens that share at once, and its streams get the opening `503` with `retry-after` while the new owner replays that shard's WAL.
  - A server holding one shard runs one SlateDB, one committer and one WAL pump for all its streams, with one `MAX_UNFLUSHED_BYTES` budget (16 MiB) where four shards had four, and at most about 9.9 WAL flushes a second (#97) where four shards could each flush that often. Not measured.
  - No status, header, body or error code changes.
- **Retry semantics:** None changed. The opening `503` keeps its `retry-after`.
- **Who is affected:** Fleet deployments that create a fresh topology without setting `INITIAL_SHARDS`. Not the launch shape (one single-server cell, fleet off), the in-process DSTs (their rigs build topologies directly; none resolves this default), nor a namespace whose topology is stored (the fleet campaign's `fleetd2` keeps its 16 shards until `FLEET_DATA_PREFIX` names a fresh one).
  - Pinned until `home-v1` placement exists (the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, item T3: a fleet rig keeps the shard count its placement was measured with, because the rendezvous draw spreads the new default unevenly): `bench/fleet/local-fanout.sh` (and `ci-fanout.sh`) sets `INITIAL_SHARDS=8`, its old default for `FLEET_MAX=2` (since the rigs commit before #119, which also needs eight shards for the topology its split phase creates outside fleet mode); `bench/fleet/deploy-fleet.sh`, the two effective-configuration families that mirror it (`fleet-server-1`, `fleet-server-n`) and RUNBOOK §7's docker fleet recipe keep 16 over `FLEET_MAX=4`. All are warned now (above `FLEET_MAX`). RUNBOOK §3.2 states the new default and that the fleet rigs pin their own counts until `home-v1`.
  - Explicit values whose topology is unchanged and that are warned before and after (below `4 × FLEET_MAX` before, above `FLEET_MAX` now): `bench/fleet/livefeed-cert.mjs` (CI's `livefeed-fleet-cert`) and `bench/canary/livefeed-canary.mjs` (8 over `FLEET_MAX=3`), `bench/docker/compose.yml` (8 over the default 4), `bench/docker/harness/cluster-deploy.sh` (8 over 4), `bench/k2cost/run-local.sh` with `SERVERS=2` (4 over 2), and the k2cost field cells with one server (`f3-*`, `smoke-single`, `smoke-wake`: 4 over the harness's `FLEET_MAX=2`). `scripts/effective-config/boot.py` classifies both texts by the prefix `INITIAL_SHARDS=`.
  - Warned before and not now: the k2cost field cells with four or six servers and 4 shards (`f1a`, `f1b`, `f1c`, `f5`, `smoke-launch`).
  - `deploy/profiles/compute-1g.env` does not set `INITIAL_SHARDS` and is unchanged.
- **Given up:** Spread. At the old default a two-server fleet's keyspace drew evenly (`[4, 4]`), a fleet growing from one server to two moved half of it, and a shard move relocated a sixteenth of a four-server cell. Gained: a fresh fleet cell runs at most one WAL writer per server, the floor of the WAL bill (NEXT-WORK §14.4: each busy writer costs up to about $97 a month at the 100 ms tier, whatever it carries).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/validation_tests.rs::validate_boundary_tests::the_fleet_mode_shard_default_is_the_largest_power_of_two_at_most_fleet_max (new; red on the unchanged code: left `[(1, 1, 0), (2, 8, 0), (3, 16, 0), (4, 16, 0), (5, 32, 0), (7, 32, 0), (8, 32, 0), (9, 64, 0), (4096, 16384, 0)]`, right `[(1, 1, 0), (2, 2, 0), (3, 2, 0), (4, 4, 0), (5, 4, 0), (7, 4, 0), (8, 8, 0), (9, 8, 0), (4096, 4096, 0)]`, as (`FLEET_MAX`, resolved shards, shard notices))
  - src/config/validation_tests.rs::validate_boundary_tests::an_initial_shard_count_above_fleet_max_warns_and_one_at_most_it_does_not (new; red on the unchanged code: the old warning for 1, 2, 4 and 8 over `FLEET_MAX=4`, none for 16, the old warning for 4 and 8 over 5 and for 4 over 3; green: the exact new text, as a warning, for 8 and 16 over 4, 8 over 5 and 4 over 3, none for 1, 2 and 4 over 4 or 4 over 5, none out of fleet mode, and the configured count used every time)
  - It replaces `validation_resolves_the_fleet_mode_shard_default`, which pinned 16 for `FLEET_MAX=3`.
  - Not pinned: the rendezvous draw at the new default, full or while the ring grows, and any rig that boots a fleet.
- **Risk reason:** medium. No answer changes shape, and nothing changes for a server out of fleet mode or for a stored topology. But a fresh fleet cell built on the default now holds a quarter of its keyspace per shard and, until `home-v1`, leaves servers without a shard: a two-server fleet serves all its writes from one server until its absorber lag moves a shard, and an autoscaled fleet of `FLEET_MAX` 2 or 3 that grows from one server to two reopens its whole keyspace at once on the new server; so under load requests that the spread used to serve can meet that server's admission cap (429 `overloaded`, retryable), and a move reopens a whole quarter or half of the keyspace (`503` with `retry-after`). There are no users yet and the launch shape is fleet off. WIRE-MATRIX is unchanged: it states no shard count, and its limits are per stream. Run locally on the tier100 branch's tip 4cd06ad4 (slate a2ae3d57), not on this push's tip, every one with its pinned count: the fleet certification, the canary and the fan-out rig (see #119). Not run for this record: those three rigs on this push's tip (CI's `livefeed-fleet-cert` job runs the certification on the push), `scripts/gate.sh`, the release lib suite, the docker ladder and any field run; no formal receipt is staled (`src/config/*`, RUNBOOK and the bench scripts feed none). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent read-only review of the branch (2026-10-08, finding F4) found that the record gave the rendezvous draws only for a full ring. Corrected the same day: "After" adds the draws while an autoscaled fleet grows, recomputed with `ring_pick`'s formula over each active ring (a scratch computation, not a test), and "Given up" and "Risk reason" name the step from one server to two. Numbered #101 on its branch, renumbered #122 when the branch was rebased onto slate a2ae3d57 and #120 in the landing order of 2026-10-08 (rebased onto cb518e57), with the owner's decision of 2026-10-08 (T3) applied: the branch had moved `bench/fleet/deploy-fleet.sh`, its two families and RUNBOOK §7's recipe from 16 shards to the new default 4; they keep 16 until `home-v1`, and "Who is affected" says so. A review of the landing chain (2026-10-08, finding F2) found "Risk reason" saying the three rigs ran on this push's tip; they ran on the tier100 branch's tip, and "Risk reason" says so.

### #119 894c9595 — While the server runs in a fleet the scaler splits no stream: a hot stream stays one segment on its owner, and its appends past the per-stream limit are answered 429 where a split used to admit part of them on another server

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-07 (README, "Owner decisions", row "Launch shape and routing": "Cross-server stream splits are switched off until the split package lands before public launch"; NEXT-WORK §14.6). The split package's last commit removes the gate, with its two-server DST written red first.
- **Surface:** both: the appends of the raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}` and the product `POST /v1/streams/{name}/records` and `records:batch` to a hot stream, and what a split used to change for that stream's readers. Also operator-debug: one log line per declined split.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `Controller::execute` (`src/scaler3/controller.rs`), the scaler's only production path to a split, first asks `Controller::declines`, which reads `ShardDirectory::ring_published` (`src/shard_directory.rs`): whether the fleet loop has published an active set (`OwnershipService::set_view`, `src/fleet.rs`), of any size. Once published the set is never empty again (`planning::active_members` falls back to the ordinal set when its filter empties it), so in a fleet the gate holds from the fleet loop's first complete pass for the life of the process.
- **Condition:** Fleet coordination on (`FLEET_PREFIX` set: the fleet loop runs whatever `FLEET_MAX` is, and publishes a ring of one at `FLEET_MAX=1`) after its first complete pass, and a segment the scaler decides to split: above `SCALE_HOT_PCT` (75%) of a per-stream limit for `SCALE_HOT_EVALS` (2) evaluations, not dominated by one key, each predicted child carrying at least 15% of the load, past the cooldown. A server with fleet off publishes no ring and splits as before.
- **Before:** The controller split the segment at its load-weighted median: the intent, the parent sealed through its committer, two children published.
  - The low child keeps the parent's route. The high child's route is salted until it lands on another shard prefix whenever the topology has one (in fleet mode at least two), and in a fleet that is usually a shard another server owns. In the new DST's red run (two one-bit shards, one per server) the high child landed on `inst-b`'s shard.
  - A ring of one active server (an autoscaled fleet at `FLEET_MIN=1` before it scales out, a peer draining or dark, a fleet of one) did not prevent it: the split committed on that server, and once the ring grew, rendezvous could give the high child's shard to the new server. In the amended DST's red run a split on the ring `{inst-a}` left the high child `inst-b`'s once the ring grew to `{inst-a, inst-b}`.
  - Appends of keys in the high half then belonged to that server: the parent's owner answers them 409 with `Streams-Replay-To` (raw `not_ring_owner`, product `not_stream_owner`; appends are not relayed), and the server holding the child admits them from its own bucket for the stream. Each server holding a child added up to one more per-stream limit.
  - Readers got segmented offsets and the lineage (the sealed parent, then the children).
  - On the other server, consumer pulls never delivered the high child (B1), an ambiguous producer retry across the split could commit twice (B2), watches missed it (B3), and the two children never merged, because a merge is proposed only by a server that sketches both.
- **After:** The controller declines the decision and keeps no debt (the pass counts it attempted, not completed, not deferred). It logs `split declined: the server runs in a fleet` at info, with the project, stream, epoch and segment.
  - The scaler holds the stream's cooldown (`SCALE_COOLDOWN_SECS`, 600 s) after each decision and decides again after it while the stream stays hot, so a hot stream is declined, and logged, about once per cooldown.
  - The stream stays one segment on its owner: one bucket and plain offsets. Its appends past the bucket get raw 429 `limit_bytes_per_sec`, `limit_requests_per_sec` or `limit_records_per_sec` with the computed `retry-after`, product 429 `rate_limited` (retryable), as any stream the scaler had not yet split always did.
  - Not gated: merges; a split whose intent was written before the gate held (reads and appends resume it through the request work, as before); and a fleet server between its start and its fleet loop's first complete pass, while its ring is empty (it then serves every shard, as before). The scaler decides a split only after `SCALE_HOT_EVALS` evaluations, 10 s apart by default, so that window matters only while the fleet store stays unreadable that long. A stream split while its cell ran with fleet off keeps its children if the cell later runs as a fleet; this change does not merge it.
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged per answer: the 429 is retryable with `retry-after`. A client above its stream's limit keeps meeting 429s where, after a split, part of its load would have been admitted on another server.
- **Who is affected:** Every fleet deployment (`FLEET_PREFIX` set), whatever its size or active count. Not the launch shape (one single-server cell, fleet off), the single-server rigs with fleet off (`bench/costab/run-split.sh`, CI's `product-field-gate`) or the in-process DSTs, whose rigs publish no ring or call the transitions directly.
  - Single-server rigs in fleet mode split no more: `bench/k2cost/run-local.sh` with `SERVERS=1` (`FLEET_PREFIX` with `FLEET_MAX=1`) and the k2cost field cells with one server (`f3-*`, `smoke-single`, `smoke-wake`, `FLEET_MAX=2`). Their scaler settings are production's (75%, 2 evaluations, 600 s); whether a k2cost point relied on a split is for the experiment's owner to judge.
  - **CI's `livefeed-fleet-cert` job** (`bench/fleet/livefeed-cert.sh`) runs three instances with `FLEET_MIN=3` and needs a real scaler split for its leg `real scaler split lands (multi-segment, nothing pending)` and for the lineage legs after it. Since the previous commit (the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, item T2, option 1') it takes that split on `streams-1` booted outside fleet mode, where no ring is published and this gate does not apply, stops it, and boots all three in fleet mode over the same bucket; its legs then certify LiveFeed over a lineage whose segments the fleet spreads over its servers, the state a cell that split before it became a fleet reaches.
  - The LiveFeed field canary (`bench/canary/livefeed-canary.mjs`) builds and splits its blackhole campaign's stream the same way before its fleet forms.
  - The docker ladder's D1, D2 and D4 (`bench/docker`) split on `streams-1` alone outside fleet mode (`compose.solo.yml`, `harness/phases.sh`) and run their order checks through the fleet formed afterwards; SCALING §8 says so.
  - The Compute cluster rung C1 (`bench/docker/harness/cluster-run.sh`, SCALING §8) takes its splits the same way: `cluster-deploy.sh solo` deploys streams-1 alone outside fleet mode, `cluster-run.sh solo` drives C1's load there, then `cluster-deploy.sh up` redeploys all four in fleet mode over the same PATH_PREFIX and `cluster-run.sh` checks C1's order through the fleet (the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, T7; a script change, the field run stays the owner's).
  - `bench/fleet/local-fanout.sh` (and `ci-fanout.sh`) splits its probe stream on `streams-1` outside fleet mode before both servers form the fleet. `bench/fleet/deploy-fleet.sh` splits nothing once two servers are active; it waits for no split.
- **Given up:** A hot stream's admitted rate above its per-stream limit in any fleet, until the split package lands. Gained: no stream enters the cross-server states in which B1-B3 lose deliveries, commit a retried append twice or miss a watch, also not when a fleet that had one active server grows.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/scaler_split_gate.rs::a_hot_stream_on_a_two_server_ring_never_splits_and_meets_its_limit (new; red on the unchanged controller: `the controller declines the split on a two-server ring, and keeps no debt`, left `(1, 1, 0)`, right `(1, 0, 0)`; green: no stored map, no `split_committed`, the next byte answered 429 `limit_bytes_per_sec` with `retry-after: 1`, the stream holding exactly the ten admitted bytes, and `inst-b` holding no shard)
  - src/dst/tests/scaler_split_gate.rs::a_hot_stream_on_a_one_server_ring_never_splits_so_a_grown_ring_keeps_it_on_its_owner (the amendment's; it replaces the control `a_hot_stream_on_a_one_server_ring_still_splits`. Red on the ring-size gate: `the controller declines the split on a one-server ring and keeps no debt, so the grown ring leaves the whole stream on its owner`, left `((1, 1, 0), ["inst-a", "inst-b"])`, right `((1, 0, 0), ["inst-a"])`; green: no stored map and no `split_committed`)
  - src/dst/tests/scaler_split_gate.rs::a_hot_stream_on_a_server_without_a_fleet_ring_still_splits (the amendment's control, green before and after: with no ring the same decision commits, leaving a sealed parent, two live children and one `split_committed`)
  - src/shard_directory.rs::directory_tests::the_ring_is_published_once_the_fleet_names_a_member (no ring, one, two and three members: `[false, true, true, true]`; it replaces `the_ring_spans_servers_only_with_two_active_members`)
  - src/dst/tests/scaler_loop.rs::the_loop_splits_a_hot_segment_at_its_load_weighted_median (unchanged, green: a server with no ring splits)
  - Not pinned: the scaler loop on a fleet ring end to end, the window before the fleet loop's first pass, and any rig that starts the binary (the rigs that need a split take it outside fleet mode; see "Who is affected").
- **Risk reason:** medium. No answer changes shape, and the 429 is the per-stream limit's existing retryable answer, but every fleet, including one with a single active server, now answers 429 to part of a hot stream's load that it served after a split. The CI gate, the field canary, the docker ladder and the fan-out rig that relied on such a split take it outside fleet mode before their fleet forms (the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, T2, option 1'). WIRE-MATRIX §1.2 now says the limit is one bucket per stream on each serving process and that a fleet splits nothing. Run locally on the tier100 branch's tip 4cd06ad4 (on slate a2ae3d57; release binaries, 2026-10-08), whose rig scripts differ from this push's only in the edge-record number their comments cite: CI's fleet certification (`bash bench/fleet/livefeed-cert.sh`: `LIVEFEED_FLEET_CERT_OK`, 29 legs, 27 run and 2 covered in process, its split taken outside fleet mode), the LiveFeed canary (`LIVEFEED_CANARY_OK`, 28 legs) and the fan-out rig (`bench/fleet/ci-fanout.sh`: `ALL CHECKS PASSED`). Not rerun on this push, whose server also carries the slate commits after a2ae3d57; CI's `livefeed-fleet-cert` job runs the certification on the push. Not run for this record: those three rigs on this push's tip, `scripts/gate.sh`, the release lib suite, the docker ladder, the mutation leg CI runs for the owner `shard_directory`, and the re-record of TLA-002 and TLA-011, which the edit of `src/shard_directory.rs` stales. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent read-only review of the branch (2026-10-08, finding F5) found "Who is affected" wrong about `bench/fleet/local-fanout.sh`, which the record said waits for no split: the rig exists to drive splits and fails without one. Corrected the same day. The same review (finding F1) found that the gate held only while the ring had more than one active server, so a split at one active server became cross-server once the ring grew. Amended by the commit that gates every published fleet ring (the review's option (B), which the owner approved on 2026-10-08, item T2): the title, the index row, the summary sentence, "Endpoint", "Condition", "Before", "After", "Who is affected", "Given up", "Pinning tests" and "Risk reason" follow it. The same approval (option 1') moved the split of CI's fleet certification, the LiveFeed canary, the docker ladder's D1, D2 and D4 and the fan-out rig outside fleet mode, before their fleets form (the commit before the gate); "Who is affected" and "Risk reason" say so. A review of the landing chain (2026-10-08, finding F2) found "Risk reason" saying the fleet certification, the canary and the fan-out rig ran on this push's tip; they ran on the tier100 branch's tip, and "Risk reason" says so. Numbered #100 on its branch, renumbered #121 when the branch was rebased onto slate a2ae3d57 and #119 in the landing order of 2026-10-08 (rebased onto cb518e57). Amended by the C1 rung's commit (the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, T7): "Who is affected" names the Compute rung.

### #115 326191ca — A write's body is refused 429 project_memory_pressure while it is buffered, once it would take its project past its memory line while the project holds other bytes

- **Program item:** The capacity review of 2026-10-07 (C3); this predates phase A, whose memory story did not cover it. The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4"); the shared-cell profile's 8 MiB body cap (README, "Shared cells Q1" (A)) follows landing.
- **Surface:** product.
- **Endpoint:** Every product `POST` and `PUT` with a body: appends, batches, creates, consumer configuration, pull and settle bodies. `admission::body::buffer_within` (`src/admission/body.rs`, the one body-buffering loop, moved out of `src/http.rs`), charging `quota::BufferedBodyGuard::try_grow`; the product surface buffers through `product::read_memory::buffered_body`. The raw surface's frozen `append_typed` keeps `http::buffer_body_charged` with no line.
- **Condition:** The cell sets `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES` (shared cells), and an arriving chunk would take the project's estimated pressure past the line while the project holds bytes besides this body.
- **Before:** Admission checked the write latch once, and every upload admitted together (all at pressure 0) then buffered up to `MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES` (32 MiB) unchecked: 64 × 32 MiB is 2 GiB on a 1 GiB single-server cell, an OOM for every tenant.
- **After:** The upload is answered `429 project_memory_pressure` (`Retry-After: 1`, project-tagged) and the server stops reading its body. A body alone, with nothing else of its project held, is still buffered whole up to the limit (as a read alone always fits). `413 body_too_large` past the limit is unchanged.
- **Retry semantics:** Nothing was written (the append runs only after the body is buffered), so the retry is safe and an idempotent producer's sequence is untouched.
- **Who is affected:** Shared-cell projects whose concurrent uploads, held pages or write backlog fill their line. Cells without a line are unchanged.
- **Given up:** Near the line, uploads the latch's hysteresis used to admit now see `429`.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_capacity.rs::concurrent_uploads_share_their_projects_memory_line (C3; red: `4 uploads of 6291456 bytes each under a line of 8388608: the project's pressure holds 25165824 body bytes; answers [None, None, None, None]`)
  - src/admission/body/tests.rs (alone past the line, refused beside 6 KiB at an 8 KiB line, exactly to the line, no line, the limit, a failing stream, no project) and src/quota/pressure_tests.rs::a_body_grows_under_its_projects_line_unless_it_is_alone
  - Control: a line of 0 passed into `buffer_within` is the red above.
- **Risk reason:** medium (drafted "low to medium"; recorded under medium as a new quota refusal on writes): writes near the line now see a `429` that the latch admitted before. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 326191ca, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit; amended in 35a1e06d (below).
- **Amendment (35a1e06d, the owner's landing call C1 of 2026-10-08):** the bytes a body is buffered beside never include its own request's reservation: a consumer pull buffers its body before it reserves its coverage (#114's amendment), so "a body alone is buffered whole" holds for a pull too, and a pull's body is refused here only for its project's other bytes. Owner-approved as part of C1.

### #113 3814cac9 — A woken long-poll takes its page's read reservation again before it reads, and one whose page finds no room before its wait ends answers as a timeout holding its cursor

- **Program item:** The isolation review (F1) and the capacity review (C2) of 2026-10-07, fixing #108 for woken long-polls. The `204` rather than a new `429`, which would need a `ReadFailure` variant through `execute_read`, `execute_fork_read` and `ResolvedRead::execute` (three scopes frozen exactly by seven approved rows), was the owner's choice (README, "Shared cells Q4"), with its cost accepted for design partners (README, "Shared cells Q1", (i)). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** product.
- **Endpoint:** `GET /v1/streams/{name}/records:long-poll` (and `records?live=long-poll`). `ReadHold::resume` (`src/admission/read_memory.rs`), reached through `admission::park::resume` from `read_request::wait_tail`'s new `resumed`, still parked.
- **Condition:** A long-poll waited (parked, holding nothing, #108) and was woken by records past its cursor. Before reading them it takes its admission reservation again, `min(maxBytes, 8 MiB)`, under its project's memory line (`PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES`, set on shared cells) and the instance's read memory, waiting for room until its own `waitMs` deadline. The condition is that no room comes by then.
- **Before:** The woken page was read and charged unreserved, past the line and the instance's read memory: one project's fan-out wake could hold its line plus up to 1 MiB per waiter (64 MiB at the inflight ceiling), and every neighbour's read waited 2 s and was refused `503 read_memory_busy`. (Before #108 nothing was reserved at all.)
- **After:** With room, the page is served as before. Without room by the deadline the long-poll answers as its timeout does: `204` with `Prisma-Next-Cursor` equal to the cursor it waited from and, on an unsegmented stream, `Prisma-Up-To-Date: true` (what the frozen timeout path renders); an applied long-poll's `Prisma-Durable-Cursor` stays at the durable frontier it waited with. Nothing is charged. A stream sealed while the long-poll waits brings no records, so the seal is answered at once.
- **Retry semantics:** The client long-polls again from the same cursor, as after any timeout; that request finds the records and is admitted under its own reservation (served, or refused `429 project_memory_pressure` or `503 read_memory_busy` with `Retry-After: 1` after a 2 s wait). No record is skipped: the cursor never moves past unread records.
- **Who is affected:** Projects whose own unread or undrained pages fill their memory line (shared cells); every project when the instance's read memory is full. Compliant projects that drain their pages see only a short wait for room; an SDK fan-out wake at the line is served a few waiters at a time.
- **Given up:** A woken long-poll without room no longer gets its records in that request; it gets them in the next.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/read_memory.rs::a_projects_woken_long_polls_stay_inside_its_line_and_its_neighbour_reads (F1; red: `project 0 holds 11799612 B against its line of 8388608 B ...; its neighbour's read: (503, Some("read_memory_busy"), false)`), ::woken_default_long_polls_stay_inside_their_projects_line_and_its_neighbour_reads (C2; red: `31465632 B against its line of 8388608 B`), ::a_woken_long_poll_without_room_ends_its_wait_as_a_timeout_holding_its_cursor (red: `left: (..., 200, 200, 9, ...) right: (..., 200, 204, 0, ...)`)
  - src/dst/tests/read_memory.rs::a_woken_long_poll_on_applied_records_without_room_keeps_its_durable_cursor_at_the_frontier and ::a_woken_long_poll_sealed_while_it_waits_at_the_line_answers_the_seal_at_once (428aab71; each kills one mutant of `resumed`)
  - src/admission/read_memory/tests.rs::a_woken_wait_takes_its_reservation_again_or_ends_at_its_deadline (paused time: at once, after a 400 ms release, refused at a 1.5 s deadline taking nothing and counting one memory shed)
- **Risk reason:** medium: the timeout claims `Up-To-Date` at the old cursor although records arrived during the wait. That is linearizable (the long-poll's interval includes the moment before the append), but a client that reads `Up-To-Date` as "caught up at response time" can raise a spurious caught-up event before its next poll serves the records. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 3814cac9 and 428aab71, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against both.

### #112 0870263b — cell-admin offboards a project only after the cell refuses its revoked credentials, deletes every stream its catalog lists, and only then omits it, for good

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 9's offboarding (findings M4, R6; blocker 7 of the shared-cells plan). Crypto-erasure now, reclamation with E11 and ids never reused are the owner's decision (README, "Shared cells Q9, offboarding and erasure"; SPEC G8). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** product: all product routes, for the project being offboarded.
- **Endpoint:** `DELETE /v1/streams/{name}` and `GET /v1/streams` (the walk), and every route the project's tokens reach. `deploy/cell-admin/offboard.ts` and `cell-client.ts`.
- **Condition:** The operator runs `cell-admin offboard --project P` and publishes the bundle, runs it again with `--cell-url`, then publishes the final bundle.
- **Before:** No offboarding procedure existed. A project removed from the feed kept its streams, which went on accruing storage billed to `ACCOUNT_ID` as unowned, and its id could be placed again, showing the next owner the old catalog, consumer and usage metadata.
- **After:**
  - **After the first bundle:** every customer token of P gets `403 credential_not_active` (each credential revoked at `grant_version + 1`; the cell refuses a changed credential at an unchanged version). An operator credential (`catalog.read`, `lifecycle.manage`, `metadata.read`) joins the same snapshot; P stays active so the operator can walk it.
  - **During the second run:** the cell must serve that snapshot (the operator credential reads the catalog, every revoked credential answers exactly `403 credential_not_active`), else exit 3 and nothing changes. Then every stream of P is deleted (`DELETE /v1/streams/{name}`, billing closed per stream; 204, 404 and 410 accepted, 429 and 503 retried after `Retry-After`), walk after walk, until one full walk lists none and started at least `--settle-secs` (default 60) after the proof. A catalog still listing streams after `--max-walks` (10) exits 3 with P still placed.
  - **After the final bundle:** P's tokens get `421 wrong_cell` (the SDK raises `WrongCellError`). P's id stays on the denylist and in the state as a tombstone: `cell-admin` never places it again on this cell, nor on any cell sharing the denylist file. A returning customer gets a new project id.
- **Retry semantics:** None for the client: `403 credential_not_active` and `421` are not retryable.
- **Who is affected:** Offboarded design partners only. Neighbours see no change: the end-to-end leg checks that a neighbour's stream of the same name and its record survive.
- **Given up:** The offboarded project's data, irreversibly; the bytes stay in the bucket, unreadable without keys the server never stored, until E11 reclamation, and their names, routing keys and sizes stay readable to the operator (SPEC G8).
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes. Each of the two publications restarts the shared cell for every tenant until the wrapper polls the feeds (the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, README "Shared cells Q6" (a), which follows landing).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - deploy/cell-admin/offboard.test.ts (six, against `fake-cell.ts`, which answers in `verify_customer`'s order; red on the previous CLI: `0 pass, 6 fail`)
  - deploy/cell-admin/e2e.test.ts, "a shared cell admits, serves and offboards projects on the real binary" (opt-in; run by CI against the release binaries since this push)
  - Controls: no revocation proof, the project omitted after the first walk, no settle time, plain http allowed to any host, revocation without a `grant_version` bump: each fails its named test (the last one also times out the e2e).
- **Risk reason:** medium: data deletion is irreversible, so the procedure waits for proof before deleting. A stream still being created during the final walk is invisible to the catalog; the 60 s settle time covers requests authorized before the revocation. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 0870263b, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit.

### #110 91c5b2af — A project on a single-server shared cell runs under the shared-cell posture and the ceilings cell-admin publishes for it

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 9, for the launch shape the owner chose on 2026-10-07 (README, "Launch shape and routing"): one 1 GiB server, one shard, no routers, the fleet off, design partners admitted by hand (findings H1, H5, M3, M4, L2 on the tool side). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4"). The memory values are being changed by the owner's decision of the same day (README, "Shared cells Q1" (A): a 32 MiB line and an 8 MiB request body on this profile), which amends this record when it lands.
- **Surface:** product: every product route (`/v1/streams`, `/v1/streams/{*name}`, `/v1/projects/{project}/usage`) of a cell deployed with `compute-1g.env` then `shared-cell.env`, for projects `cell-admin admit` placed. The raw surface and the operator and debug routes stay deployment-bearer only.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. The profile `deploy/profiles/shared-cell.env` and the operator tool `deploy/cell-admin` (`profile.ts` derives each ceiling as max(1, ⌊bound ÷ k⌋), `commands.ts` `admit`). Every behaviour below already exists in the binary; this record says when a design partner meets it.
- **Condition:** The cell is a single-server shared cell (k = 8): one shard, the fleet off, `STREAMS_AUTH_MODE=enforce`, `BILLING_MODE=required`, `ROLLUP=1`, and each project's feed quotas written by `cell-admin`.
- **Before:** No shared cell existed. A dedicated cell ran `compute-1g.env` with whatever quotas its feed carried (a 0, or no quota, meant no project limit), `SSE_FEED_PROJECT_BYTES` at 32 MiB and no project memory line.
- **After:**
  - **Quotas.** Every bounded axis is published explicitly at most bound ÷ 8, and exactly that unless the operator set a lower value: 176 requests/s, 257,500 appended bytes/s, 3,750,000 read bytes/s, 64 requests in flight, 150 live subscriptions, 8,192 streams. A project over one gets the existing typed refusals: `429 project_rate_limit` (+`Retry-After`), `429 project_concurrency_limit` (requests in flight and live subscriptions alike), `429 stream_limit` (not retryable). Append records/s and queued append bytes are published as 0 (no project limit), as the server's own ceiling leaves them (#100). `cell-admin` refuses a 0 quota and a quota above its ceiling.
  - **LiveFeed.** A project's retained feed bytes are capped at 8 MiB (was 32 MiB): a project holding many slow SSE feeds sees lag disconnects and resumes on its own feeds sooner.
  - **Memory line.** `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES=16384000` (the instance read memory ÷ k): a project's page reads wait, then get `429 project_memory_pressure` (`Retry-After: 1`) once its held pages would pass the line, and its writes latch with the same code when its modelled pressure passes it (#108, #109).
  - **Sleep.** `KEEP_AWAKE` is not set: a request to a sleeping cell waits for its wake.
  - The binary does not read `PROJECT_SHARE_K` or `CELL_ENVELOPE_*` until the owner-approved `bootstrap::run` wiring (#100); until then `cell-admin` is their only reader, and every quota it publishes is explicit and at most bound ÷ k, so its feed is the same under either binary.
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged per code: rate and memory refusals are retryable after `Retry-After`; `stream_limit` is not.
- **Who is affected:** Design partners admitted to a shared cell. Nobody on a dedicated cell: `compute-1g.env` is unchanged.
- **Given up:** A design partner's ceilings are fixed shares of one server, whatever its neighbours use.
- **Deployment comparison:** A new profile layered on `compute-1g.env`; its header names what the deploy step sets per cell and what must stay absent.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - deploy/cell-admin/profile.test.ts (the profile's values, ceilings and posture checks; it pins the tool's copies of server facts against the tree)
  - deploy/cell-admin/cell-admin.test.ts, "admit > publishes the project at its ceilings…" and the refusal table
  - the server-side refusals: the `shared_cell_hostile` and `read_memory` DSTs (#100, #108)
  - Controls: no ceiling check, a missing quota left at 0, a 0 quota accepted, no feed-share check, no memory-line check: each fails its named test (red without the change: `cannot read profile .../deploy/profiles/shared-cell.env`).
- **Risk reason:** medium: the values are not certified. The envelope (1,411 req/s, 2.06 MB/s, 30 MB/s) was measured on another shape, and the memory line can latch a compliant project's own writes near its subscription ceiling (about 15 MiB modelled against a 15.6 MiB line); the owner's certification (README, "Shared cells Q5") and the Q1(A) values address both before the first cell. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 91c5b2af, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit; amended in f0e9f2de, 75d28313 and be15f313 (below).
- **Amendment (f0e9f2de, the owner's Q1 (A) values of 2026-10-08, README "Shared cells Q1"):** the memory line is `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES=33554432` (four default 8 MiB page reads of one project at once; a fifth waits 2 s, then `429 project_memory_pressure`), and `MAX_REQUEST_BODY_BYTES=8388608`: a body above 8 MiB gets `413 body_too_large` on a shared cell (the ceiling is stored with the namespace at creation). The owner accepted that four projects' unread pages can fill the 125 MiB read memory (neighbours wait 2 s, then `503 read_memory_busy`) and that the instance budgets pass the shed line only when all are full at once (603,499,008 bytes, pinned on the profile as a deploy layers it, so raising any of them in either profile file fails the pin). `cell-admin` checks that at least four projects at their lines fill the read memory. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Amendment (75d28313, #130):** the binary reads `PROJECT_SHARE_K` and `CELL_ENVELOPE_*` and holds each project to the same ceilings itself; queued append bytes join the bounded axes (the profile states `MAX_UNABSORBED_BYTES_PER_INSTANCE=536870912`, and `cell-admin` publishes 67,108,864 or less, where it published 0). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Amendment (be15f313, #132):** the profile sets `STREAMS_RELEASE_POSTURE=1`, and `cell-admin` requires it. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.

### #108 385bd8e7 — A page read reserves its page's memory before it runs and its body holds the page's bytes until it ends, under the instance's read memory and its project's memory line, waiting 2 s for room before 429 or 503

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 6, finding H3. Reads reserve memory instead of pre-debiting the read quota, and the instance read memory is on by default on every cell, dedicated ones included, as the owner chose (README, "Shared cells Q4"; its sizing for a 1 GiB cell is "Shared cells Q1"). `/operator/data.json`'s two members fold into this record. The owner ratified it as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** product; operator-debug for `GET /operator/data.json`.
- **Endpoint:** `GET /v1/streams/{name}/records`, `GET /v1/streams/{name}/records:long-poll`, `GET /v1/streams/{name}:scan`. Not affected: SSE (`records:sse`), the raw surface (deployment bearer only) and the fleet-internal routes. Owners: the instance read memory (`src/admission/read_memory.rs`, `ReadHold`), the project's read bytes (`src/quota/read_reservation.rs`), the product backstops (`src/product/read_memory.rs`, called from `product_entry_axum_inner` in place of the POST memory gate, line-neutral).
- **Condition:** The read reserves `min(maxBytes, 8 MiB)` after project admission: 8 MiB when a records read gives no `maxBytes` (every SDK read), 4 MiB for a scan; an unparseable `maxBytes` also reserves 8 MiB and the route answers it 400 as before. It waits when (1) the instance's read memory has no room: a quarter of `ADMIT_RSS_SHED_MB`, 125 MiB at the default 500 MB and none when the shed line is 0, holding the reservations of reads in flight and the exact rendered pages of bodies not yet ended; or (2) where `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES` > 0, the project already holds read bytes and the reservation would take it over its line.
