- **Before:** Every page read was admitted at once unless the project's read-byte bucket was in debt, and the page was debited after it was served. 64 concurrent 8 MiB reads materialized 512 MiB at once on a 1 GiB server, and a body the client never drained pinned its page with nothing counted.
- **After:**
  - The read waits up to 2 s for room, then is refused: at the project's line `429 project_memory_pressure` (`Retry-After: 1`, the project's audit tag, one memory shed counted on the project); in the instance's read memory `503 read_memory_busy` (`Retry-After: 1`). A read alone always fits, so a page larger than a budget is served alone.
  - While a long-poll waits for data it holds nothing (the reservation is released while parked; #113 takes it again on waking). When the page renders the reservation becomes the page's exact size, released when the body ends or is dropped; the body is framed in at most 64 KiB frames, `Content-Length` and the bytes on the wire unchanged. A consumer pull's batch is held the same way from render.
  - Operator-debug: `GET /operator/data.json` adds `local.read_memory_bytes` and `local.read_memory_capacity_bytes`.
- **Retry semantics:** Both refusals are retryable after 1 s; the SDK retries a retryable 503 after `Retry-After`. The cursor is unchanged, so the retried read returns the same page.
- **Who is affected:** The instance budget applies on every cell (`ADMIT_RSS_SHED_MB` defaults to 500): a client sees a wait only when more than about 15 default-size reads are in flight on one instance at once or unread bodies hold the budget, and a 503 only when that lasts 2 s. The project line applies only where `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES` is set: the shared-cell profile sets it (#110).
- **Given up:** Concurrency of large default reads: the reservation is pessimistic (8 MiB for every SDK read until its page renders), so about 15 execute at once per instance and the rest queue for milliseconds. Reserving only the bytes available to the read is the owner's decision for before self-serve (README, "Shared cells Q1" (D)).
- **Deployment comparison:** No new setting; `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES` gains a read meaning (#110 sets it on the shared-cell profile).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/read_memory.rs::a_page_is_held_until_its_client_reads_it_or_leaves, ::a_project_at_its_memory_line_waits_and_is_refused_and_its_neighbour_is_not, ::a_read_waits_for_the_instance_read_memory_and_is_refused_after_the_wait, ::waiting_long_polls_hold_no_read_memory (new; red with no reservation and no body hold: `left: (0, 0) right: (3146583, 3146583)`; on the wire with the body hold but no reservation: `the fourth read left: (200, None, None) right: (429, Some("project_memory_pressure"), Some("1"))` and `left: (200, None, None) right: (503, Some("read_memory_busy"), Some("1"))`)
  - src/admission/read_memory/tests.rs (6) and src/quota/read_reservation/tests.rs (3)
  - Controls: no unreserve while waiting fails `waiting_long_polls_hold_no_read_memory` (parked 0 of 4); one-frame bodies fail `a_page_is_held...` and the 64 KiB-frame unit test; no body hold fails the three holding legs.
- **Risk reason:** medium: new retryable refusals on reads, on every cell. A project line below 8 MiB plus the project's held pages refuses a burst of its own reads after 2 s; unread bodies hold real memory, so a client that reads slowly but above the drain floor (#107) can make other reads wait. With the line at most the budget ÷ k, k − 1 projects at their lines leave the k-th its room. Residuals accepted with the record: the render's 2× transient, kernel buffers and the last 64 KiB frame are not counted; waiting is not FIFO; raw and internal routes reserve nothing. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 385bd8e7, from the draft the owner ratified with `/operator/data.json`'s members folded in, and checked against that commit.

### #107 40603091 — A client that accepts less than 16 KiB of a pending response in 10 s is disconnected, so a reader that never drains no longer pins the response and the memory behind it

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 6, finding H3 ("a minimum drain rate"). The floor on every connection, SSE included, with code-constant values, was the owner's choice (README, "Shared cells Q4"); the effective-configuration summary's two members fold into this record. The owner ratified it as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** both: every HTTP/1.1 connection the server accepts (product, raw, fleet-internal and operator routes, SSE included); process for the effective-configuration summary.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `serve_h1` serves every accepted socket through a `DrainListener` (`src/http/serve/drain.rs`, under hyper, on the socket); `HttpConfig` gains `drain_window` (10 s) and `drain_min_bytes` (16 KiB), with no environment name (rigs shorten them), and the effective-configuration summary (`src/config/summary.rs`) reports `http.drain_window_ms` (10,000) and `http.drain_min_bytes` (16,384).
- **Condition:** The server has response bytes the socket cannot take, and in a 10 s window the client accepts fewer than 16 KiB of them. A window opens when a write finds the socket full and closes when a write is taken whole.
- **Before:** No write deadline existed: hyper's only deadline never runs while a response is written, and once its write buffer is full it stops polling the body. A client could pin a response, and the page behind it, for as long as it held the socket, with no admission slot held (Layer B's H9b leg).
- **After:** The write fails `TimedOut` and hyper closes the connection, dropping the response body and its read hold (#108). The client sees the connection end before the response does: a reset, or a body shorter than its `Content-Length`. Idle keep-alive connections, parked long-polls and idle SSE subscriptions never open a window, since no write is pending. Reads, flush, shutdown, vectored writes and `set_nodelay` pass through.
- **Retry semantics:** The client reconnects. A page read retried from the same cursor returns the same page; an SSE subscriber resumes from its last event id, as after any disconnect.
- **Who is affected:** Clients that read a pending response at under about 1.6 KB/s (16 KiB per 10 s), and clients that stop reading altogether. An SSE subscriber that stops reading already met its session's 10 s send timeout (`sse_send_billed`). On Compute the server's client is usually the platform edge, which reads promptly.
- **Given up:** A legitimately very slow reader's connection; the plan asked for the floor on non-SSE bodies only, but the socket cannot tell the bodies apart.
- **Deployment comparison:** No environment name; the effective-configuration summary gains two members with the code constants.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/http/serve/drain/tests.rs::a_response_the_client_never_reads_fails_when_its_window_ends, ::a_client_draining_below_the_floor_is_cut_at_the_end_of_a_window, ::the_serve_loop_drops_a_body_its_client_never_reads (red with the floor removed: the write never failed; `the body a silent client holds is dropped`), ::a_client_draining_above_the_floor_receives_the_whole_response, ::a_client_that_catches_up_closes_its_window, ::the_floor_is_16_kib_per_10_s
  - Control: a window a caught-up client closed never closing fails `a_client_that_catches_up_closes_its_window` (the old window cuts the next response).
- **Risk reason:** medium (drafted "low to medium"; recorded under medium because it ends connections that used to live): no server-side bound on unread responses existed, and this is the H9b defence; a legitimate client this slow is rare. The values are code constants. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 40603091, from the draft the owner ratified with the summary members folded in, and checked against that commit.

### #105 950b84ae — A waiting consumer pull walks its lineage again only when a record became durable, the consumer's queue state moved or one of its leases expires, never every 50 ms

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 5 (cost review 2.4). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4").
- **Surface:** product.
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:pull` with `waitMs` > 0. The 50 ms sleep in `pull` is one call, `doc.park.wait(..)`, on a per-request `PullPark` (`src/application/consumer/pull_wait.rs`).
- **Condition:** The lineage walk leased nothing and the pull's deadline has not passed.
- **Before:** The pull slept 50 ms and walked again until its deadline (capped at 25 s): about 20 walks per second, each a SlateDB get, a merged read and a `Receive` through the shard's committer. A new record, an ack that unblocked a key or an expired lease reached the waiting pull within 50 ms. One tenant at 64 parked pulls pushed about 1,300 `Receive`s a second into a quiet shard's committer.
- **After:** The pull parks until something can have made a message deliverable: a record became durable in a lineage segment, the consumer's queue state moved (a settle, a nack, a lease another pull took), or the earliest of the consumer's leases expires; then it walks again. Each wait also walks once more right after the first walk, and once at the deadline. An idle pull walks 3 times per wait instead of about 20 a second, and a walk that leases nothing wakes no other pull. A record, an unblocking ack or an expiry reaches the waiting pull at once instead of within 50 ms. A pull whose lineage holds a segment another instance serves, or whose engine closed, still walks every 50 ms.
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged: the same answers, messages or an empty batch at the deadline.
- **Who is affected:** Consumers that pull with `waitMs`, the SDK's `consumer.pull({ waitMs: 20000 })` included: delivery is earlier, nothing is answered differently.
- **Given up:** Nothing a client sees; a missed wake source would now cost up to the pull's whole wait instead of 50 ms, which the four controls below guard.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/parked_waits.rs::an_idle_pull_walks_twice_more_while_it_waits_and_wakes_no_other_pull (red on the unfixed pull: `walks while waiting a second (2 at once) left: 20 right: 2`), ::an_append_reaches_a_waiting_pull_at_once, ::an_expired_lease_reaches_a_waiting_pull_at_its_deadline, ::an_ack_that_unblocks_a_key_wakes_a_waiting_pull, ::a_record_committed_during_the_first_walk_reaches_the_pull (all new)
  - Controls: walking every 50 ms again (19 walks, not 2); no lease timer (redelivered after 10.0 s); no consumer-state digest (an unblocked key delivered after 10.0 s); no re-walk after the first walk (a record committed during the first walk delivered 9.98 s after the walk resumed).
- **Risk reason:** medium: delivery timing on a successful path changes, and a missed wake would now cost up to the pull's whole wait (25 s), not 50 ms; four controls guard the four wake sources. A lease's expiry is read from the handle's published queue state and the wall clock, as the committer reads it. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 950b84ae, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit.

### #103 640646f8 — A token verifies only under a signing key the keys feed pins to its audience, so the customer issuer's key no longer mints workload tokens for the internal surface

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 8, finding H6, and the keys contract revision r7 (`contracts/streams-platform/v1/keys.schema.json` with `aud` required and three golden vectors, MULTITENANCY §14.1 r7, CONTROL-PLANE-INTEGRATION §7.5 rule 7), which the owner accepted on 2026-10-08 with the cryptography of phase A (README, "Shared cells Q8"). "`aud` required, fail closed" was the owner's choice over "absent `aud` means customer" (README, "Shared cells Q4"). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** both: the product surface verifies customer tokens; the fleet-internal surface (`/v1/internal/*`, `/v1/segments/*`) and the raw surface under enforce verify workload tokens. The platform contract (`STREAMS_AUTH_KEYS_FILE` and every producer of it) changes with it.
- **Endpoint:** Every route that verifies a token. `JwksKey::pinned` (`src/auth/signing_key.rs`) checks the header alg and the verifier's audience before the signature; `verify_customer` passes `Customer`, `verify_internal` `Internal`. `KeyDoc.aud` is required in the feed (`src/auth_feed.rs`), and `key_fp` fingerprints audience plus material, so `publish_jwks`'s "a kid never rebinds" rule also refuses re-pinning a kid.
- **Condition and before/after:**
  - **A key without `aud`, or with any other value** (empty, a typo, a third audience). Before: `aud` did not exist, and a feed carrying it was refused as an unknown field. After: the whole snapshot is refused (`auth snapshot refused; previous snapshot retained`); the previous key set keeps serving and ages toward `keys_stale` (21,600 s); with no previous set (a boot) every token is `401 kid_unknown`.
  - **A kid published under one audience reappears under the other.** After: the snapshot is refused (`kid rebound to different key material or audience`) and the previous set stays.
  - **A workload token (`aud: prisma-streams-internal`) signed under a kid pinned to `prisma-streams-data`**, the customer issuer's key, on the fleet-internal routes or the raw surface under enforce. Before: accepted when its operations claim named the route (`GET /v1/internal/segment-read/{name}` with the victim's target headers and stream key returned `200` and the victim's records). After: `401 unauthorized` "fleet-internal credential required" (`internal_unauthorized`).
  - **A customer token signed under a kid pinned to `prisma-streams-internal`** on the product surface. Before: accepted when its claims were valid. After: `401 wrong_audience` "the bearer token failed verification", not retryable, journaled as a denial.
  - Unchanged: a customer token under a customer-pinned kid and a workload token under an internal-pinned kid; alg pinning, kid retirement, generation digests, staleness windows and the unknown-kid nudge; off mode, where workload JWTs are never consulted.
- **Retry semantics:** None by the client. A refused feed is the publisher's to fix (republish with `aud`); a token signed by the wrong key never becomes valid.
- **Who is affected:**
  - Every keys-feed producer must emit `aud`. In the repository: the reference emulator (now one customer key plus its own workload key; `/admin/mint-workload` can still sign with the customer key, for the e2e's negative check), the reference gateway (verifies only customer-pinned kids), the golden vectors and three bench tools (`bench/k2cost/field/feeds.mjs`, `bench/soak/mtgen.mjs`, `scripts/mt-noisy-campaign.mjs`, customer keys only), all updated in the same commit. The Control Plane's Stage 1 producer must emit it when it lands.
  - Any fleet whose workload tokens are signed by the customer issuer's key, as the emulator's were: its relays get `401` until its workload issuer publishes its own internal-pinned kid. Platform P7 (workload identity) must use a separate key.
  - Customers with correctly issued tokens: none.
  - Format cutover: old binaries refuse a feed with `aud` (`KeyDoc` denies unknown fields) and new ones a feed without it, so a binary that boots against the other format answers every token `401 kid_unknown`. With no users, the owner's rule is that the feed switches to the `aud` format with its binary (README, "Shared cells Q6" (d)); a field campaign and its binary come from one tree.
- **Given up:** One key can no longer sign both audiences; a fleet without its own workload key cannot relay.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes; the keys feed's format does.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::an_internal_token_signed_by_a_customer_key_is_refused (A10 H6, un-ignored: the customer kid's workload token is `401`, and on the same cell the same request under a fleet-pinned kid reads exactly `[{"secret":1}]`. Red on the unfixed tree: `customer-key internal token read proj-sc0001's segment: [{"secret":1}] left: 200 right: 401`)
  - src/auth.rs::tests::internal_audience_is_a_separate_boundary (red: `called Result::unwrap_err() on an Ok value: InternalPrincipal { subject: "slot-3", ... }`)
  - src/auth_feed.rs::tests::keys_pin_each_kid_to_one_audience (red: `a key without aud parsed as Ok(1)`) and ::golden_key_vectors_parse_as_the_schema_judges_them
  - src/auth/signing_key.rs::tests::a_feed_names_exactly_the_two_audiences, ::a_fleet_key_cannot_sign_a_customer_token, ::a_kid_is_never_repinned_to_another_audience
  - scripts/platform-e2e.mjs, two new checks: the schema catches the missing-`aud` hostile vector, and a workload token signed by the customer key is refused (release build; run by CI's `platform-e2e` job)
  - Controls: `JwksKey::pinned` without the audience comparison fails the A10 leg (200 against 401), the boundary test and the fleet-key test; `key_fp` without the audience fails the re-pin test.
- **Risk reason:** medium: the security change is one comparison before signature verification, on paths whose refusals already exist; the risk is the feed-format cutover, which fails closed (every token `401 kid_unknown` on a cell booted against the other format). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 640646f8, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit (the pin, the fingerprint, the required `aud`, the producers, the contract files); amended with F2 in 78f8c66e (below).
- **Amendment (78f8c66e, one key material under one audience; isolation review F2):** a kid's pin did not bind the key it names, so a key set publishing ONE public key under a customer kid and again under an internal kid made the customer signing key a fleet key. Since 78f8c66e such a set is refused whole: the keys feed by `parse_keys` ("kids .. and .. name one key material under two audiences"), with the previous set retained as for a key without `aud`, and a direct publication by `publish_jwks` ("one key material published under two audiences"). The material is the key bytes jsonwebtoken verifies with (an SPKI's subjectPublicKey, or a PKCS#1 key's DER), so re-armouring or rewrapping a PEM does not make it another key. Who is affected: a producer that publishes one key under both audiences; no in-tree producer does (the emulator's workload key is its own), and the DST rigs, which did, now give the fleet kid its own test key. Pinning tests: src/auth_feed.rs::tests::a_key_signs_for_one_audience_whatever_kid_names_it (red: `one PEM under both audiences parsed as Ok(2)`), src/auth/signing_key.rs::tests::one_key_material_signs_for_one_audience_whatever_its_kid (red: `left: (false, true) right: (true, false)`), ::a_key_material_is_the_key_whatever_its_armour and ::a_rearmoured_customer_key_cannot_be_published_for_the_fleet. Ratified with the record (README, "Shared cells Q4" and "Shared cells Q8"); written in the records commit of the push that lands 78f8c66e and checked against it.

### #100 30d369cf — On a cell shared k ways a project's quota on each shared bound is at most the bound ÷ k, and a 0 or missing quota is exactly that share, so a crowd at its ceilings leaves a neighbour room

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 2, finding H1; k = 8 and "a 0 quota takes the ceiling" are the owner's (README, "Shared cells Q9, k = 8"). b3179a34 adds the subscription-axis leg. The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing, choosing ÷ k over ÷ (k + 1) (README, "Shared cells Q4").
- **Surface:** product (the per-project quota refusals).
- **Endpoint:** Every authenticated product request: inflight and request rate; `PUT /v1/streams/{name}` (streams); appends (append bytes); reads, `:scan` and `:pull` (read bytes); SSE and LiveFeed subscriptions; watch capabilities, through `status_and_quotas`. The ceiling is applied where `AuthService::publish_policies` publishes a policy snapshot (`CellCeiling`, `src/auth/ceiling.rs`; `src/auth/publication.rs`), so every reader of a published policy (token verification, capability status, the lease) holds the project to the same effective quotas.
- **Condition:** The cell installed a shared ceiling (k > 1), and the project's feed value on a bounded axis is 0, absent, or above ⌊bound ÷ k⌋.
  - **Bounded axes:** requests/s, append bytes/s, read bytes/s, inflight, live subscriptions, streams (a bound of 0 is no bound).
  - **Not bounded:** append records/s and queued append bytes keep their feed value (queued bytes ÷ k follows with the wiring, README "Shared cells Q10" (b)).
- **Before:** Each quota was the feed value, and a 0 meant no project limit, so only the instance bounds applied. In A2b (inflight bound 64) 7 tenants parking long-polls took all 64 slots, and the eighth tenant's append got `429 rate_limited`.
- **After:** Each effective quota is min(feed, ⌊bound ÷ k⌋), never below 1, and a 0 or missing quota takes ⌊bound ÷ k⌋. Beyond it the project gets the existing typed refusal of that field: `429 project_concurrency_limit` (inflight and subscriptions), `429 stream_limit`, `429 project_rate_limit` (requests, append bytes, read bytes).
  - At k = 8 over the shared-cell profile's bounds (#110): 176 requests/s, 257,500 append B/s, 3,750,000 read B/s, 64 inflight, 150 subscriptions, 8,192 streams. The rate bounds are the measured envelope, pending re-measurement on the one-shard shape (README "Shared cells Q5").
  - k = 1 (dedicated) keeps every quota exactly as fed, a 0 included.
  - **Live in production: nothing yet.** A production binary publishes through the dedicated ceiling (k = 1, no bounds), the identity: `AuthService` is built inside `bootstrap::run`, a frozen scope, and the owner-approved wiring that installs `CellCeiling` from `PROJECT_SHARE_K` and the bounds lands right after phase A (README "Shared cells Q2" (a)), with an amendment of this record. Until then only the test rigs install a shared ceiling.
- **Retry semantics:** The existing 429s with their existing `Retry-After` behaviour; concurrency and stream refusals clear when the project releases a slot or deletes a stream.
- **Who is affected:** Projects on cells installed with k > 1 whose feed sets 0, nothing, or a value above the share on a bounded axis. Dedicated cells (k = 1) are unaffected by construction. `cell-admin` (#110) publishes every bounded quota explicitly at or below its ceiling, so its feeds are the same under either binary.
- **Given up:** A project on a shared cell cannot use more than its share of a bound even when its neighbours are idle; wrong envelope bounds would cap honest tenants, which is why the rate bounds stay 0 (no ceiling) until a measured value is configured.
- **Deployment comparison:** No setting changes in this commit; the wiring adds four (`PROJECT_SHARE_K`, `CELL_ENVELOPE_{REQUESTS,APPEND_BYTES,READ_BYTES}_PER_SEC`), recorded with it.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::a_compliant_crowd_at_its_ceilings_leaves_the_victim_admitted (A2b, inflight; un-ignored. Red on the unfixed tree: `the victim among a parked crowd left: ((429, Some("rate_limited")), 200) right: ((200, None), 200)`)
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::a_crowd_without_stream_quotas_holds_exactly_its_share_of_the_stream_maps (new: each tenant creates exactly 8 of 16, the rest `429 stream_limit`, the victim creates)
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::a_crowd_without_subscription_quotas_holds_exactly_its_share_of_live_connections (b3179a34: each holds exactly 2 of 4, the victim subscribes)
  - src/auth/ceiling.rs::tests (every axis at k = 8, a share never rounds to 0, k = 1 and the dedicated default are the identity) and src/auth/publication/tests.rs::policies_publish_through_the_ceiling_fixed_at_the_first_publication
  - Controls: publishing without the ceiling fails A2b exactly as its red, and the stream leg with `proj-sc0000 created left: 16 right: 8`.
- **Risk reason:** medium, once wired: it adds client-visible 429s, deliberately, at a value the operator sets through k and the bounds. With the ceiling at ⌊bound ÷ k⌋, k tenants at their ceilings fill a bound exactly, so a cell keeps room for a victim while at most k − 1 sit there (the owner's choice, Q4). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 30d369cf and b3179a34, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against both (the ceiling, its first-publication `OnceLock`, the `#[cfg(test)]` install hook, the three legs); amended in 75d28313 (below).
- **Amendment (75d28313, the `bootstrap::run` wiring, #130; the owner's decisions of 2026-10-08, README "Shared cells Q2" (a) and Q10 (b)):** live in production. Boot installs `CellCeiling::of_config` from `PROJECT_SHARE_K` and the `CELL_ENVELOPE_*` settings before the first policy snapshot, and queued append bytes are a seventh bounded axis, ⌊`MAX_UNABSORBED_BYTES_PER_INSTANCE` ÷ k⌋ (67,108,864 bytes at k = 8, `429 queued_bytes` past it); only append records/s keep the feed value. "Live in production: nothing yet" no longer holds. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.

### #97 this record's commit — A server that does not set WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS starts each shard's WAL flushes at least 100 ms apart, the one write tier, so an append on a busy shard, or from a producer with one append in flight, waits up to about 100 ms longer for its acknowledgement

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-07 (README, "Owner decisions", row "Write tier": one 100 ms tier for everyone, no second tier; NEXT-WORK §14.2, item 2). The WAL failsafe at 60 s and the usage drain at 8 s, approved in the same row, are not part of this record; each has its own. Performance acceptance stays with the owner (AGENTS.md §1).
- **Surface:** process (operator-debug for the `pump` block of `GET /v1/debug/timings`, which counts fewer flushes on a busy shard; the object store's request count is a cost the operator sees). What a client can observe is time, and, near an admission cap, a 429 (see "After").
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. The startup setting `WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS` (`--wal-flush-gap-ms`), which `bootstrap::shard_config` gives every shard engine as `ShardConfig::wal_flush_gap` and the group-commit pump of `ShardEngine::start` (`src/shard.rs`) reads. It times the acknowledgement of every append (raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}`, product `POST /v1/streams/{name}/records` and `records:batch`, a seal with a final record, the server's own appends to its system streams), and with it the publication of the appended records to live tails (long-poll and SSE wakes), which happens at the acknowledgement. The history, telemetry, rollup and read-spool databases do not run the pump and are not affected.
  - It times every other operation the shard's committer answers at durability as well. A plain seal and a raw close-only (`POST /v1/streams/{name}:seal` without `final`, `POST /v1/stream/{name}` with `Stream-Closed: true` and no body) are `CommitOp::Close`, and the fleet-internal `POST /v1/internal/seal-fence` (#88) is `CommitOp::SealFence` (`src/shard.rs`). The consumer-group operations are `CommitOp::Queue`, answered through `DurableEffects::queue_acks` (`src/shard/commit_plan.rs`): `:pull` (`QueueOp::Receive`), `:settle` (`Settle`, also the dead-letter handoff), consumer create and configure (`PUT .../consumers/{consumer}`, `ConfigPut`), consumer delete (`DELETE .../consumers/{consumer}`, `ConfigLifecycle` and `ConfigDeleteStep`) and the consumer read (`GET .../consumers/{consumer}`, `ConfigGet`).
  - A pull and a settle each first load the consumer record with `QueueOp::ConfigGet` through the same committer (`ConsumerService::active`, `src/application/consumer.rs`). That load writes nothing, but a group with no writes joins the barrier of the group still in flight before it (`CommitTransaction::join_prior_barrier`, `src/shard/transaction/finalize.rs`, which attaches its reply to the last pending group in `CommitHandoff::attach`), so on a shard with a commit waiting for the gap it is answered only when that commit's flush is durable.
- **Condition:** The process runs the pump (`WAL_GROUP_COMMIT=1`, the default since #77) and sets neither the variable nor the argument. `WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS=0` still selects the gap of `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS`.
- **Before:** Default 10. The pump started a shard's WAL flush when a commit waited and the previous flush had completed, no sooner than 10 ms after the previous flush started, and 1 ms later for the herd-settle, so two flushes started at least 11 ms apart. Against a WAL write of about 40 ms (Tigris) the gap never bound: the pump flushed again as soon as the previous write completed.
- **After:** Default 100: two flushes of one shard start at least 101 ms apart. An append waits for the rest of the gap since its shard's last flush started, `max(0, 100 ms - elapsed)`, then the 1 ms herd-settle, then one WAL write.
  - On a shard whose last flush started at least 100 ms ago (a quiet shard): unchanged, the settle and one write.
  - A producer with one append in flight on an otherwise quiet shard is acknowledged once per about 101 ms, about 9.9 times a second: its write overlaps the gap while the write is shorter than the gap. At 10 ms it was acknowledged once per write and settle (about 18-20 times a second measured on Tigris).
  - A busy shard flushes at most about 9.9 times a second, and an append waits on average about half a gap longer than at 10 ms. Interpolated for the fra field frame in the owner's tier analysis (outside the repository), not measured: append p50 about 0.09-0.14 s and p99 about 0.17-0.29 s, against 70 ms and 252 ms measured at 10 ms with 16 producers.
  - Admission, derived and not measured: the instance cap (`ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT`, 512) counts every request from arrival to response, and the per-stream cap (`ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT_PER_STREAM`, 256) every append until it is answered (#79). At a given append rate the count in flight grows with the acknowledgement time (Little's law), so a load that stayed below the instance cap with acknowledgements of about 70 ms can cross it with about 100-140 ms and have its excess answered 429 `overloaded` (`retry-after: 1`, the 25 ms tarpit). With only appends in flight the instance cap binds near 512 / 0.10-0.14 s, about 3,700-5,100 appends a second per process, where it bound near 7,300 at 70 ms. The per-stream cap would need about 1,800-2,600 appends a second to one stream segment, above the default request limit of 1,000 a second (`LIMIT_REQS_PER_SEC`), which refuses first.
  - Consumer groups, derived from the code and not measured: on a quiet shard a pull and a settle are unchanged (the record load finds no pending group and answers at once, then one settle and one write). On a busy shard each waits up to about two pump cycles, about 2 x 101 ms plus two WAL writes: its `ConfigGet` waits for the pending flush, then its `Receive` or `Settle` for the next one. A consumer that settles each pull before its next pull can so wait up to about four cycles per batch, about 0.4 s plus four writes, where at 10 ms each of those waits was about one WAL write (the gap never bound). A pull leases at most `max` records per cycle, so a consumer pulling one record at a time pays the cycles per record. A seal or close-only on a busy shard waits like an append.
  - No status, header, body or error code changes otherwise. The former behaviour is one setting: `WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS=10`.
- **Measured:** locally only, by `bootstrap::tests::write_tier::an_isolated_append_waits_for_one_wal_write_and_the_rest_of_the_gap`: one shard engine built by `shard_config` and `shard_settings` from the default command line, an in-memory store whose every WAL write takes exactly 50 ms, the dev profile, one append in flight. SlateDB's own flush timer, the pump's failsafe, is moved to 60 s in that test because at its 1 s default it would fire inside the one-second run. Over ten runs: an append on a shard that had rested a gap waited 54.9-58.8 ms (the settle, the write and 4-8 ms of rig overhead); each of five appends sent back to back waited 102.9-109.0 ms; from the first send to the last acknowledgement the six flushes took 579-586 ms, against at least 556 ms by the rule and 806 ms if the gap ran from the end of each write. At the old default (one run) the same back-to-back appends waited 54.0-56.9 ms and the six flushes took 337 ms. Not measured: anything on Tigris at 100 ms, and the local model check of NEXT-WORK §14.8.
- **Retry semantics:** None for the timing. The admission caps' 429s keep `retry-after: 1`.
- **Who is affected:** Every deployment that does not set the name.
  - Beyond appends: consumer groups on busy shards (`:pull` and `:settle`, most of all a consumer that pulls few records and settles each pull before the next; the run leases of the owner's consumer-group decision of 2026-10-07, not part of this record, spread the two cycles over up to `max` records), plain seals and closes, and the fleet's seal fences (fleet-internal, timing only).
  - A bare binary (the `defaults` family) and CI's SDK smoke (`sdk/smoke.mjs`, whose waits are seconds); `bench/docker/compose.yml` (sets only `WAL_GROUP_COMMIT`); the six `bench/sse-probes` scripts, which set none of the WAL names; the k2cost points that do not set `WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS` (L0, L1-tiny, L3-tiny, L5, L6, L7).
  - Edited to follow the default (each set 10 and now sets 100): `bench/costab/run-keyed.sh`, `run-mature.sh`, `run-soak.sh`, `run-split.sh`, `run-wide.sh` and `wedge-liveness.sh`; `bench/docker/harness/cluster-deploy.sh`; `bench/fleet/local-fanout.sh` (and so `ci-fanout.sh`) and `deploy-fleet.sh`; `bench/soak/mt-tenants.sh`, `wc-ladder.sh` and `deploy-region.sh` (the default of `SOAK_WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS`); the recipes of `docs/STAGING.md` and `docs/GUIDE-COMPOSER.md`; and the eight effective-configuration families that mirror those scripts. The k2cost points that take `WAL_GAP` (L1-full, L2-full, L2-tiny, L3-full) default to 100, and the field harness's default posture is `P-100`; `P-exp` now pins 10 ms, which the recorded cells that name it ran.
  - Not changed: the rigs that pass `--wal-flush-gap-ms 2` (conformance, CI's field gate, `scripts/platform-e2e.mjs` and its negative twin, the LiveFeed canary and certification, `scripts/mt-noisy-campaign.mjs`, `bench/livefeed-perf/run-one.sh`); the reports and evidence that record runs at 10 ms (`docs/PUMP-AB-REPORT.md`, `bench/sinmax-report.md`, the effective-configuration evidence of 2026-09-24).
  - On Compute the environment is merged per project (RUNBOOK §7.3): a project that once set 10 keeps 10 until its environment is restated. Whether any does is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11).
- **Given up:** Acknowledgement time on busy shards and for producers with few appends in flight, for fewer WAL writes on busy shards: about 10.8 WAL PUTs a second per saturated shard against 19.1 measured at 10 ms, about $107 against $189 per busy shard-month at Tigris's request price (the owner's tier analysis, interpolated from local measurements; not part of this repository).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned (red on the unchanged code: left `("wal-flush-gap-ms", "WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS", "10")`, right `"100"`)
  - src/config/tests.rs::the_default_commit_pipeline_is_the_pump_and_the_switch_still_selects_the_tick (red: left `(1, 10, 6, 25)`, right `(1, 100, 6, 25)`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests.rs::the_parsed_shard_settings_reach_every_shard_engine (red: left `((true, 10ms, 6ms), ...)`, right `((true, 100ms, 6ms), ...)`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests/write_tier.rs::an_isolated_append_waits_for_one_wal_write_and_the_rest_of_the_gap (new; red: `6 appends back to back take at least 556ms (flush starts gap + settle apart) and less than 806ms (the gap counted from the end of each WAL write): took 336.7555ms`; also the exact count of one WAL object and one pump flush per append)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_fixture_matches_scrubbed_parse (the fixture equals a parse in a cleared environment)
  - Not pinned: the admission consequence; the consumer-group, seal and seal-fence timing; any rig at the default gap end to end (every rig that starts the binary with a timing budget passes 2 ms); the field.
- **Risk reason:** medium. In the engine this is timing and the object-store request count only: no request that succeeded fails for that reason, and an idle shard's append is acknowledged as before. But the acknowledgement time of a successful append on a busy shard, and the rate of a producer that waits for each acknowledgement, change by up to the gap, and through the admission caps a load that was served can now be partly answered 429 (retryable). Clients with deadlines near 100-300 ms on appends are the ones to watch. Consumer groups are the second surface: on a busy shard a pull and a settle each wait up to about two pump cycles (about 0.2 s plus two WAL writes), so a consumer with pull or settle deadlines near 0.2-0.5 s, or one that settles each pull before the next, is the other client to watch. WIRE-MATRIX is unchanged: it states no acknowledgement time, and its 429 lines are unchanged. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the conformance suite, the platform e2e, the LiveFeed certification, the SDK smoke and any field run; no formal receipt is staled (`src/config/cli.rs`, `src/config/tests.rs` and `src/bootstrap/tests*` feed none). Awaits ratification.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent read-only review of the branch (2026-10-08, finding F3) found the record incomplete: the change times every operation the committer answers at durability, not only appends. Corrected the same day: "Endpoint" names the plain seal and close-only, the seal fence and the consumer-group operations with the record load that precedes a pull and a settle, "After" gives their derived waits, and "Who is affected", "Pinning tests" ("Not pinned") and "Risk reason" name the consumer surface. The code paths were re-read for the correction; the waits are derived, not measured.

### #89 82a14a8f — A takeover coordinated on an instance that does not own the old final's segment fences it at the owner, and every answer but the owner's parsed closed-report leaves the seal resumable

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §5 F1-a, the owner's decision; the sender, step 3 of the F1-a plan, over the receiver of #88. The plan's defaults are taken on the owner's instruction: the conservative mapping (every non-200 is Resumable, never ChangedIncarnation), a 40 s relay deadline and zero `Streams-Replay-To` hops, and the formal scope below.
- **Surface:** both (product and raw)
- **Endpoint:** every path through `claim_seal` (`src/application/lifecycle.rs`): product `POST /v1/streams/{name}:seal` (plain, and with a final record); raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}` with `Stream-Closed: true`. The coordinator relays to the owner's `POST /v1/internal/seal-fence/{*name}?fence_to=` (#88).
- **Condition:** All of these hold:
  - the collection holds a lapsed final-bearing claim (the claim owes its final and `claimed_ms` is older than `SEAL_CLAIM_MS`, 15 s);
  - the old final's segment is on a shard the ring assigns to ANOTHER instance;
  - the request is handled on that non-owner.
- **Before:** The takeover's fence resolved the shard locally and mapped the non-owner answer to `Resumable("segment engine unavailable")`. Every such attempt answered product 503 `seal_incomplete` (`retryable: true`, "segment engine unavailable; the seal is resumable") or raw 503 `seal_incomplete`, until a request happened to land on the owner. The collection stayed Sealing.
- **After:** `fence_segment_for_key` resolves the shard as before. On `ResolveError::NotOwner { owner }` it relays the fence at the reserved generation to that owner (`src/application/lifecycle/fence_relay.rs`), carrying the four target headers and the fleet credential; the owner answers its committer's closed-report only after the fence is durable (#88). Only a 200 whose body parses as `{"closed": bool}` is a verdict:
  - `false`: the new claim installs (`install_reserved_claim`, newest reservation only). A plain product `:seal` then answers 200: its segment closes are relayed as before. A raw close with its final then meets the existing non-owner answer at submission, 409 `not_ring_owner` + `Streams-Replay-To: <owner>`, and its exact retry at the owner completes. A product seal-with-final takes the same submission path to its final append (not pinned by a test of its own).
  - `true`: the old operation's close committed, so its transition is completed on its behalf from the foreign instance (`mark_final_committed`, then `run_seal` of the old operation), and the request gets the answer a takeover coordinated on the owner gives today (`AlreadySealed`: product 409 `sealed`, `retryable: false`; raw Conflict `sealed`).
  - Anything else is `Resumable` and the old claim stays untouched and unmarked: no published peer URL, 401 after the send's one token refresh, 404, 409 of any code (the redirect is never followed), 5xx including 503 `fence_unconfirmed`, an unparsable 200, a transport error, the 40 s deadline. The client sees today's 503 `seal_incomplete` with the relay's reason, for example "seal-fence relay to inst-b answered 409 Conflict; the seal is resumable".
  - Unchanged: takeovers coordinated on the owner; every claim that has not lapsed; the rule that a timestamp only decides when a takeover may start; the segment-close relay.
- **Retry semantics:** Every relay refusal keeps 503 `seal_incomplete` retryable. Each retry re-reads the descriptor, reserves a higher generation and re-resolves ownership, and the fence at the owner is the maximum, so an orphaned lower fence is dominated. An attempt can now wait up to 40 s for the owner where it failed at once, and up to six relays (six times 40 s) in a pathological takeover race, since `claim_seal` loops at most six times.
- **Who is affected:**
  - clients of multi-instance fleets after a final-bearing seal crashed or stalled: collections that stayed Sealing until a request reached the owner now complete from any instance;
  - SDK retry loops that saw a permanently retryable collection;
  - JWT-only fleets see today's 503 until the platform mints `seal-fence` (the plan's commit 5, awaiting the owner);
  - single-instance deployments are unaffected.
- **Given up:** An immediate 503 on a non-owner: the relay may wait for the owner.
- **Pinning tests** (src/dst/tests/seal_fence_relay.rs; two instances over one store, the ring active on both with the stream's shard on B, the coordinator A; each asserts that A holds no writer for that shard):
  - a_non_owner_seal_takes_over_through_the_owners_fence (wrong ingress, product): a plain `:seal` on A answers 200; the collection is sealed; B's durable fence row holds the reserved generation; the stream holds exactly `[{"n":0}]`. Red: left 503 (`seal_incomplete`, "segment engine unavailable; the seal is resumable"), right 200.
  - a_non_owner_raw_close_takeover_installs_then_redirects (wrong ingress, raw): a close with another final on A answers 409 + `streams-replay-to: inst-b`; the claim is the new operation's at the reserved generation and owes its final; the exact retry at B answers 2xx and the stream holds that final once and the old one never. Red: left 503 (`seal_incomplete`), right 409.
  - a_relayed_fence_reports_an_old_close_that_already_won (competing, the old close committed first): `AlreadySealed`; sealed under the old operation; its final once. Red: `Err(Resumable("segment engine unavailable"))`.
  - a_relayed_fence_is_decided_after_the_old_final_queued_ahead_of_it (competing, the old final queued ahead in the owner's held committer): `AlreadySealed`; the old close answers 2xx; sealed under the old operation; its final once. Red: `the relayed fence never reached the owner's engine`.
  - a_relayed_fence_refuses_the_old_final_that_arrives_after_it (competing, `false` is a barrier): `Installed` at the reserved generation; the old close released afterwards answers >= 400; the stream holds exactly `[{"n":0}]`; the claim is the new operation's; a close at the old generation is `SealSuperseded`. Red: `takeover did not install: Err(Resumable("segment engine unavailable"))`.
  - a_second_ownership_move_leaves_the_takeover_resumable_and_unchased (owner movement): with B's ring moving the shard to a third instance, the takeover is Resumable "answered 409"; B holds no writer; the claim is unchanged and the reservation stands; after convergence the retry installs above it; after the shard moves to A, A's fresh engine holds the fence and refuses the old generation. Red: `resumable for another reason: segment engine unavailable`.
  - a_relay_without_a_peer_url_or_credential_leaves_the_claim_untouched (wrong ingress, sender side): no peer URL gives Resumable "has no published peer URL", a credential B refuses gives Resumable "answered 401"; the claim is unchanged and B has no fence row. Red: `resumable for another reason: segment engine unavailable`.
  - a_relayed_fence_in_a_failed_owner_group_never_installs (crash around durability, a failed write): the old close and the relayed fence share a held group that fails; the takeover is Resumable "answered 503", never installed; the claim is unchanged; the retry installs, B's reopened engine holds that fence, and a close at the old generation is `SealSuperseded`. Red: `the relayed fence never reached the owner's engine`.
  - a_durable_relayed_fence_whose_reply_is_lost_survives_the_owners_restart (crash around durability, after it): with B's dispatch held, the relayed fence reaches B's engine and the takeover stays pending; B shuts down; the takeover is either Resumable with the claim unchanged or Installed at that fence, never sealed (observed: Resumable, "answered 503 Service Unavailable", the retired engine's refusal); B's next incarnation holds a fence at least that high, and the takeover completes against it. Red: `the relayed fence never reached the owner's engine`.
  - unchanged and green: the six `dst_tests::durability_fences` tests, `durability_failures::a_fence_in_a_failed_group_reports_failure_not_closed`, `dst_tests::seal_fencing` (10), `security_lineage::seal_fence_resolves_the_owning_project`, `dst_tests::seal_convergence` and `dst_tests::seal_fence_receiver`.
- **Formal:** `SealProtocol.tla` gains the relay arm in `TFence` (the fence queued on the owner's engine from a non-owner) and a `Timeout` at `fwait` in two-process layouts; `MC_SealTakeover_xproc.cfg` sets `MaxTimeout = 1`; ASM-SEAL-OWNER is reworded; `fence_relay.rs` joins the TLA-002 and TLA-003 source paths. Both receipts are stale until the re-record; TLC was not run for this record.
- **Risk reason:** medium: a permanently retryable refusal becomes completion, and a takeover can finish an old operation's transition from a foreign instance. Safety rests on the unchanged committer barrier, which the receiver answers from only after the fence is durable, and on the conservative mapping: only a parsed 200 is a verdict, so a lost, refused or timed-out relay can never install a claim over a close that may still commit. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the TLA-002 and TLA-003 re-record, and the platform e2e.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. Made on the owner's instruction of 2026-09-30 ("Do them all in that order! use your own judgement"); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-01.

### #8 668bc80c + 5d60dc63 — Append routed by a pre-transition descriptor is retried on the successor

- **Program item:** bug #9
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Raw POST /v1/stream/{name} (append/close). Product POST /v1/streams/{name}/records and POST /v1/streams/{name}/records:batch, plus the final-record append inside POST /v1/streams/{name}:seal. Also internal consumer DLQ delivery appends (AppendService::execute).
- **Condition:** The engine answers Closed for the segment the append was routed to (an engine closure, not one the descriptor declares), and the descriptor was not sealed. Typical causes: the descriptor was cached (5 s TTL, or re-inserted by a raced registry read) before a split or merge, so the append went to a sealed parent or a merged-away child. Also covered: the registry refresh fails, the stream was deleted, or the name was deleted and recreated. Declared closures (descriptor sealed or sealing, closed_tail_failure) are excluded.
- **Before:** Retries happened only when the PREPARED descriptor was already 'wrapped' (segments.len() > 1 or a pending transition). An unwrapped descriptor (for example one from before the first split) got 1 attempt. Result for a LIVE collection: raw 409 {"error":{"code":"stream_closed","message":"stream is closed"}} with Stream-Closed: true and Stream-Next-Offset; product 409 {"error":{"code":"sealed","message":"collection is sealed","retryable":false}}. A wrapped descriptor was invalidated and re-read with a registry GET. If the GET errored or found nothing, the client got the same 409 (fail-open). If the fresh route was live, the client got the same 409 even when it named a different segment than the one attempted. If a transition was pending, the append waited for it (a ticket error gave 503 segment_transition with Retry-After: 1). Otherwise it slept 10/20/30/40 ms and re-prepared, for 4 attempts. Exhaustion gave raw 503 segment_transition 'segment map transition did not converge; retry' with NO Retry-After, and product 503 temporarily_unavailable with retryable:true and no Retry-After. A recreated name got 409 stream_closed (product: sealed).
- **After:** Every descriptor retries an engine closure: 4 attempts, waiting 10, 20 and 30 ms before attempts 2, 3 and 4, with no sleep after the last. The closure goes to the client only when the prepared descriptor is sealed, or when a fresh registry read (closure_is_current) still routes the key to the SAME seg_id, live, with no pending transition. In both cases the 409 is the same as before. Otherwise: a stale route lands on the successor and returns normal success (raw 204/200, product 200). A refresh read error gives raw 503 segment_transition with Retry-After: 1 (product 503 temporarily_unavailable, retryable:true, Retry-After: 1). A changed epoch (recreated name) gives raw 409 {"code":"target_incarnation_changed","message":"append target incarnation changed"} with no Stream-Closed or Stream-Next-Offset. The product surface gives 409 {"code":"conflict","message":"producer or configuration conflict","retryable":false}. A pending transition is waited out, then retried. A descriptor that no longer exists is retried, and the next prepare returns the missing/gone answer (404 not_found or 410 gone) instead of 409. Exhaustion gives the same 503 codes, now with Retry-After: 1. Declared closures return 409 at once, with no refresh.
- **Retry semantics:** A permanent 409 (retryable:false) on a live collection becomes a success, after up to 60 ms of internal retry. A permanent 409 becomes a retryable 503 with Retry-After: 1 when the refresh cannot be read. The exhaustion 503 gains Retry-After: 1. The recreate case stays a permanent 409, but the code changes: raw stream_closed becomes target_incarnation_changed and loses the Stream-Closed header, and product sealed becomes the generic conflict. Deleted-mid-retry changes from 409 to 404/410. Every engine closure on an unsealed descriptor, including on unsegmented streams, now costs an invalidate plus one registry GET.
- **Who is affected:** SDK/product writers and raw writers on collections that split or merge. Any writer whose request met a closed segment through a stale cached descriptor. Clients that branch on raw stream_closed/Stream-Closed or product sealed (the recreate case now looks different). Internal DLQ delivery.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/topology_scaling.rs::an_append_routed_by_a_pre_split_descriptor_lands_on_the_child
  - src/dst/tests/topology_scaling.rs::an_append_routed_by_a_pre_merge_descriptor_lands_on_the_merged_segment
  - src/dst/tests/topology_scaling.rs::an_append_routed_by_a_pre_seal_descriptor_is_still_refused_as_sealed
  - src/dst/tests/append_application.rs::r02_a_closure_the_refresh_cannot_confirm_is_retryable_not_final
  - src/dst/tests/append_application.rs::r02_a_sealed_descriptors_closure_costs_no_refresh
  - src/dst/tests/append_application.rs::r02_a_closure_from_a_replaced_incarnation_is_not_the_new_streams
  - NOT pinned: the exhaustion 503 with Retry-After, the 10/20/30 ms waits, and the 4-attempt count. No test drives the loop to exhaustion.
- **Risk reason:** The main effect turns a wrong permanent 409 into a success. It also changes statuses and codes on error cases clients may branch on: the recreate case (stream_closed plus Stream-Closed, or product sealed, becomes target_incarnation_changed or the generic product conflict), refresh failure (409 becomes 503), and delete during retry (409 becomes 404/410). It also changes retry semantics by adding Retry-After: 1 to exhaustion.
- **Check against commit:** The recorded text is accurate, but '409 sealed' is product wording only; raw clients got 409 stream_closed with Stream-Closed: true and Stream-Next-Offset. On product, TargetIncarnationChanged surfaces as the generic 409 'conflict' (retryable:false), not a dedicated code. The Retry-After value is 1 s. The 'Gone' path (409 -> 404/410) and the extra registry GET per engine closure on unsegmented streams are not recorded. 5d60dc63 says the existing tests pin the attempt count, but no test found exercises exhaustion.

### #10 2374751d — Settle retry delay and extend visibility are clamped to 12 h

- **Program item:** rank 24
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:settle, body fields retries[].delayMs and extends[].visibilityMs. The :pull visibilityMs and consumer PUT visibilityTimeoutMs clamps are unchanged; they only moved to the shared owner in src/queue.rs.
- **Condition:** A settle whose retries[].delayMs exceeds 43,200,000, or whose extends[].visibilityMs is below 1,000 (including 0) or above 43,200,000.
- **Before:** The committer received the u64 values unchanged and computed deadline = now + window as i64. By value: from 12 h up to about i64::MAX - now, the window was honoured exactly (for example a 24 h delay or extend). Around 9e18 the lease never expires: the record is never redelivered, never reaches maxAttempts, and never dead-letters, and its routing key stays blocked until an ack or a consumer delete. Near i64::MAX the add overflows: a wrapped deadline in release, and a committer-task panic under overflow checks. Above i64::MAX (for example u64::MAX) the cast sign-wraps to now - 1, and the next pull redelivers at once (attempts: 2). An extend with visibilityMs 0 meant 'visible now'; 1-999 ms was honoured. The settle response was 200 with counts.
- **After:** delayMs is clamped to 0..=43,200,000 (default 1,000 when omitted; 0 still releases at once). Extend visibilityMs is clamped to 1,000..=43,200,000 (default: the consumer's visibilityTimeoutMs). QueueOp windows are u32, so now + window is always defined. The response is unchanged: 200 with the same {acked, retried, extended, dlq, stale, ...} counts. The clamp is silent: no error, and no field reports the applied window. Lease rows already written unbounded by an older binary are not healed.
- **Retry semantics:** Redelivery timing changes. A retry delay or extend above 12 h now redelivers at 12 h, where it used to be honoured exactly. An extend of 0-999 ms now holds for 1 s; to release at once a client must now send retries[{delayMs: 0}]. Absurd values are bounded instead of producing a permanent lease or an immediate redelivery. No status change: settle still never errors on these fields.
- **Who is affected:** Product consumer users: SDK message.retry({delayMs}) and message.extend({visibilityMs}) forward user numbers verbatim. Direct HTTP settle callers.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/consumer_dlq.rs::a_retry_asking_for_the_longest_delay_is_not_redelivered_at_once
  - src/dst/tests/consumer_dlq.rs::a_retry_delay_beyond_the_lease_window_is_held_to_twelve_hours
  - src/dst/tests/consumer_dlq.rs::an_extend_asking_for_the_longest_visibility_is_not_redelivered_at_once
  - src/dst/tests/consumer_dlq.rs::an_extended_visibility_is_held_between_one_second_and_twelve_hours
  - src/dst/tests/consumer_dlq.rs::a_pulled_visibility_beyond_the_lease_window_is_held_to_twelve_hours
  - src/queue.rs::tests::lease_windows_are_held_between_their_floor_and_twelve_hours
  - src/queue.rs::tests::quality_lease_windows_follow_the_u64_clamp
- **Risk reason:** No status or code changes, but the queue's redelivery semantics change silently. Legitimate windows above 12 h are shortened, and an extend-0 'release now' becomes a 1 s hold. Clients get no signal that their window was altered.
- **Check against commit:** The recorded text is correct but narrower than the change. Extend visibilityMs 1-999 also becomes 1 s, not only 0. Legitimate windows between 12 h and about 9.2e18 ms, which were honoured exactly before, are now cut to 12 h. The clamp is silent in the response.

### #12 71345c03 — HTTP/1.1 request-head and idle keep-alive deadline of 120 s

- **Program item:** rank 17
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Every HTTP/1.1 connection on the server's single listener (bootstrap::run -> http::serve_h1 -> http::serve::h1_builder). That covers raw /v1/stream, product /v1/streams, /health, /v1/debug, operator routes and fleet-internal peer routes alike.
- **Condition:** A connection waits for a request head longer than h1_header_timeout (default 120 s). This covers a fresh socket that sends nothing, a slow or partial head, and an idle keep-alive after a completed response.
- **Before:** hyper's documented 30 s header_read_timeout was silently dropped because no timer was installed, and there was no server-side idle close. Idle keep-alive, headless and partial-head sockets were held indefinitely (a file descriptor plus a task) until the client or edge closed them.
- **After:** hyper closes the connection at the deadline without writing any HTTP response: no 408, just a FIN, or an RST if unread bytes are queued. The timer runs only while waiting for a head and re-arms after each response, so in-flight responses, long-polls (25 s max) and SSE bodies are unaffected. New knob SSE_H1_HEADER_TIMEOUT_MS, in ms: values > 0 apply; 0 or unparseable keep the default, so it can never be disabled. It is reported as http.h1_header_timeout_ms in the 'effective configuration (redacted)' startup log. The bench and livebench client pool_idle_timeout went from 120 s to 60 s.
- **Retry semantics:** The server now closes idle connections. A client or edge that reuses a pooled connection idle for about 120 s or more can race the close. It then sees a connection reset or EOF before any response, or the edge returns 502/503. That failure is transport-level and retryable, but HTTP clients usually do not auto-retry non-idempotent POSTs such as appends. Clients whose pool idle is under 120 s are unaffected: the SDK's fetch on Node/undici defaults to 4 s; peers, pilot and the store client use 4 s; edgesim 90 s; the bench tools now 60 s.
- **Who is affected:** All HTTP/1.1 clients that hold keep-alives idle for more than 120 s. The main one is the platform edge's upstream pool, whose idle timeout is unmeasured per the commit; long-idle raw-client pools are another. Slowloris or half-open sockets. Operators get the new knob and a bound on file-descriptor use.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/http/serve.rs::tests::headless_connection_is_closed_at_the_deadline
  - src/http/serve.rs::tests::idle_keep_alive_is_served_inside_and_closed_past_the_deadline
  - src/http/serve.rs::tests::in_flight_response_outlives_the_deadline
  - src/config/tests.rs::default_values_are_pinned (asserts the 120 s default)
  - NOT asserted: src/config/tests.rs::env_overlay_applies_with_legacy_parse_semantics sets SSE_H1_HEADER_TIMEOUT_MS=0 but asserts nothing about h1_header_timeout. No test checks that a positive override is applied, and no SSE-specific test exists.
- **Risk reason:** No status or code change, but every route's connection lifetime changes. If the platform edge's upstream pool idle exceeds 120 s, stale reuse turns into intermittent edge 5xx errors or resets on otherwise-good requests, including appends. The knob can mitigate this, and the commit itself says the edge idle is unmeasured.
- **Check against commit:** The recorded text is accurate. Nuances: the deadline also closes a slowly dribbled partial head (the test pins this), although the commit prose says it 'never runs while a head is parsed'. The commit claims 'Config pins: ... the 0-is-filtered overlay', but the overlay row has no assertion, so the 0 filter is unpinned. SSE is unaffected by mechanism only; no test covers it.

### #14 fd810688 — Panicking shard opener fails its open instead of wedging its prefix

- **Program item:** rank 15
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Every raw and product request that resolves a shard prefix through OpenGate::get_or_open. Raw: POST/GET/HEAD/PUT/DELETE /v1/stream/{name} including ?live=sse/long-poll connect. Product: /v1/streams/{name} create, /records append/read/:sse, consumers. Operator: /health readiness. Process: the unready watchdog exit. The PostingsCache half has no wire surface.
- **Condition:** The shard opener panics, while building its future or while polling it. The production route: bootstrap::on_slatedb_rt turns a panic in the SlateDB open task into a panic on the opener task. For the postings half: a panic in the spawned owned postings load.
- **Before:** The open task died with its watch sender and the in-flight marker was never cleared. The first caller and every coalesced waiter got OpenOutcome::Failed("shard open task vanished"). Every later caller for that prefix coalesced onto the dead channel and got the same answer, until process restart: no strike, no holdoff, health not recorded, and shutdown counted a phantom pending open until its grace ran out. Rendered as: raw append/other raw routes 500 {"error":{"code":"shard_open","message":"open shard \<p>: shard open task vanished"}}; raw read/SSE connect 500 {"error":{"code":"shard_open_failed",...}}; product append 500 {"error":{"code":"append_failed","retryable":false}}; product create 500 shard_open retryable:true; product consumers 500 shard_open retryable:true; product read/SSE connect 503 temporarily_unavailable retryable:true (every ResolveError maps there). Postings: every later read of the key spun 4 Wait turns on the dead channel, loaded uncached, and prefetch stayed disabled; results were correct and only slower.
- **After:** unwind_proof maps the panic to Err("shard open panicked: \<payload>"), which takes the normal failed-open arm: strike+1, holdoff armed (holdoff_for(strikes): 6 s after a first strike, then 12/24/48, capped at 60 s), health.failed recorded, marker cleared, waiters answered. The first caller and its coalesced waiters get the same 500 code as before with the message 'shard open panicked: \<payload>'. Callers during the holdoff get 503 with Retry-After = remaining whole seconds (min 1): raw {"error":{"code":"shard_moving","message":"shard not currently serving here; retry"}}; product append 503 temporarily_unavailable retryable:true + Retry-After; product create 503 shard_moving retryable:true + Retry-After; product consumers 503 shard_moving retryable:true; product read unchanged (503 temporarily_unavailable). After the holdoff the next caller re-runs the opener: success serves normally, and a repeat panic gives another 500 and a longer holdoff. /health: panicked prefixes now count toward ShardHealth.failed, so an instance that never opened any shard and has >=3 distinct failed prefixes reports 503 'shard storage unavailable' and the unready watchdog eventually exits it (process). Postings: a panicked owned load is a failed load; the next read leads a fresh owned load and the cache recovers.
- **Retry semantics:** For callers after the first: a non-retryable, permanent 500 (shard_open/shard_open_failed; product append retryable:false) becomes a retryable 503 shard_moving (product: temporarily_unavailable retryable:true) with Retry-After, and the prefix can recover without a restart. The first caller's status and code are unchanged; only the message differs.
- **Who is affected:** SDK and raw clients hitting a prefix whose open panicked; they now see 500-then-503-with-Retry-After instead of 500 forever. Operators: /health can go unready and the watchdog can restart an instance whose opens all panic, which never happened before; shutdown no longer waits on a phantom open. Postings cache: internal latency only.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/sharddir/unwind.rs::tests::a_panicking_opener_fails_its_open_and_the_next_attempt_installs
  - src/sharddir/unwind.rs::tests::a_synchronous_opener_panic_is_the_same_failed_open
  - src/postings_cache/tests.rs::a_panicking_owned_load_clears_its_single_flight_marker
- **Risk reason:** The status and code change for an error case clients may branch on: raw 500 shard_open/shard_open_failed becomes 503 shard_moving with Retry-After, and product append retryable:false becomes temporarily_unavailable retryable:true. The change is strictly toward retryability and recovery, and the trigger (an opener panic) is rare. The pinning tests are gate-level (OpenOutcome), not HTTP-level; the 503/500 rendering relies on the shared existing ResolveError mappings. /health and watchdog behaviour also change for a node whose opens all panic.
- **Check against commit:** None against the recorded text. Nuances: (1) the commit message says 'No wire change', but later callers' status changes from 500 to 503; the recorded text states this correctly. (2) The 503 lasts only for the holdoff window, after which the opener is retried; it is not 503 forever. (3) Product read paths already returned 503 temporarily_unavailable, so they see no status change.

### #16 d93b421b — Live SSE tail on a retired engine gets a typed EngineRetired cutoff

- **Program item:** rank 14
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** GET /v1/stream/{name}?live=sse (raw, SingleSource) and GET /v1/streams/{name}/records:sse (product, SingleSource or LineageSource LiveLocal tail). Operator: GET /v1/debug/load gains sse_livefeed.cutoff_engine_retired.
- **Condition:** A live SSE session (parked at the tail, driving a read, or a joiner below the frozen head) whose feed source pins an engine incarnation that closes while ownership stays on this instance: the acker's close_reason on a fatal store error, a required worker's exit, a sub-tick ownership flap, or an explicit shutdown retirement. Ownership is checked first, so a moved tail is still WrongOwner.
- **Before:** cut_off/read_batch checked ownership only. begin_close woke parked sessions once; each re-checked ownership, found it unchanged, and re-parked on the dead handle's notify. The client kept an open HTTP 200 text/event-stream with keep-alives forever: no data, no control, no EOF. A reconnect resolved the replacement engine but install_source returned AlreadyCurrent for an equal-length signature, so the reconnect joined the dead feed and hung too. A joiner below the frozen head spun on the catch-up retry every 100 ms. In the drive race the dead engine's tail ring could serve a record before the session re-parked forever.
- **After:** live_tail_cutoff(owned, engine) returns SourceCutoff::EngineRetired when the pinned engine is_closed(); both the park guard and the live-tail read_batch guards use it. On the wire this is the existing typed nonterminal disconnect: the chunked body ends (EOF) with no terminal control and no sealed/streamClosed flag, and no record is served from the retired engine. FEED_CUTOFF_ENGINE_RETIRED and FEED_TOPOLOGY_DISCONNECTS increment, and /v1/debug/load sse_livefeed.cutoff_engine_retired is a new additive key. The feed tears down. A resume from the client's cursor may first get 503 while the old incarnation terminates or its holdoff runs: raw shard_closing with Retry-After 1, or shard_moving with Retry-After = remaining holdoff (the close is a Died departure, so it strikes); product 503 temporarily_unavailable retryable:true. It then gets 200 served by a NEW engine incarnation that delivers new appends.
- **Retry semantics:** A hang becomes a disconnect that clients must resume from their cursor. The resume can meet a retryable 503 (shard_closing/shard_moving/temporarily_unavailable) before it succeeds. No terminal signal is emitted, so clients must not treat the EOF as stream closed.
- **Who is affected:** Raw and product SSE subscribers (SDK and raw clients) whose engine retires under the same owner; they now see an EOF and must resume. Operators get a new /v1/debug/load counter; docs/LIVE-FEED.md says its deltas should track engine_closed ops events.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/livefeed_engine_retired.rs::livefeed_engine_retired_under_the_same_owner_cuts_a_parked_session
  - src/dst/tests/livefeed_engine_retired.rs::livefeed_engine_retired_under_the_same_owner_cuts_a_parked_lineage_session
  - src/dst/tests/livefeed_engine_retired.rs::livefeed_engine_retired_before_a_drive_is_a_typed_read_cutoff
- **Risk reason:** This changes the retry semantics of an error condition on both public SSE surfaces: a new trigger for mid-stream nonterminal EOF, and the resume can hit a 503 holdoff first. False positives are not possible, because a closed incarnation can never serve again, and the prior behaviour was a silent hang, so no working session is lost. All three pinning tests use the product route; the raw route shares SingleSource but has no dedicated raw-surface test.
- **Check against commit:** None against the recorded text. The lineage (LiveLocal) leg is product-only in practice; the single-segment leg covers both surfaces.

### #19 527d3d3a — Read-byte quota on :scan

- **Program item:** rank 28 step A
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** GET /v1/streams/{name}:scan[?cursor=&maxBytes=] (product_entry scan arm -> src/product/scan.rs::product_scan). Also re-plumbs the debit for GET /v1/streams/{name}/records and /records:long-poll with no wire change.
- **Condition:** Enforce auth mode (STREAMS_AUTH_MODE=enforce, so a verified principal exists), the project policy sets quotas.read_bytes_per_sec > 0, and the project's read-byte bucket is below zero (in debt from earlier served bytes). Deployment-bearer (Off/Shadow) requests carry no principal and are never checked or debited.
- **Before:** A :scan in read debt was served normally: 200, JSON array of {routingKey,value|valueB64}, Content-Type application/json, Cache-Control no-store, Prisma-Scan-Complete or Prisma-Next-Scan-Cursor. Scan pages (up to 8 MiB) were never debited, so they never pushed the project into debt either. Records GET/long-poll were debited after the fact from the finished Response's exact size hint (debit_read_response).
- **After:** check_read_quota runs at the scan arm before anything else in product_scan: in debt the answer is 429 {"error":{"code":"project_rate_limit","message":"the project's rate quota is exhausted; retry","retryable":true}} with retry-after: \<secs> (>=1), Cache-Control no-store and the project audit tag. Every served scan page debits body.len() (the framed page) through debit_read_bytes. Because the check comes before key, cursor and existence checks, a scan in debt with a missing or wrong key, a bad cursor, or no stream now gets 429 where it used to get 400 missing_key/invalid_cursor, 403 wrong_key or 404 not_found. A refused page is never debited. The records GET debit moves to render_product_read (payload.len()), which should be byte-identical to the old size hint; a 204 long-poll timeout debits 0.
- **Retry semantics:** 200 -> 429 retryable (retryable:true + Retry-After) while the project is in debt. The SDK's req() retries 429 at most 3 times and caps each sleep at 5 s (about 15 s in total). If the debt lasts longer, the scan() async iterator throws StreamsError(429, project_rate_limit, retryable=true). The iterator does not expose the next scan cursor, so an SDK caller cannot resume that export partway and has to start a new scan. A raw-HTTP client can re-issue the same cursor after Retry-After.
- **Who is affected:** Product API and SDK users who run :scan exports on enforce-mode projects with a configured read_bytes_per_sec, when they read faster than their budget. Scan traffic now shares one bucket with records GET and long-poll, so heavy scans can cause 429s on records reads and the other way round. Projects without a read budget (0) and Off/Shadow deployments are unaffected.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/quota_read_volume.rs::scan_pages_draw_on_the_read_byte_quota
  - src/dst/tests/quota_enforcement.rs::volume_quotas_meter_appends_and_reads
- **Risk reason:** This adds a new retryable 429 class to a public route, but only under a quota condition: enforce mode, a non-zero read budget and a bucket in debt. The common path is unchanged. It is not permanent (Retry-After is provided, and scan cursors are stateless, so re-issuing one is safe). It is still a real behaviour change for SDK exports: when debt outlasts the SDK's roughly 15 s bounded retry, the iterator ends partway and cannot be resumed. Neither the error-precedence change (429 before 400/403/404 while in debt) nor the debit of peer-relayed scan pages is pinned by a test.
- **Check against commit:** No mismatch. Precision: the check comes before the key, cursor and stream-existence checks (it only follows the principal's scope check), so 400/403/404 scan errors turn into 429 while in debt. The same commit also moves the records GET/long-poll debit from the Response size hint to the framed payload length, with no wire change.

### #20 f1c9e77e — Read-byte quota on consumer :pull

- **Program item:** rank 28 step B
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:pull (src/product/consumer_pull.rs::product_consumer_pull)
- **Condition:** Quota refusal: enforce mode (ConsumerAccess::Account(principal)), quotas.read_bytes_per_sec > 0, and the project's read-byte bucket in debt. Cache-Control change: every 200 pull response in all modes.
- **Before:** A pull in read debt was served normally: 200 {"messages":[...],"backlog":n} (an empty poll is also 200). Content-Type was application/json with NO Cache-Control header (json_ok never set it, although WIRE-MATRIX §2.15 documented no-store). Pulls were billed through meter_pull but never checked against or debited from the read-byte quota.
- **After:** The first statement of the handler is check_read_quota, which runs before consumer_key, authorize (the consumer lookup), activation, body parsing and leasing. In debt the answer is 429 {"error":{"code":"project_rate_limit","message":"the project's rate quota is exhausted; retry","retryable":true}} + retry-after: \<secs>, and no lease is taken. A 200 debits the framed body length, including empty polls of about 27 bytes, and now carries Cache-Control: no-store; content-type and body bytes are unchanged. Error precedence while in debt: 429 replaces 400 (missing or invalid key, invalid_body), 404 consumer_not_found and 409 consumer_deleted/consumer_deleting. The consumers.pull scope is still checked first, at product_entry.
- **Retry semantics:** 200 -> 429 retryable (retryable:true + Retry-After) while in debt. A refused pull leases nothing, so retrying is safe. The SDK's Consumer.pull() retries 429 at most 3 times with sleeps capped at 5 s. After that the `for await (… of consumer)` / messages() iterator throws and ends with status "failed"; the application has to restart its consumer loop. Adding Cache-Control: no-store on 200 does not change retry behaviour.
- **Who is affected:** Consumer-queue workloads (SDK and raw-HTTP product clients) on enforce-mode projects with a configured read_bytes_per_sec. Queue draining now shares one bucket with records GET, long-poll and :scan, so read traffic on any of those routes can stall consumers, and consumer traffic can cause 429s on reads. Browsers and intermediaries now see no-store on pull batches. Off/Shadow deployments (ConsumerAccess::Deployment) are never checked or debited.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/quota_read_volume.rs::consumer_pulls_draw_on_the_read_byte_quota
- **Risk reason:** This adds a retryable 429 class to a hot, steady-state route, but only while the project is in read debt. It is not permanent, and a refused pull leases nothing. It has the most exposure in this group: consumer workers previously never throttled by the read quota now can be, and the SDK consumer iterator ends after about 15 s of sustained debt. At documented budget sizes (the example policy uses 50 MiB/s) debt should be rare. Test gaps: the pinning test does not assert Cache-Control: no-store on the 200, does not check that the refused pull leased nothing (backlog and attempts are not inspected), and does not cover the error-precedence change.
- **Check against commit:** No mismatch. Precision: the check comes before key, consumer-existence and body validation, so those 400/404/409 errors turn into 429 while in debt; empty polls are debited too; Cache-Control: no-store is added only to the 200 (error responses from perr already had it); no test pins the no-store header.

### #24 74391f9c — Relayed read verdict is typed on the fleet page route

- **Program item:** item 22
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** Wire change: GET /v1/internal/segment-read/{\*name} with streams-internal-read-page: 1 (src/http/read.rs::respond_read/typed_refusal; coordinator src/application/read_remote.rs::remote_read_page/peer_refusal). Client-visible via GET /v1/streams/{name}/records?deliver=applied (non-long-poll pages the read service relays to another owner, allow_remote=true). Raw clients cannot set deliver=applied: ReadParams.deliver is serde-skipped.
- **Condition:** A product records read with deliver=applied is coordinated by a non-owner instance and relayed to the span owner, and the owner's read service decides CursorBeyondTail (applied cursor ahead of the applied tail). The internal typed body is also emitted for ChangedIncarnation, Missing and Gone decided by the read service.
- **Before:** Owner page route: every refusal used the public envelope, e.g. 409 {"error":{"code":"cursor_beyond_tail",...}}. The coordinator did not read the body and mapped by status only (404 Missing, 410 Gone, 401 Unauthorized, 409 ChangedIncarnation, 429/503 Retryable). The client therefore received 409 {"error":{"code":"target_mismatch","message":"stream incarnation changed","retryable":false}}. The SDK treats target_mismatch as non-retryable and threw, ending the applied subscription instead of rewinding.
