- **After:** Owner page route: for a verdict the read service decided (cursor_beyond_tail / changed_incarnation / missing / gone), the status is unchanged (409/409/404/410) and the body is {"refused":"\<verdict>"}. Every other refusal (ownership 409 + Streams-Replay-To, 503-class, 400/403 for the coordinator's key or cursor, and anything decided before the read service ran: 401, registry 404, 400 invalid_target, 409 stale_target), and every request without the header, keeps the public envelope. The coordinator reads the body first and decodes a typed body as the verdict whatever the status. An untyped answer keeps the old status mapping, including a bare 409 -> ChangedIncarnation, and error.code is never read. Client: 409 {"error":{"code":"cursor_beyond_tail","message":"cursor is ahead of the stream tail; resume from the durable cursor","retryable":false}}, the same answer the local owner path gives. Side effect: the coordinator now reads a non-2xx body before classifying it, so a body that exceeds the wire bound or fails mid-transfer becomes Remote -> 503 temporarily_unavailable where it used to use the status class.
- **Retry semantics:** The public error code changes from target_mismatch to cursor_beyond_tail for this case; status 409 and retryable:false stay the same. SDK: applied subscriptions go from a thrown error to a rewind to Prisma-Durable-Cursor. Rolling-deploy safe both ways: an old owner sends an untyped 409 (still ChangedIncarnation), and an old coordinator ignores the body and maps the unchanged status. stale_target is deliberately still ChangedIncarnation.
- **Who is affected:** Product API and SDK users of deliver=applied records reads that are served across owners (segmented streams whose span owner is another instance). Fleet peers: the page-route refusal body format changes, and both old and new versions tolerate it. Raw clients are unaffected.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/read_application.rs::relayed_applied_read_beyond_the_tail_keeps_the_owner_verdict
  - src/dst/tests/read_application.rs::the_page_route_types_its_refusal_and_the_public_route_keeps_its_envelope
  - src/application/read_remote_tests.rs::the_page_route_refusal_names_the_owner_verdict
  - src/application/read_remote_tests.rs::a_typed_refusal_is_relayed_and_an_untyped_answer_keeps_its_transport_class
- **Risk reason:** This changes a public product error code, for an error case the SDK explicitly branches on. The change restores the documented contract and turns a failure into a rewind, but by the rubric it is a code change. Tests pin the coordinator verdict (remote_read_page returns CursorBeyondTail), the internal wire body, and the envelope staying on the public route and on requests without the header. Nothing drives an end-to-end product HTTP 409 cursor_beyond_tail through a relay, or the SDK rewind, and the body-read-before-status side effect is not covered.
- **Check against commit:** No mismatch in substance. The wire change is on the fleet-internal page route (recorded as fleet-internal), but its only client-visible effect is a public product error-code change on GET /v1/streams/{name}/records?deliver=applied (target_mismatch -> cursor_beyond_tail), so surface is recorded as product. Long-poll is not affected: remote_read_page is skipped for ReadMode::LongPoll.

### #26 a8c99c23 — Append larger than a fresh bucket is 413; boot refuses unadmittable limits

- **Program item:** item 25
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Raw POST /v1/stream/{name}, including append-and-close. Product POST /v1/streams/{name}/records and /records:batch. Also process startup (ServerConfig::validate → main.rs exits 1) and the GET /v1/debug/load limiter counters.
- **Condition:** A content append whose body bytes exceed LIMIT_BYTES_PER_SEC×LIMIT_BURST_SECS (10,000,000 at defaults) or whose record count exceeds LIMIT_RECS_PER_SEC×LIMIT_BURST_SECS (10,000 at defaults), with no deferred producer verdict. Raw has no smaller per-POST record cap, so it is reachable at defaults. The product batch cap is MAX_BATCH_RECORDS=10,000 (400 batch_too_large above that), so the product records case is reachable only when record capacity is below 10,000. Boot: any limit knob that is non-finite, negative, a burst \<= 0, a rate×burst that overflows, or an enabled bucket with rate×burst \< 1.
- **Before:** Records over capacity. Raw: 429 {"error":{"code":"limit_records_per_sec","message":\<limit message>}} with retry-after = ceil((records − tokens)/rate) s, min 1 (1 only when the bucket is full); every retry refused, forever. Product: 429 {"error":{"code":"rate_limited","message":"admission or rate limit","retryable":true}} with retry-after. Decided in admit_usage after renew_ttl, so the refused request still renewed a sliding TTL, and it was counted by note_limit_refusal (/v1/debug/load). Bytes over capacity (non-close): raw 413 payload_too_large 'request exceeds the per-stream ingest capacity', product 413 body_too_large retryable:false. Also decided after renew_ttl, and also counted as a bytes limiter refusal. Close with content already got an early 413 (bytes, records, or a sub-token 'requests' arm). Boot accepted any float: LIMIT_BURST_SECS=0 made every append 429 forever; NaN, negative or infinite values silently disabled a limit; a sub-token bucket (e.g. 0.1 req/s × 2 s) made every append 429 limit_requests_per_sec forever.
- **After:** Both kinds are refused in parse_content before renew_ttl and before install_intent, with no retry-after. Raw: 413 {"error":{"code":"payload_too_large","message":"request exceeds the per-stream ingest records capacity"}} (bytes: '… ingest bytes capacity'). Product: 413 {"error":{"code":"body_too_large","message":"request body exceeds the limit","retryable":false}}. A deferred producer verdict still answers first (e.g. 400 invalid_body). note_limit_refusal is never reached, so /v1/debug/load counts transient 429s only. The admit_usage bytes-413 arm is deleted, and every 429 it answers now names a wait the bucket will honour. Boot: validate() rejects the postures above, prints 'Error: …LIMIT\_…' to stderr and exits with code 1. The rejection applies to all three buckets (bytes, requests, records).
- **Retry semantics:** Records over capacity: a retryable 429 with Retry-After becomes a permanent 413 without Retry-After (product retryable true -> false). The request could never be admitted under either behaviour. Bytes over capacity: status and code unchanged (already a permanent 413); only the decision point, the message text and the debug counter change. Startup: some configurations that used to boot now exit 1.
- **Who is affected:** Raw clients that POST more than 10,000 records at defaults: SDK loops that retried the 429 now get a terminal 413. Product SDK users only on cells whose record capacity is below 10,000. Operators whose env sets a limit knob to NaN, inf, a negative value (previously an ad-hoc 'disable'), burst 0 or a sub-token rate: the process now refuses to start. The repo's bench and soak scripts set only large finite values, so none of them is refused. Dashboards that read the /v1/debug/load limiter counters. TTL streams: an oversized append no longer renews the sliding TTL.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/admission_maintenance.rs::over_capacity_record_count_is_a_permanent_413_not_a_429
  - src/dst/tests/admission_maintenance.rs::product_batch_over_record_capacity_is_413_without_retry_after
  - src/dst/tests/admission_maintenance.rs::a_deferred_producer_verdict_outranks_the_capacity_refusal
  - src/usage/runtime_tests.rs::the_runtime_owner_decides_only_the_requests_own_size
  - src/usage/runtime_tests.rs::permanent_refusal_is_exactly_fresh_bucket_refusal (proptest, 1,024 cases)
  - src/config/admission_limits.rs::tests::defaults_validate_and_zero_disables_a_bucket
  - src/config/admission_limits.rs::tests::non_finite_or_negative_limits_are_refused_by_name
  - src/config/admission_limits.rs::tests::an_enabled_bucket_must_hold_one_token
  - src/config/validation_tests.rs::validate_boundary_tests::validation_rejects_a_limit_posture_that_can_never_admit
- **Risk reason:** A public status and code change (429 limit_records_per_sec / rate_limited -> 413 payload_too_large / body_too_large), and retry semantics flip from retryable to permanent. This hits an error case clients may branch on, and on raw it is reachable at the default posture. Not graded high because no such request could ever succeed. The boot refusal is fail-closed but can block an upgrade for an operator with an exotic limit env. Test gaps: no wire test pins the bytes-over-bucket 413 on a non-close append at its new early position, or the 'before TTL renewal' ordering; the /v1/debug/load exclusion is deliberately left unasserted.
- **Check against commit:** Matches, with imprecisions. (1) The old Retry-After was computed (ceil(deficit/rate), min 1) and was 1 s only when the bucket was full, so 'retry-after: 1' is not exact. (2) The boot refusal covers sub-token bytes and records buckets too, not only the request bucket. (3) The raw bytes-413 message text changed ('ingest capacity' -> 'ingest bytes capacity'). (4) Unrecorded side effect: oversized appends used to renew the stream's sliding TTL before being refused, and no longer do.

### #34 85017d84+729c52ac — Split or merge the segment map refuses keeps its intent pending

- **Program item:** item 64
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Appends to the parents' key range: POST /v1/stream/{name} (raw) and the product append under /v1/streams/{name}, both via AppendService::execute_prepared → closure_is_current. Reads of that range (read_request.rs seal_gap) also change. Operator: GET /v1/debug/load scaler.segment_splits / segment_merges / segment_map_refreshes, and ops events split_committed / merge_committed (/v1/debug/ops-events and the ops sink).
- **Condition:** A latent state: phase B of a split or merge reaches map.split / map.merge with the intent still pending and the parent engine(s) already physically closed, and the segment map refuses. Cases: IdExhausted, with the allocator at u32::MAX-1; the high child's id overflowing at u32::MAX (now a checked add); a merge whose parent is already sealed (AlreadySealed); or, via 729c52ac, a merge at u32::MAX, which previously panicked in debug and wrapped to 0 in release. The commit says no reachable production path produces this state.
- **Before:** The Err(\_) arm was treated as an idempotent completion: it set map.pending = None, bumped map.version, wrote the map and returned published = true. The parent stayed live in the map over a closed engine. A writer's append met the engine's closure, and closure_is_current found no pending transition and the segment live, so it returned the closure as final. Raw answered 409, code stream_closed, 'stream is closed', with stream-closed: true and stream-next-offset headers and no Retry-After. Product answered 409, code 'sealed', 'collection is sealed', retryable:false. Readers saw closed && !seal_gap, i.e. Stream-Closed / sealed reported as final. SEGMENT_SPLITS or SEGMENT_MERGES and SEGMENT_MAP_REFRESHES were incremented, the scaler retired the parents' sketches, and split_committed / merge_committed ops events were emitted.
- **After:** Every MapError now returns Mutation::Decline(false) and nothing is written. The intent stays pending, the map version is unchanged, resume\* returns false, and one tracing::error! line is logged with the typed MapError ('split stays pending…' / 'merge stays pending…'). SegmentMap::merge allocates the child id with checked_add before sealing either parent, so a refused merge leaves the map untouched. A writer to the range sees the closed engine, then closure_is_current sees the pending intent, schedules a topology ticket and waits (bounded by JOB_TIMEOUT 10 s). It retries up to 4 attempts with 10/20/30 ms waits and ends with 503 and Retry-After: 1. On raw the code is segment_transition, 'segment map transition did not converge; retry' (or the WorkError text). On product it is 503 code temporarily_unavailable, 'retry shortly', retryable:true, Retry-After: 1. Readers see seal_gap = true, so closed and Stream-Closed are not reported and upToDate is false. No counters are incremented and no \*\_committed events are emitted.
- **Retry semantics:** A permanent 409 (stream_closed / product 'sealed', retryable:false, Stream-Closed: true) becomes a retryable 503 (segment_transition / product temporarily_unavailable, retryable:true, Retry-After: 1). Because the refusal (e.g. allocator exhausted) does not clear itself, writers in this state retry indefinitely against a range that stays unavailable until an operator steps in. Before, they were told, wrongly, that the collection was finally closed.
- **Who is affected:** Only in the latent refused state: SDK and raw writers and readers of the parents' key range; operators reading split/merge counters and \*\_committed ops events, which no longer over-count; and the scaler, whose parent sketches are no longer retired early.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - a_split_the_allocator_cannot_number_keeps_its_intent_pending (src/dst/tests/topology_scaling.rs)
  - a_split_with_no_id_for_its_high_child_keeps_its_intent_pending (src/dst/tests/topology_scaling.rs)
  - a_merge_the_segment_map_refuses_keeps_its_intent_pending (src/dst/tests/topology_scaling.rs)
  - an_exhausted_allocator_refuses_before_any_parent_seals (src/segmap.rs tests)
  - r02_a_closure_the_refresh_cannot_confirm_is_retryable_not_final (src/dst/tests/append_application.rs; pre-existing pin of the writer-side retryable segment_transition contract, not specific to the refused state)
- **Risk reason:** The status, error code and retry semantics change for an error case clients may branch on: a permanent 409 stream_closed/sealed becomes a retryable 503 with Retry-After. The state is latent (no reachable path per the commit), and the old answer was false finality. But the new state never clears itself, so clients retry indefinitely. Neither form is pinned at the HTTP level for this state.
- **Check against commit:** The recorded text matches both commits but is imprecise in two ways. (1) On the product surface the retryable answer is 503 code 'temporarily_unavailable' (retryable:true, Retry-After: 1), not 'segment_transition'; only raw exposes segment_transition. (2) It omits the reader-side twin: with the intent kept pending, reads compute seal_gap = true and no longer report Stream-Closed/sealed for the parents' range. The new DST tests pin map state (resumed=false, intent kept, version unchanged), not the writer's 503.

### #46 1791207a — Live SSE lease ends when its project is placed on another cell

- **Program item:** item 65
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** GET /v1/streams/{name}/records:sse (product_read with live=sse; enforce mode only, where a customer principal attaches an AuthLease). This is the only producer of customer leases (product.rs:2821). Also the lease_terminations.project_missing counter.
- **Condition:** A project's policy is republished, with new policy and feed versions that publication accepts, placing it on another cell, while this cell holds a live SSE subscription or is establishing one for that project.
- **Before:** Request verification already refused a foreign placement with 421 wrong_cell (prisma-error-code: wrong_cell, retryable:false). lease_check only asked whether the project was present in the snapshot, so a live subscription's lease kept re-proving Ok and the stream kept delivering records on a cell that no longer serves the project. It also established with 200 text/event-stream if the republish landed between verification and LeaseWatch::new_checked.
- **After:** lease_check asks served_policy (present AND cell_id == this cell) and returns LeaseInvalidReason::ProjectMissing. For a live subscription, the next generation wakeup or per-frame gate ends the SSE body cleanly: EOF, no error frame, queued frames discarded unbilled. lease_terminations.project_missing is counted once. The client's reconnect then gets the unchanged 421 wrong_cell. At establishment, reachable only in the verification-to-establishment race window, the response is 403 with body {"error":{"code":"project_missing","message":"authorization was invalidated before the subscription could be established"}}. There is no retryable field and no Retry-After (err_resp shape). Request verification's answer is unchanged (421 wrong_cell). No new code or metric key.
- **Retry semantics:** A live subscription that previously continued now terminates. Reconnect semantics are unchanged: 421 wrong_cell, meaning re-resolve the project's endpoint. In the establishment race, a request that previously succeeded now gets 403 project_missing, a Denied class that looks permanent, where the request path says 421. That asymmetry is documented in auth/refusal.rs.
- **Who is affected:** Enforce-mode product SDK users holding SSE subscriptions during a project re-placement (cell transfer). Operators see the project_missing termination counter rise during transfers. Watch-capability status (status_and_quotas) keeps the same gap; that was held as a decision.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - auth::tests::a_lease_ends_when_its_project_is_placed_on_another_cell (new, red-first: left Ok(()), right Err(ProjectMissing))
  - sse::auth::tests::lease_refusals_answer_their_class_status (pre-existing; pins ProjectMissing -> 403 with code project_missing)
  - sse::auth::tests::lease_terminations_have_one_slot_per_reason (pre-existing)
- **Risk reason:** This changes the lifetime of successful live product subscriptions: they now end on re-placement. It also introduces a 403 project_missing at establishment (race-only) for a condition the request path answers 421, which clients may branch on as a permanent denial instead of re-resolving. The previous behaviour was incorrect (serving from a cell that no longer owns the project), and reconnect semantics are unchanged. No end-to-end SSE test pins the live termination on re-placement; only the unit lease_check test does.
- **Check against commit:** None in behaviour. Precision: '403 at establishment' is reachable only when the republish lands between request verification (already 421 wrong_cell for a foreign placement) and LeaseWatch::new_checked. The common visible effect is a clean EOF on a live stream. Cosmetic defect: served_policy was inserted between status_and_quotas's doc comment and its fn, so at HEAD (src/auth.rs \~726-741) status_and_quotas's doc comment is attached to served_policy and status_and_quotas has no doc.

### #55 (839135a4) — A raw close whose seal intent failed transiently answers retryable, not sealed

- **Program item:** release hold, follow-up to skeptic finding F3 (the same defect class on the raw close path; plan decision D6). Recorded by its implementer for owner ratification.
- **Surface:** raw
- **Endpoint:** Raw POST /v1/stream/{name} with Stream-Closed: true, close-only or append-and-close, when the close publishes a fresh seal intent (application::lifecycle::begin_sealing_for_close, called from close::install_intent). The product :seal never takes this step, because its final record carries its claim.
- **Condition:** The intent step fails with a transient error. SealError::Storage: the descriptor store could not be read or written by the intent CAS, or by the takeover of a lapsed final-bearing claim (its reservation, its fence's descriptor read, its installation, or the completion of the old operation). SealError::Resumable: a split or merge kept the collection busy through all six claim rounds, the takeover's fence could not be placed or was refused, or completing the old operation's seal is resumable.
- **Before:** 409 {"error":{"code":"sealed","message":"\<error>"}}, with no Stream-Closed or Retry-After header. A raw client reads 409 sealed as a permanent state of the collection, although nothing was sealed: the intent is absent, or, after an ambiguous write, installed and resumable by the same close.
- **After:** 503 {"error":{"code":"seal_incomplete","message":"\<error>"}}, with no Retry-After. This is the answer the same close already gives when its completion fails (close::complete). The message text is unchanged. These keep 409 sealed: another seal's live claim ("a seal with a final record is in flight; retry that request to finish it", "a different seal operation is in flight"), the settled lifecycle refusals (changed incarnation, invalid claim, other operation, owed final, already sealed, missing), and the superseded owed-final resume.
- **Retry semantics:** A permanent 409 becomes a retryable 503. The retry re-enters the same operation: a plain close joins its own Empty intent, and a takeover restarts from the lapsed claim, which the failed attempt left in place. The collection then closes.
- **Who is affected:** Raw clients closing a stream while the descriptor store fails once, while a split or merge keeps the collection busy, or while their close takes over a lapsed final-bearing seal. Clients that treated 409 sealed on a close as "already closed" now see a 503 when the close did not happen.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/append_application.rs::r02_a_close_whose_intent_cannot_read_the_registry_is_retryable_not_sealed (red at cf5d6e05 with (Conflict, Sealed); the same test pins that a live final-bearing claim still answers (Conflict, Sealed), and that the retry closes and seals the collection)
  - NOT pinned: the Resumable variants. No test drives six busy claim rounds or a refused fence through a raw close.
- **Risk reason:** Medium: a status and code on an error case that raw clients may branch on changes (409 sealed becomes 503 seal_incomplete), as record #8 did for the append's refresh read (409 becomes 503). The earlier 409 was wrong, because the collection was not sealed. No code is new: seal_incomplete is already a raw append answer.
- **Check against commit:** Not checked by an independent pass. **Ratified by the owner on 2026-09-25**, with one follow-up done in the same batch as #56/#57: a close resuming an owed final whose claim renewal cannot be written (only a registry read or write failure) answered 503 `internal`; it now answers 503 `seal_incomplete` like the rest of the close step (src/application/append/close.rs::install_intent; no dedicated test: the renewal's store failure needs an owed final whose operation matches the close's own).

### #63 (this record's commit) — A fleet instance drains its ownership before a termination signal stops it

- **Program item:** item 40, step 4 (planned drain)
- **Surface:** process
- **Endpoint:** SIGTERM/SIGINT handling of a fleet instance (`FLEET_PREFIX` set): `ShutdownRequest::request` (src/tasks.rs), `tasks::drain`, `fleet::drain`; `GET /health` / `GET /readyz` during the drain; the deploy wrapper's forward grace (deploy/app-*/supervise.ts). `fleet/<instance>.json` gains `seq`, `viewed` and a live `draining` flag.
- **Condition:** a termination signal to a runtime whose fleet loop runs, whose ring would keep another member within the desired count, and whose every live, progressing peer publishes `seq` (this version). Otherwise (no fleet, a fleet of one, no other member within the count, a peer of an earlier version) the drain ends at once without announcing anything; a stop a critical exit requested skips it.
- **Before:** The signal cancelled every loop at once: the listener closed (connections refused), the heartbeat stopped, and peers took the instance's shards over after its last beat (step 2: its last, withdrawing beat). The stop was bounded at 30 s; the wrapper killed the binary 35 s after forwarding the signal.
- **After:** The runtime first drains, with every loop still running: it publishes `draining` at once, keeps beating and serving, and its ring and every peer's drop it, so its tick yields its shards and their new owners open them (fencing it). The drain ends when it holds nothing (no shard, no open still running, no unsettled close) and every live, progressing peer has published a view that read its drain and left it out; or with `failed` if a close it owes failed; or at its 144 s budget, derived from what it waits for. Then it stops as before. While it drains, `/health` answers 503 `runtime draining` (`TaskMonitor::readiness_reason`), or `critical task terminated: <name>` if another critical loop fails meanwhile; requests for shards already moved answer 409 `not_ring_owner` + `Streams-Replay-To` the new owner; requests for shards it still holds are served. The log names the outcome: `planned drain complete`, `no peer can take ownership through a drain`, `planned drain failed`, or `planned drain timed out` with what was pending. The whole stop is bounded at 175 s, armed when the drain begins (also for a drain that ends at once); the wrapper kills at 180 s. During the drain a critical loop's `Done` does not stop the process; a failure stops it at once. A second signal during the drain is not observed.
- **Retry semantics:** Clients of a stopping instance see 409 + `Streams-Replay-To` (a live owner) or a served request, where they saw connection refused and then 409s pointing at the stopped instance until peers dropped it. `/health` is 503 for the drain's duration.
- **Who is affected:** Platform and router: the listener stays open up to 145 s longer after a stop signal and reports 503. Operators: new log lines; a fleet instance's stop takes longer (typically a few seconds; up to 175 s), and a second Ctrl-C does not cut it short. Whether Compute's own stop grace allows 175 s is unverified (D10); a platform kill ends the process at once with no ordered stop, as any kill does, and fencing takes over what was not yet handed off.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_planned_drain_hands_every_shard_to_its_peer_before_the_stop
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_drain_a_live_peer_never_reads_times_out_and_names_it
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_drain_a_peer_never_acknowledges_times_out_and_names_it
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_peer_of_an_earlier_version_means_no_drain
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_fleet_of_one_has_no_peer_to_hand_off_to
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_sole_member_within_the_count_has_no_peer_to_hand_off_to
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_requested_stop_drains_the_fleet_runtime_first
  - src/tasks/drain.rs::tests (drain before cancel, budget, first registration, second request, readiness while draining and after a failure or the stop, a panicking drain, process root)
  - src/fleet/drain.rs::tests (budget derivation, waited peers, the drain's echo, holdings, the outcome's report)
  - src/fleet/repository.rs::viewed_tests::only_the_ticks_read_records_the_draining_beats_its_view_read
- **Risk reason:** medium: it changes what a stopping instance answers and how long its stop takes. Fencing still rules out two writers; acknowledged data is durable before the ack, and the peer serves it while the drain runs, and a write through the drained instance is refused (pinned). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-28. AMENDED after ratification, following a second adversarial review: the drain's bound became 144 s (derived; it was 79 s), the whole stop 175 s (was 110 s) and the wrapper's grace 180 s (was 115 s); the drain now also ends at once when no other member within the desired count exists, reports `failed` for a failed close, and the heartbeat's ring echo was dropped for a bounded `viewed`.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

### #65 (commits 1b65e15d, 61068487, 8182730e, db126ccd) — Replaced and dead storage stops billing in a fleet, at the instant it ended

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §2, the owner's release blocker for idle-expiry recreation billing; found by its closure-debt DSTs
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** No request changes. What changes is the storage the billing rows record and bill: `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage[/current]`, the project usage routes, and the invoices built from the rollup, for streams recreated or ended on a fleet of two or more instances, or recreated while their billing row was still dirty.
- **Condition:** (a) A fleet of two or more instances, where a replaced incarnation's closure debts (or its segments) live on shards different instances own. (b) A fleet of two or more, where the tombstone walk reaches a terminal descriptor on another instance's shard. (c) A recreation over an incarnation whose billing row was still dirty (not yet acked), when the drain reached the row before the closure-debt pass. (d) A walk page where a shard the walk cold-opened held nothing to close.
- **Before:** (a) Each instance's closure-debt pass stopped at the first segment it may not open, so debts owned by different instances deadlocked and a replaced gauge stayed open, billing storage every month for good. (b) The walk stopped and re-read its page at a foreign route (a regression from R29), so in any fleet of two or more no instance walked past its first page holding another instance's dead stream: those tombstones were never closed or purged. (c) The drain closed the row at its own clock, over-billing storage from the expiry to the drain. (d) The walk kept that shard in scheduler custody, so at the resident budget a later segment on the page was deferred on every sweep.
- **After:** (a) and (b) A segment or route on another instance's shard is skipped, and its owner closes it; a budget deferral or an open that does not complete still stops and resumes at the same place. (c) The drain closes a replaced row at its closure debt's persisted instant, falling back to its clock only when no debt names the row; a failed debt read keeps the row dirty. (d) The walk hands back every shard it opened, whatever the step found.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Customers and invoices on multi-instance deployments: storage for replaced and ended streams stops at the instant it ended instead of running on. Operators: tombstones on foreign shards are purged again.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/billing_closure_owners.rs::closure_debts_owned_by_different_instances_all_settle (a)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_closure_owners.rs::a_crash_left_debt_is_walked_closed_behind_another_instances_dead_stream (b)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_closure_debts.rs::a_dirty_row_of_a_replaced_incarnation_still_closes_at_its_expiry (c)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_walk_custody.rs::a_shard_the_walk_opened_for_nothing_to_close_is_handed_back_before_the_next_segment (d)
- **Risk reason:** medium: it changes billed amounts. Every change moves them to what the persisted records say was owed; no amount grows. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-29.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. The owner decided the two policy questions on 2026-09-29: B3, a close that arrives after its month was finalized corrects that month (a correction against a frozen invoice is allowed); B5, an expired source whose fork still reads it stops billing at its expiry (pinned in the commit that ratified this record). B3's change is #66.

### #66 0b7840c7 — A storage close that reaches the rollup after its month was finalized corrects that month to the close instant, and no later month bills the closed segment

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §2, B3, found by the closure-debt DSTs. Owner decision of 2026-09-29: a correction against a frozen (finalized) invoice is allowed; the month must net to what the shard recorded up to the close, and later months must bill 0 for the segment.
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** No request changes. What changes: `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage?month=M[&streamId=]` for a replaced, ended or deleted incarnation (`status`, `storageByteSeconds`, `effective`, `correctionTotals`, `correctionList`, `ownedStoredBytesNow`); `GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage?month=M` (`effective`, `correctionTotals`); the rollup's create-only correction objects `telemetry/usage-monthly/{account}/{project}/{streamId}/{M}.corrections/{correctionId}.json`; the operator status field `pendingCorrectionArtifacts` until they publish. Rollup code: `src/rollup/page.rs` (`Page::apply_snapshot`, `apply_late_snapshot`, and the new `close_later_months`, `advance_segment_state`, `reverse_carried_month`, `uncarry`).
- **Condition:** A segment's storage close reaches the rollup after month M (the month of its close instant) was finalized. The close arrives as the drain's live snapshot with gauge 0, or as the month-finals of a later clock advance. This happens when the closer (the tombstone walk, the closure-debt pass, the drain reconciler, deletion) runs after M's boundary plus the 24 h grace, for example a walk circle longer than 24 h (above about 74k descriptors), or when the rollup lags the ledger by more than the grace. A later month is affected too when it was also closed before the close arrived. Separately, a close that lands on M's own row after M's carry committed and before its freeze.
- **Before:** M stayed billed to its boundary at the carried gauge, over-billed by gauge × (boundary − close instant): the late snapshot's lower figure saturated to a zero delta and the floor kept the maximum, so no correction and status `finalized`. The segment state kept the stale gauge (the finalized branch returned before updating it), so every later month's close billed gauge × the whole month, indefinitely, and the current-month fallback kept extrapolating it. A later month already closed kept its carry. A close inside M's own carry-to-freeze window froze the right stream row, but the name and project aggregates kept the carry's excess.
- **After:** A late snapshot whose figure is settled (a month-final, or gauge 0) sets M's floor to exactly the shard's figure, and the signed difference becomes one correction: id `corr/snap/snap/{streamId}/{segment}/{M}/{version}[/final]/{M}`, reason `late segment snapshot v{version} after finalization`, `storageByteMsDelta` the difference (negative for a close; positive when the gauge rose after the carry's knowledge). M's status becomes `corrected`; `effective.storageByteSeconds`, the top-level `storageByteSeconds` and the project's effective storage move to what was owed; `ownedStoredBytesNow` for M reads the settled snapshot's gauge: 0 for a close, the month-final's gauge otherwise (before, the carry's gauge stayed). `nameAggregate.storageByteSeconds` is the name aggregate's base and keeps the carried figure; the name aggregate's corrections are in the project answer's `correctionTotals` only (unchanged). A gauge-0 late snapshot also advances the segment state the carries read, so a month not yet closed carries 0; after the month was finalized, its accounted-through instant never moves back from what the carries accounted, so `stream_segment_states` shows the carried boundary, not the close instant, for such a segment (in the month's own carry-to-freeze window the open path still records the close instant, which every carry clamps with max(through, month start)). Every later month already finalized with the stale carry gains one correction reversing exactly that carry (id `corr/snap/{the close's event id}/{K}`, reason `late segment snapshot v{version} closed segment {segment} before {K}; its carried storage is reversed`), so it bills 0 for the segment; a later month whose carry committed but whose freeze had not run is lowered in place (floor, name and project aggregates) and freezes without the carry, with no correction. A close inside M's own carry-to-freeze window lowers the name and project aggregates by the carry's excess together with the stream row. Each correction publishes one create-only object; the monthly object is never rewritten. A late snapshot that still owns bytes keeps today's upward-only rule and leaves the segment state alone.
- **Retry semantics:** None at the HTTP level. In the rollup, correction ids are deterministic and the per-(segment, month) version fences drop a re-delivered, reordered or older snapshot before any write, so a replay corrects nothing more. The rollup page fails whole (and is retried, stalling the ledger until an operator acts) when a carried month cannot be read, when a carried month holds anything but the carry's exact figure from the superseded state, when an aggregate holds less than the carry being reversed, when the carried span exceeds the month close's 600-month cap, or when a figure does not fit a signed 128-bit correction: the same fail-closed policy as #52.
- **Who is affected:** Customers whose replaced, expired or deleted streams closed after their month was invoiced: they are credited the storage billed past the close instant, and later months stop billing it. Invoice consumers: receive the first automatically generated negative storage corrections (the data model always allowed them; `eff_u128` floors a total at 0); they must apply a negative `storageByteMsDelta` as a credit. Operators: `pendingCorrectionArtifacts` counts the new objects until publication.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/billing_closure_debts.rs::a_month_closed_before_settlement_still_bills_only_up_to_the_expiry_instant
  - src/dst/tests/billing_late_close.rs::a_close_that_reaches_the_rollup_after_february_closed_reverses_february
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_close_after_two_closed_months_corrects_the_first_and_reverses_the_carried_one
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_late_close_corrects_once_whatever_its_delivery
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_later_zero_gauge_version_first_settles_the_same_books
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_close_between_a_months_carry_and_its_freeze_lowers_that_month_in_place
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_close_inside_its_own_months_carry_window_lowers_the_aggregates_too
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_close_carried_only_by_month_finals_corrects_every_carried_month
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_carried_month_the_page_cannot_read_fails_the_late_close_whole
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_gauge_rise_and_a_close_settle_the_same_books_in_either_order
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_late_snapshot_that_does_not_close_keeps_the_upward_only_rule
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_carried_span_beyond_the_month_close_cap_fails_the_late_close
  - src/rollup/page/late_close_tests.rs::a_carried_month_whose_floor_the_carry_did_not_bill_fails_the_late_close
  - unchanged: src/rollup/tests.rs::rollup_applies_deltas_and_closes_months (a late gauge-40 snapshot still corrects storage by "0"), src/rollup/accounting_failure_tests.rs::a_late_byte_time_no_correction_can_carry_fails_its_page (a gauge-0 late figure beyond a correction still fails its page with nothing written)
- **Risk reason:** medium: it changes billed amounts on frozen months. Every change moves a month's storage to what the shard's persisted records say it owed: down for a close (the common case), up only when the gauge rose after the carry's knowledge and the close then settled the month. The rule rests on three shard facts, named on `settles` in `src/rollup/page.rs`: a segment's gauge only grows on append, only `billing_close` zeroes it, and a closed incarnation takes no appends or renewals; a change to gauge semantics must revisit it. Left unchanged, each a separate owner call: a late snapshot that still owns bytes does not advance the segment state (later months keep the older gauge); a late non-settled snapshot still marks its month final-seen, so its same-version month-final is dropped; a gauge-0 month-final that lands inside its own month's carry-to-freeze window is still dropped; `finalize_row`'s own extrapolation (`src/rollup/close.rs`) never reaches the aggregates, so a settled correction on a floor that extrapolation (not the carry) wrote leaves the project under the sum of its streams; storage is not reconciled. Awaits the owner's ratification. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-29.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

### #68 6fb8d8f0 — A server that sets no shed line refuses appends above 500 MiB of memory, the line production runs

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 2 of `config-simplification.md`. Owner decision of 2026-09-29: "I want the 1gig profile to be the default."
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Everything that passes `AppendService::check_memory`, which has three callers: `append_typed` (raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}`, and through `billing::system_append` the server's own appends to the usage, audit, ops-events, ops-metrics and fleet ops-events streams), `submit_product_append` (product `POST /v1/streams/{name}/records` and `records:batch`, and the final record of a product seal) and `AppendService::execute` (the consumer's dead-letter handoff). A raw create (`PUT /v1/stream/{name}`) does not pass the gate. Operator surfaces: `/operator/data.json` `rss_shed_mb`, `/v1/debug/load` `shed_line_mb`, `/v1/debug/absorb` `budget.shedLineMb` and `config.shedLineMb`, the startup line `memory budget: ...`, and `/operator/runbook`. Process: the default of `--admit-rss-shed-mb` / `ADMIT_RSS_SHED_MB`.
- **Condition:** The deployment neither sets `ADMIT_RSS_SHED_MB` nor passes `--admit-rss-shed-mb`, and the sampled resident memory (every 250 ms, in MiB) plus the absorber's reserved bytes is above 500 and at most 600.
- **Before:** Default line 600. In that band the append was admitted. The memory sampler asked the allocator to return memory (at most every 10 s) while sampled resident memory alone was above 600.
- **After:** Default line 500. In that band the append is refused after a 25 ms tarpit: raw 429 `overloaded`, message `instance memory pressure; retry`, `Retry-After: 2`; product 429 `rate_limited`, retryable, `Retry-After: 2`. Status, code, body and header are those the line already answered above 600. Nothing is written. A refused system append is retried by its own loop; a refused dead-letter handoff keeps the source lease. The allocator is asked to return memory above 500. With the 128 MiB shared cache of #67 the fixed budgets the boot check adds up are 336 MiB; 336 + 100 = 436, 64 below the line (with the 192 MiB cache the sum was 400 and the line would have sat on the check's boundary). A deployment that sets the line, or sets 0 (off), is unchanged.
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged in kind: a transient 429 with `Retry-After: 2`. It starts 100 MiB earlier. While a gather holds its reservation (12 MiB at the floor, 96 MiB at worst) the line is reached that much earlier in sampled resident memory.
- **Who is affected:** Servers started without the production profile: local runs, the harnesses that boot the real binary with defaults (platform e2e, conformance, the LiveFeed fleet certification), and a deployment that opted out of the profile without setting the line. Every deployed family sets 500 through the profile and is unchanged.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned (red-first: left `("admit-rss-shed-mb", "ADMIT_RSS_SHED_MB", "600")`)
  - src/config/tests.rs::shipped_shed_line_keeps_the_boot_checks_headroom (new, red-first: left 600, right 500)
  - src/config/tests.rs::the_rss_shed_line_is_described_on_its_own_flag (new, red-first: the flag had no help; its text sat on `--project-memory-pressure-bytes`)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_fixture_matches_scrubbed_parse
  - unchanged: src/admission.rs::rss_gate_counts_reserved_bytes (the gate itself)
- **Risk reason:** medium: a request that was admitted is refused, on a server that sets nothing. The refusal is the one clients already handle, production is unchanged, and the lower line is the certified one. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. Not reviewed independently. The review of 2026-09-29 of #79 found two sentences that this record's "After" shares with it too strong, and the text above is not changed: "Nothing is written" (no record is written, but a product seal whose final record the line refuses keeps its Sealing claim, and a product append has debited its project's append-volume quota before the line is checked), and "A refused system append is retried by its own loop" (a refused `_ops_metrics` snapshot is dropped). See #79, "After". For the owner to correct with the ratification.

### #70 f85990ab — A server that does not set SSE_MAX_CONNECTIONS admits 1,200 live subscriptions

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 2 of `config-simplification.md`. Owner decision of 2026-09-29: "I want the 1gig profile to be the default."
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** raw `GET /v1/stream/{name}?live=sse`; product `GET /v1/streams/{name}/records:sse`. Operator: `GET /v1/debug/load` (`sse_max_connections`, `sse_configured_max_connections`, `sse_effective_max_connections`) and the boot notices about the descriptor ceiling.
- **Condition:** An instance that does not set `SSE_MAX_CONNECTIONS` already holds 1,200 live SSE subscriptions and a further subscription arrives.
- **Before:** The cap was 10,000 (clamped to 3,072 under the release posture on a platform with 4,096 descriptors, with the notice `SseCapClamped`; outside the release posture the notice `SseCapExceedsDescriptors` at every boot). Subscriptions 1,201 to 10,000 (or to 3,072) were admitted.
- **After:** Subscription 1,201 is answered `503` with code `subscription_capacity`, message `instance at live-subscription capacity; retry another instance or later`, header `retry-after: 5`, before the stream opens. Subscriptions already open are not touched. `/v1/debug/load` reports 1200, and neither descriptor notice is logged on a platform with 4,096 descriptors. An explicit value, including 0 (unlimited, refused under the release posture), behaves as before.
- **Retry semantics:** The refusal is retryable after 5 s and is released as soon as any subscription of the instance ends; in a fleet another instance may admit it. No new status or code: the same refusal, reached earlier.
- **Who is affected:** Deployments without the profile that hold more than 1,200 concurrent SSE subscriptions on one instance. Production cells that source the profile already run 1,200. `docs/PERF-LIVEFEED.md` records that 10,000 admitted an all-solo geometry that fails near 5,000 on the 1 GiB class, so the old default admitted load the class cannot carry. A larger instance class must set the name.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned (red-first: left `("sse-max-connections", "SSE_MAX_CONNECTIONS", "10000")`)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_fixture_matches_scrubbed_parse
  - src/config/validation_tests.rs::an_unset_subscription_cap_is_1200 (new, red-first: left `Development(10000)`)
  - unchanged: src/admission.rs::subscription_budget_is_exact (the refusal at the cap), src/config/validation_tests.rs::release_capacity_never_turns_the_sse_gate_off
- **Risk reason:** medium: a subscription that was admitted is refused with a retryable 503, only where the name is unset. 1,200 is the 1 GiB Compute class value, not a measured capacity of every class. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

### #79 4e9cc917 — A server that sets neither admission cap sheds appends above 512 requests in flight and admits 256 concurrent appends to one stream

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 3 of `config-simplification.md` (the table "Also in this package", rows 2 and 3). Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this change; it was made on that delegation. Performance acceptance stays with the owner (AGENTS.md §1).
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Three gates of one controller (`src/admission.rs`), two settings.
  - The instance cap, `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT` (`--admit-max-inflight`), for writes: `AppendService::authorize` (`src/application/append.rs`), which every append passes after its descriptor and key were checked: raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}` (`append_typed`, and through `billing::system_append` the server's own appends to its four system streams, `_usage`, `_audit_events`, `_ops_events` and `_ops_metrics`), product `POST /v1/streams/{name}/records` and `records:batch` and the final record of a product seal, `POST /v1/streams/{name}:seal` with a final (`submit_product_append`), `AppendService::execute` (the consumer's dead-letter handoff), and the re-preparation of the topology retry. Fleet-internal: `POST /v1/internal/telemetry-append/{name}` (`src/http/telemetry_append.rs`), on which the owner of a system stream appends a peer's relayed batch through `billing::append_local` and so through the same gate.
  - The survival bound, four times the instance cap, in the middleware `track_inflight` (`src/http.rs`): every request whose path starts with `/v1/stream`, so both surfaces, reads, creates, deletes and subscriptions included, before authentication.
  - The per-stream cap, `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT_PER_STREAM` (`--admit-max-inflight-per-stream`): `execute_once` (`src/application/append.rs`), one slot per append on the stream segment the append was routed to, held until the append is answered.
  - Operator: `/operator/data.json` `admit_max_inflight` and `admit_max_inflight_per_stream` (the "instance cap" and "per-stream cap" of `/operator`), the counters `admit_shed`, `admit_shed_inflight`, `admit_shed_survival` and `stream_shed` of `GET /v1/debug/load`, and `/operator/runbook`.
- **Condition:** The process sets neither the variable nor the argument of the setting in question. The count in flight is the instance's: the middleware counts every request on every route it serves (health, operator, debug and fleet-internal routes included) from arrival until its response is returned, so a long-poll counts while it is parked, and a subscription until its response head is sent.
  - Instance cap: more than 512 requests are in flight when an append reaches the gate.
  - Survival bound: more than 2,048 are in flight when a request to a stream path arrives.
  - Per-stream cap: between 64 and 255 appends hold a slot of one stream segment when a further append to it arrives.
- **Before:** `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT` 0: no instance cap and no survival bound. An append was admitted whatever the count in flight; the memory shed line, the per-stream cap and the engine's own backpressure were the limits. `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT_PER_STREAM` 64: the 65th concurrent append to one stream segment answered raw 429 `stream_overloaded`, message `too many concurrent requests for this stream`, `retry-after: 1`, without a tarpit (product: 429 `rate_limited`, retryable, `retry-after: 1`).
- **After:**
  - `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT` 512. With more than 512 in flight an authenticated append waits 25 ms and answers raw 429 `overloaded`, message `instance at admission capacity; retry`, `retry-after: 1`; product 429 `rate_limited`, retryable, `retry-after: 1`. No record is written. An unauthenticated request still gets its 401 and no capacity answer. A refused dead-letter handoff keeps the source lease. Reads are not shed by this gate.
    - What a refusal leaves behind on the product surface. A seal with a final record publishes its Sealing claim before it appends the final record (`seal_final`, `src/application/lifecycle.rs`), and releases the claim only when the append is definitively rejected; a capacity refusal is not (`definitively_rejected`, `src/application/append/contract.rs`). Such a seal answers 429 `rate_limited`, retryable, and the collection stays Sealing: ordinary appends to it answer 409 `sealed`, message `collection is being sealed`, not retryable, until the same seal is retried to completion, or until another seal, plain or with a final, takes the claim over once its lease of 15 s (`SEAL_CLAIM_MS`) has lapsed. A product append of a principal that carries append quotas has debited the project's append-volume buckets before it reaches the gate (`state.quotas.admit_append`, `src/product.rs`); the debit is not refunded, so the retry can meet a quota refusal. Neither is new in kind: a refusal of the final record that is not a definitive rejection leaves the claim, the memory shed line's among them (it has the same class), and a refusal after the quota check leaves the debit. The instance cap is a new cause of both on a server that sets nothing, reached whenever more than 512 requests are in flight, parked long-polls included.
    - The server's own appends. A refused `_usage`, `_audit_events` or `_ops_events` batch is sent again by its loop. A refused `_ops_metrics` snapshot is not: the telemetry loop logs `ops metrics emit: ...` and takes a new snapshot one `METRICS_INTERVAL_SECS` (15) later, and the two gauges that `collect_snapshot` reads by resetting them, `rss_peak_since_scrape_mb` and `history_flush_wait_ms_max`, are lost for that interval. The telemetry pipeline runs only where `USAGE_STREAM_KEY` is set. Any failed append already dropped a snapshot; the cap is a new cause on a server that sets nothing. On the fleet-internal route a peer's relayed batch gets the raw 429 `overloaded` from an owner above the cap.
  - With more than 2,048 in flight every request to a stream path, reads included, answers `503` with the body `{"error":{"code":"overloaded","message":"retry"}}` and `retry-after: 1`, at once and before authentication.
  - `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT_PER_STREAM` 256. The 65th to 256th concurrent append to one stream segment is admitted; the 257th gets the answer the 65th got.
  - Status, codes, bodies and headers are those the gates already answered where they were configured; no new answer exists. A deployment that sets a name keeps its value; 0 turns the gate off, as before. The former behaviour is two settings: `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT=0 ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT_PER_STREAM=64`.
- **Retry semantics:** Both refusals of the instance cap are retryable after 1 s, and clients must honour `Retry-After` with jitter (RUNBOOK §3.6: a client that retries a 429 at once creates a reject storm). They are new to a client of a server that set nothing: a write that waited in the server's queues is now refused and must be sent again. A seal with a final record that the cap refused must be retried as the same request (the operation is identified by the final record, the routing key and the producer headers), which finishes it; until then, or until another seal takes the lapsed claim over after 15 s, ordinary appends to the collection answer 409 `sealed`, which is not retryable. The per-stream refusal is unchanged in kind and is reached later.
- **Not measured:** 512 and 256 are the values the deployments run. Neither is derived in a document. RUNBOOK §3.6 records a direct-path capacity of about 510 concurrent requests for one instance and names 256 as the guarded setting for router-fronted 1-CPU instances. For the per-stream cap the only measurements are 64 and no cap (`bench/aws-comparison-plan.md`: 1,944 records/s on one stream at 64, 6,396 uncapped). Memory with 256 concurrent appends of large bodies on one stream has not been measured; the instance cap and the memory shed line (500, the value of `ADMIT_RSS_SHED_MB` in `src/config/cli.rs` at this commit) bound it.
- **Who is affected:** A deployment that sets neither name. Eight of the nine server families that set `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT` set 512, and the same eight set the per-stream cap to 256 (`region-server`, `region-server-scale`, `fleet-server-1`, `fleet-server-n`, `mt-tenants-off`, `mt-tenants-enforce`, `wc-ladder`, `wc-ladder-diet`), as do the `bench/costab` scripts, `bench/fleet/local-fanout.sh`, `bench/docker/harness/cluster-deploy.sh` and `docs/STAGING.md`; they are unchanged. `scripts/bench-fra-ab.sh` sets the instance cap to 256 and did not set the per-stream cap: it and its family now set `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT_PER_STREAM=64`, the value it ran, because 256 under an instance cap of 256 would let one stream take every slot; RUNBOOK's Docker example (§4.2), which sets 256 too, gains the same line. Changed: a bare binary (the `defaults` family; the SDK smoke of CI) and the rigs that start the binary without the names: conformance, CI's product field gate, `scripts/platform-e2e.mjs` and its negative twin, the LiveFeed canary and certification (`platform-e2e` and `livefeed-canary` families), `scripts/mt-noisy-campaign.mjs`, `bench/docker/compose.yml` with its overlays `compose.d3.yml` and `compose.d4.yml` (the docker ladder of three 1 GB instances, whose image is built from the tree: no cap and 64 before, 512 and 256 now), and the six `bench/sse-probes` scripts (`hub-smoke.sh`, `ka-slope.sh`, `sse-slope.sh`, `sse-1per.sh`, `sse-matched.sh`, `sse-matched-loaded.sh`); two of the probes, `sse-matched.sh` and `sse-matched-loaded.sh`, report the counter `admit_shed_inflight`, which could not move while the cap was 0. Not changed: `bench/livefeed-perf/run-one.sh` sets neither name, but it runs the pinned arm binaries of 1834b726 and 3a8016e6 (`arms/`, git-ignored), not a build of the tree, and both commits compile the defaults 0 and 64. Whether one of the changed rigs holds more than 512 requests in flight is not determined: none was run for this record. For the noisy-neighbour campaign it can be read from the script: one sequential victim loop and `NOISY` sequential loops (48 by default, 24 in CI), so at most 49 requests of its own in flight. Outside the repository, the owner's A/B rig (`~/.streams-ab`) runs its point P1 with 1,024 clients, above the cap: it must set `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT=0` or its figures change. Whether a Compute project lacks the names is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11), which has not been run; on Compute the environment is merged per project (RUNBOOK §7.3), so a project that once set a name keeps its value.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned (both defaults; red-first on the unchanged code: left `("admit-max-inflight", "ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT", "0")` and `("admit-max-inflight-per-stream", "ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT_PER_STREAM", "64")`, right `"512"` and `"256"`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests.rs::a_server_that_sets_nothing_sheds_writes_above_512_in_flight (new, red-first: at 513 in flight left `Ok(())`, right `Err(Overloaded)`; it holds 512 admitted, 513 refused, 2,048 not refused and 2,049 refused only when the caller says that the path is a stream path, and the counters; it holds the controller's decision for a given flag, not the middleware's path test, for which see the DST below)
  - src/bootstrap/tests.rs::a_server_that_sets_nothing_admits_256_concurrent_appends_to_one_stream (new, red-first: left `(64, 236)`, right `(256, 44)`, slots granted and requests refused of 300; it holds that another stream is not affected and that a released slot is granted again)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_fixture_matches_scrubbed_parse (the fixture equals a parse in a cleared environment)
  - unchanged, the wire answers: src/dst/tests/security_routes.rs::inflight_admission_answers_only_after_authentication (401 before the cap answers, the tarpitted 429, the 503 of the survival bound) and ::the_survival_refusal_is_marked_as_this_servers_answer (its exact body and headers); both send only a raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}`, at caps of 4 and 1. src/admission.rs::survival_and_ordinary_gates_are_distinct_mechanisms and ::stream_slots_are_bounded_and_released (the gates at other caps)
  - src/dst/tests/security_routes.rs::the_survival_refusal_covers_reads_and_the_product_surface_at_the_default_caps (added after the review of 2026-09-29; on a rig with a bearer token, the cap set to the default of 512 and 2,048 requests held in flight, six requests without a bearer get the exact refusal: raw `POST` and `GET /v1/stream/any`, a raw long-poll, `GET /v1/streams`, product `GET` and `POST /v1/streams/any/records` each answer 503, body `{"error":{"code":"overloaded","message":"retry"}}`, `retry-after: 1`, `content-type: application/json`, the origin marker and `nosniff`; `GET /health` answers 200; the counters read 6 survival refusals of 6; with 2,047 held the same six requests answer 401. It pins behaviour that existed, so it has no red on the unchanged code. Controls, each a temporary edit of `track_inflight` that was reverted: with the prefix narrowed to `/v1/stream/` it fails at `GET /v1/streams` with 401 `unauthorized` on the left, and with the refusal limited to `POST` it fails at `GET /v1/stream/any`, while the two tests above pass)
  - Not pinned: that the two values reach the controller of a running server. The two new tests build the controller from the settings of a server that sets nothing, as `bootstrap::run` does in two field copies; `bootstrap::run` cannot be called by a test and its scope is frozen, so the copies are restated in the test. Also not pinned by any test: the wire answer of `stream_overloaded` (the code is named only in `src/application/append/contract.rs`).
- **Risk reason:** medium: a request that was served can now be refused, a read included above 2,048 in flight, on a server that sets nothing; and because the count covers parked long-polls, a server with more than 512 of them parked refuses every append until some return. A seal with a final record that is refused in that state leaves its collection Sealing, where ordinary appends get a 409 that is not retryable, until the seal is retried or taken over. The refusals are those clients of the deployments already handle, and the deployments are unchanged. The per-stream half only admits what was refused. Not run for this record: the rigs that start the binary. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent review of 2026-09-29 corrected this record: "Endpoint" gains the final record of a product seal and the fleet-internal relay route; "After" said "Nothing is written" and that every refused system append is retried by its loop, and now states the Sealing claim a refused seal leaves, the quota debit that is not refunded, and that a refused `_ops_metrics` snapshot is dropped; "Retry semantics" and "Risk reason" gain the seal; "Who is affected" gains the docker ladder and the six SSE probes and no longer lists `bench/livefeed-perf/run-one.sh` as changed (it runs pinned older binaries); the pinning list says what the two wire tests send, and gains a DST for the survival refusal of reads and of the product surface, which no test held. Corrected with it, outside this record: the help of `--admit-max-inflight`, which still said that the cap sheds `/v1/stream` requests with 429. Not corrected: record #68 has the same two sentences ("Nothing is written", "A refused system append is retried by its own loop") for the memory shed line, and the same holds there; its commit is outside the reviewed ones, so its text is left for the owner and only its check line says so. For the owner: whether a product seal should check the instance cap before it claims, or release its claim on a capacity refusal, is a client-visible change and was not made.

### #85 a1a851ac — Ingest is metered for every customer stream: BILLING_METER is not read

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 5 of `config-simplification.md` (switches with one live path), its fifth and last row. Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this package; the removal was made on that delegation. The audit of the setting (`evidence/config-audit-2026-09-29/detail.md`, `BILLING_METER`) put one question to the owner and named one thing it could not determine; both are listed under "For the owner" below.
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** On a server that did not hold the name nothing changes. On a server that held `BILLING_METER=off` no answer of an append changes while the billing row of the appended stream can be read and validates; where it cannot, the commit group is rejected and the append answers 500 `internal` (see "After"). What changes is what the committer counts for the appends `execute_once` submits (`src/application/append.rs`): raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}`, product `POST /v1/streams/{name}/records` and `records:batch`, the final record of `POST /v1/streams/{name}:seal` (the same product append), and `AppendService::execute` (the consumer's dead-letter handoff). A client reads the counts on `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage`, `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage/current` and `GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage` (`ingestPayloadBytes`, `ingestRecords`, `ownedStoredBytesNow`, `storageByteSeconds`, and `averageStoredBytes` and `gbMonth`, which are derived from the same byte-time), and they are what the usage ledger and the rollup bill. Process startup: the environment name `BILLING_METER` (it never had a command-line argument, so clap's surface and `--help` do not change), and the key `billing.meter_enabled` of the startup summary (a log line).
- **Condition:** A process holds `BILLING_METER=off`. Any other value, and an absent name, meant "on" before and mean nothing now.
- **Before:**
  - An append to a customer stream carried no billing reference (`AppendReq::billing` was `None`), so the committer (`CommitTransaction::bill_append`, `src/shard/transaction/append.rs`) did not touch the segment's billing row: the append's payload bytes and records were not added to the ingest totals, and its frame bytes were not added to the storage gauge, so the storage they occupy was not billed either.
  - The process started without a message, under `BILLING_MODE=required` too: `validate_billing_prerequisites` checks the usage key and the three identities, not the switch. A deployment with required billing could run with ingest unmetered.
  - Not switched off by it: the body of a create (`src/application/creation/initialization.rs` never read the switch), the count of append requests, the read meter, the usage ledger, the rollup and the ops streams (`docs/OBSERVABILITY-BILLING-STATUS.md`, "Claim correction").
  - The startup summary printed `billing.meter_enabled`, `true` in every deployment of the repository.
- **After:**
  - The environment name is not read. A process that holds it starts as a process that does not, whatever the value; nothing is logged.
  - Every append to a stream whose name does not start with `_` carries its billing reference and is counted in the write of its records: what every process without the name already ran. Appends to the `_`-reserved streams (the server's own usage, audit and ops streams) stay unmetered, as before.
  - For a process that held the switch, each commit group now reads the billing row of every customer stream it appends to: one read of the shard database per distinct stream per group (`CommitTransaction::billing_rows`, `src/shard/transaction/prepare.rs`). It writes the row and the usage-dirty marker in the write batch of the append (`src/shard/transaction/finalize.rs`). A row that cannot be read, or that does not decode or validate (`ShardEngine::load_billing_meta`), rejects every operation of the group with 500 `internal` and the log line `accounting group read failed`. Before, such a process reached that rejection only when a group also held an operation that reads a row: a create with initial content, which always carried its billing reference, or an accounting operation (`UsageAck`, `BillingClose`, `BillingRetained`); an append alone in its group read no row. This work of the committer is what the switch took out of the experiment arm it existed for (`docs/OBSERVABILITY-BILLING-STATUS.md`, "Claim correction"), and what every process without the name already ran.
  - Nothing is counted in arrears. Records appended while the switch was off stay uncounted: the ingest totals and the storage gauge of such a stream continue from the row it already has (a create with initial content was always metered, and so was every append made before the switch was set), or start from the first append after the deploy if it has none.
  - The startup summary has no key `billing.meter_enabled`.
- **Retry semantics:** None for a server that did not hold the name. For one that did, the 500 `internal` above is the existing answer of a failed accounting read; it carries no `Retry-After`.
- **Who is affected:** Nobody in the repository: no profile, family, rig, bench, test, workflow or deploy script sets the name (outside `src` it appeared only in the review documents, WIRE-MATRIX and `docs/OBSERVABILITY-BILLING-STATUS.md`). The effective-configuration evidence of 2026-09-24 shows `billing.meter_enabled` true in all 12 families on both sides. The switch was an arm of the OOM review's experiments. A Compute project keeps every name it was ever given: a project that still holds `BILLING_METER=off` from that experiment starts to be billed for the ingest and the storage of what it appends from the deploy onward. Whether such a project exists is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11), which has not been run. A reader of the startup summary that expects the key finds it absent; nothing in the repository reads it.
- **Deployment comparison:** `billing.meter_enabled` is declared as a removed leaf in `scripts/effective-config/rename-map.json`, with its proposal in `proposed.json`; the pin of HEAD's leaves goes from 139 to 138. The tool itself was not run (it builds rc.4 and HEAD); its unit tests were.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::retired_environment_names_change_nothing (the table gains `("BILLING_METER", "off")`; red-first: `assertion `left == right` failed: BILLING_METER`, the two configurations differing in `meter_enabled: false` on the left, `meter_enabled: true` on the right)
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::required_billing_cannot_be_loaded_with_ingest_unmetered (new; under `BILLING_MODE=required` the configuration loaded with `BILLING_METER=off` equals the one loaded without the name; red-first: the same difference, with `billing_mode: "required"` on both sides)
  - src/config/tests.rs::default_values_are_pinned and ::env_overlay_applies_with_legacy_parse_semantics (each loses its assertion on the removed field; the second also loses the name from its environment)
  - scripts/effective-config/test_effective_config.py::PinTest::test_new_leaf_pin_is_the_source_field_count (red-first after the field was removed and before the pin was lowered: `AssertionError: 138 != 139`)
  - unchanged, the metering itself: src/dst/tests/billing_usage.rs::billing_meta_is_exact_durable_and_ackable (the billing row after raw appends: exact ingest bytes, version and identity) and ::usage_pipeline_end_to_end_exactly_once (the usage answers after product appends: exact `ingestPayloadBytes` and `ingestRecords`)
  - Not pinned: an append to a server that was started with `BILLING_METER=off`. The rig's configuration is loaded from an empty environment (`fixture_config`, `src/dst/tests/fixture_http.rs`) and takes none, and its builder is a frozen exception scope. The condition that read the switch is deleted with the field, so no append can consult it. Also not pinned: the absence of the key from the startup summary, for the same reason.
- **For the owner:**
  1. The audit asked whether to remove the switch outright or to keep it as an investigation arm that is refused under `BILLING_MODE=required`. It is removed outright here. An experiment that needs appends without the billing row now needs a source change.
  2. The audit could not determine whether a Compute project still holds the name. This is the one change of package 5 that can alter a bill, so the platform export must be searched for `BILLING_METER` before the binary is deployed; that gate is the owner's.
  3. `src/application/append.rs` feeds TLA-003 (16.8 min), which was stale on the branch before this change through `src/shard/transaction/maintenance.rs`, so the re-record that is already due does not grow. `src/http.rs` selects the mutation leg of the owner `http` and Miri in CI; neither was run for this record.
- **Risk reason:** medium: no status, code, header or body of any request changes on a server that did not hold the name, nor on one that did while the billing rows of the appended streams read and validate, and the direction is the correct one (required billing can no longer run with ingest unmetered), but the usage a project reads and is billed for changes for a deployment that held the switch, and no such deployment can be enumerated from the repository. For such a deployment the operator-visible cost is one read of the shard database and one billing-row write per appended stream per commit group. Every deployment of the repository ran with metering on and is unchanged. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the library suite, the release profile, the rigs that start the binary, the effective-configuration comparison, the mutation leg, Miri, and the re-record of TLA-003. WIRE-MATRIX: the metering line of the raw append no longer names the switch. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent review of 2026-09-29 corrected this record for the population of its own condition, a process that held `BILLING_METER=off`: "Endpoint", "Retry semantics" and "Risk reason" said without a condition that no answer of an append changes, and such a process now reads and writes the billing row of every appended customer stream in the committer and answers 500 `internal` where that row cannot be read or is invalid; "After" gains that path and its cost, and says that the totals of a stream continue from the row it already has instead of starting at the first append after the deploy; the list of usage fields gains `averageStoredBytes` and `gbMonth`. A process that never held the name is unchanged, as the record said.

## Low risk (86)

None of these changes alters a status or code on a path that worked before, and only #92 and #95 add a header to one (`Cache-Control: no-store` on successes that WIRE-MATRIX already listed with it: one for #92, four for #95), except shared cells phase A's #101 and #104, which refuse requests that crossed a trust boundary (a reserved identity's tokens, a watch carrier with only a stream key), as their risk reasons say; #102's amendment adds `Retry-After: 1` to an existing retryable `503`; #114, #116 and #133 change answers on paths that worked (a pull may now wait for memory and be refused retryably or answered empty; a deleted stream's watchers, and since #133 an expired stream's and those returning after ten idle minutes, are answered `stale`), as their records say; and #132 lets a boot pass that was refused. Most are internal, operator-facing or timing-only; the rest correct data inside successful responses, or turn a failure (or a hang) into a success.

### #134 7529b198 — A shard's close does not wait for its history partition's final flush, so a partition at its L0 cap no longer holds the close, the drain or stop that waits on it, or the prefix's reopening

- **Program item:** The owner's landing call C2 of 2026-10-08 ("all recommended": investigate the shard-close hang and fix it red first if it is real). The hang was found by the landing chain's T9(a) lane: an HTTP rig whose shard held about 200 or more streams never settled `shards.shutdown(120 s)` ("0 opens, 1 engines; owners retained"). Reproduced and traced on 8bed7fda; cherry-picked from 7c896899. Awaits ratification.
- **Surface:** process (the stop and the planned drain). The raw and product surfaces observe it only as the duration of a retired prefix's `shard_closing` refusals.
- **Endpoint:**
  - The stop and the planned drain (`ShardDirectory::shutdown`, `fleet::drain`), through `ShardEngine::begin_close` and the engine's `storage-close` finalizer.
  - Any request routed to a prefix whose retired engine is still closing: `OpenGate::get_or_open` answers it `shard_closing` through `ResolveError::Opening`, which raw routes and the product routes that use `resolve_error_response` answer `503 shard_closing` with `Retry-After: 1`, a product append as `AppendCode::RateLimited("shard_closing")`, and the other product handlers as their retryable unavailable answer.
  - Code: `HistoryPartition::close` (`src/shard/history_partition.rs`), which now calls `close_opened`: `Db::close_with_options` with `with_flush_memtables(false)`, judged by the same `close_verdict`. The shard log's own close (`close_db`, from `shard::lifecycle`) keeps its final flush.
- **Condition:**
  - The shard's history partition holds a gather's write waiting for an L0 slot, and the partition's L0 is at its cap (64) as its Db last saw it. A Db learns of a compaction's freed slots only when it writes its own manifest, which nothing can do while no flush can run, or at its manifest poll, every 300 s for a history database.
  - Production reaches the cap whenever 64 gathers (at least 320 s, at one gather per 5 s tick per shard) land with no L0 compaction between them: a sorted-run merge that holds the one compaction slot (`compactor_max_concurrent` 1, the certified 1 GiB posture) for that long is enough, without any compaction failing; failing compactions reach it too. A new owner that opens a stored manifest already at the cap starts there.
- **Before:**
  - The close waited in its final memtable flush until the history Db's next manifest poll after compaction freed a slot (up to 300 s), and for good while compaction freed none (on the rig, 300 streams: 298.09 s, ending exactly 300 s after the partition opened, while nine compactions had finished unseen).
  - A planned drain timed out at 144 s ("planned drain timed out"), and a single-instance stop exited 1 at its 30 s bound.
  - The prefix answered `shard_closing` on that instance for as long.
- **After:**
  - The partition closes without its final flush: the close ends in milliseconds (11-14 ms on the rig) and the prefix reopens at its holdoff.
  - The waiting write is not stored. It was never relied on: a gather writes, flushes, and only then submits its boundary, so its debt stays in the shard log and the next owner absorbs it again with the same keys and values, as it would have even had the final flush landed.
  - With no final flush, `close_verdict`'s last arm (the failed final flush of a healthy Db) is unreachable for the history partition: a history store failure at close is no longer reported as a failed engine close. The shard log's close still reports one (item 38's verdict).
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged. `shard_closing` is retryable; it now ends when the close ends.
- **Who is affected:** Instances whose history compaction falls 64 gathers behind, which one long sorted-run merge can cause, and their clients during a drain, stop, move or eviction on that instance.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/shard/history_partition.rs::a_partition_at_its_l0_cap_closes_without_waiting_for_a_slot (production `history2_settings` with the cap at 2, two rows flushed and a third waiting; the close returns within 10 s, the Db is Clean, the flushed rows reopen and the unflushed one does not; red with the final flush: `left: Err(Elapsed(())) right: Ok(Ok(()))`; `close_opened -> Ok(())` fails it with `left: None right: Some(Clean)`)
  - src/dst/tests/runtime_retirement.rs::a_shard_whose_history_partition_is_at_its_l0_cap_closes_and_loses_nothing (the rig's default absorber, streams appended until a shard's history has 64 L0 in the Db's view and a memtable row a durable scan does not see; `rig.shutdown()` within 10 s, every close terminated, no open running, the partition Clean, and a second process reads exactly one record from every stream; red: `"shutdown ongoing or failed: 0 opens, 1 engines; owners retained; joined reports: []"`)
- **Risk reason:** low: no answer's bytes change, a retryable refusal ends sooner, and no stored data depended on the dropped flush.
