Residual, not changed here: the absorber still stalls at the cap until the history Db's next manifest poll after compaction frees a slot (NEXT-WORK 14.7, for the owner). WIRE-MATRIX: no row changes; `shard_closing`'s meaning is unchanged, only its duration in this state. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the `history_partition` mutation leg CI selects, Miri, Kani, TLC and any field run; no formal receipt is staled. Awaits ratification.
- **Check against commit:** written for 7529b198 from the analysis's draft, with a Fable 5.1 review's corrections: production reaches the cap whenever one sorted-run merge holds the only compaction slot for 64 gathers, not only under a failure backlog; a history store failure at close is no longer reported as a failed engine close; and the draft's "Given up" line is dropped, because the next owner re-absorbs the waiting write whether or not the final flush lands. Not checked by an independent pass.

### #133 3c34f64f, 3091944e — A watch journal idle for ten minutes, or whose stream expired and was closed by the tombstone walk, is retired: its flusher ends and a watcher returning with its cursor is answered stale once

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-08 (README, "Shared cells Q10" (a); NEXT-WORK 14.4: idle journal retirement, and the lifecycle walk's close retiring an expired stream's journal), the capacity review's C9 residuals that #116 left. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Surface:** both (the product watch wait and the raw touch wait observe it).
- **Endpoint:** `GET /v1/streams/{name}/watches/{watch}/keys/{key}` waits and the raw touch wait. The registry's idle sweep (`src/touch.rs`: `retire_idle`, a look every 60 s, started with the registry); `billing::walk::walk_segment` (`src/billing/walk.rs`) for a descriptor the tombstone walk found terminal.
- **Condition:** (a) A journal with no parked waiter, no pending touch, no request holding it (an admitted append whose touch awaits durability, a wait about to register) and no new wait or published touch for 10 minutes (retired at the next look after that); a look that finds it held starts the 10 minutes again, so a retirement never drops an admitted append's touch. (b) The tombstone walk steps an expired (or deleted) descriptor, on a cell with billing on.
- **Before:** A journal lived until a fence or move (never, on a one-shard cell) or a delete of its incarnation (#116), its 25 ms flusher ticking: tens of thousands of once-watched streams were 10^5-10^6 wake-ups a second on one core, attributed to none. An expired stream's journal lived until a delete of its name found it.
- **After:** The journal is closed and dropped, so its flusher ends; a waiter that raced in is answered `stale`. A watcher returning later with its cursor reaches a fresh journal with a new epoch and gets one `{"invalidated": true, "reason": "resync"}` (raw: stale), then resynchronises; a wait from `now` is unaffected.
- **Retry semantics:** A stale answer means "resynchronise", as after a fence.
- **Who is affected:** Watchers returning to a stream after ten quiet minutes (one extra resync), and watchers of an expired stream (stale at the walk's close instead of at their timeout).
- **Given up:** Cursor continuity across ten idle minutes.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/touch/journal_tests.rs::a_journal_idle_for_ten_minutes_is_retired_and_its_watchers_resynchronise (paused clock; red on the unfixed registry: the idle journal still held after 12 minutes), ::a_journal_an_admitted_append_holds_is_kept_until_its_touch_lands (a journal quiet for 9.5 minutes, then held by an admitted append's touch feed across two looks: the watcher arriving in between finds the same journal and is woken by the touch; red without the holder rule: the watcher's wait still `pending`, the touch dropped on the retired journal) and ::a_journal_waited_on_between_looks_stays_until_ten_minutes_after_the_last_wait
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_capacity.rs::expired_watched_streams_leave_no_touch_journal_after_the_walk (red: `32 more tasks alive after 32 watched streams expired and the walk closed them (36 before, 68 with them open, 68 after)`)
  - Controls: without the registration count, or with the threshold doubled, the unit tests fail; counting the registry's own two holders as a request's (`>=` for `>`) retires nothing, and the idle and waited-on tests fail.
- **Risk reason:** low: the answer a fence already gives, after ten idle minutes or at the stream's expiry. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written in this push's records commit and checked against 3c34f64f and 3091944e.

### #132 be15f313 — The release posture accepts a fleet-off server without a static token, so the single-server shared cell boots with STREAMS_RELEASE_POSTURE=1

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-08 (README, "Shared cells Q6" (b)). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Surface:** process (boot validation and its notices).
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `validate_fleet_auth` (`src/config/validation.rs`); `deploy/profiles/shared-cell.env`; `deploy/cell-admin/profile.ts`.
- **Condition:** `STREAMS_RELEASE_POSTURE=1` with `FLEET_AUTH_MODE=static` (the default), fleet mode off (no `FLEET_PREFIX`, or `FLEET_MAX` of 1) and no `FLEET_INTERNAL_TOKEN`.
- **Before:** Refused at boot ("FLEET_AUTH_MODE=static is the bridge posture and is refused under STREAMS_RELEASE_POSTURE=1 — configure workload identity (§14.1)"), and workload mode demands a `WORKLOAD_TOKEN_FILE` a fleet-off server never reads, so the shared-cell profile left the posture off.
- **After:** Accepted, without the static-bridge notice. Static mode under the posture is still refused in fleet mode or beside a `FLEET_INTERNAL_TOKEN`, and the refusal adds "; only a fleet-off server without FLEET_INTERNAL_TOKEN runs it". The shared-cell profile sets `STREAMS_RELEASE_POSTURE=1`, so a shared cell also boots only with the posture's record ceiling, feed-ring fit and bounded SSE cap, and `cell-admin` requires it of the profile.
- **Retry semantics:** Not applicable.
- **Who is affected:** Operators of fleet-off servers that set the posture; shared cells, which now carry it.
- **Given up:** Nothing: such a server holds no shared credential to bridge.
- **Deployment comparison:** The shared-cell profile gains `STREAMS_RELEASE_POSTURE=1`.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/validation_tests.rs, validate_boundary_tests::the_shared_cell_profile_validates_under_the_release_posture (`compute-1g.env`, `shared-cell.env` and the per-cell values layered as a deploy does pass validation; red on the unfixed validator with exactly the refusal above) and ::validation_rejects_static_fleet_auth_under_release_posture (fleet mode without a token, and a token on a fleet-off server, still refused); config_validation_tests::release_posture_refuses_every_static_credential_shape, unchanged
  - Control: without the fleet-mode clause the second test fails (the fleet-mode token check answers instead).
- **Risk reason:** low: boot accepts one more shape, which holds no permanent shared credential, and the shared-cell profile gains the posture's checks. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written in this push's records commit and checked against be15f313.

### #127 6d5d0fb8 — FRAME_COMPRESS is not read: stored pages compress whenever that pays

- **Program item:** layout 5 (spec §11: the setting is removed).
- **Surface:** process.
- **Endpoint:** process startup: the environment name `FRAME_COMPRESS` (it never had a command-line argument); the startup summary loses its `crypto` section (`crypto.frame_compress`).
- **Condition:** a process holds `FRAME_COMPRESS`, with any value.
- **Before:** `1` or `true` (any case) made writers zstd-1 each record before encrypting it (frame version 5) and made `format=frames` responses compress; any other value, or none, meant off. The code default was off; every deployment script, family and bench in the repository set `FRAME_COMPRESS=1`.
- **After:** the name is not read; a process that holds it starts as one that does not, whatever the value, and nothing is logged (the policy of edge records #80 to #85: retired environment names are ignored, retired arguments refused; this one had no argument). Storage compression is decided per page by the page codec. `CryptoConfig::frame_compress`, the constant `false` this left, is removed with `CryptoConfig` in the commit that follows, 25228d64 (landing call L4): `bootstrap::run` no longer passes it and `render_raw_read` asks `read_payload` for uncompressed frames itself. `format=frames` is #128.
- **Retry semantics:** none.
- **Who is affected:** operators. The bench scripts, effective-configuration families and `bench/k2cost/field/deploy.py` still pass `FRAME_COMPRESS=1`, now inert; they are not edited. The removed configuration leaf `crypto.frame_compress` is declared in `scripts/effective-config`'s `rename-map.json` by 25228d64.
- **Pinning tests:** `config::retired_tests::retired_environment_names_change_nothing` (`FRAME_COMPRESS` = `1`, `TrUe`, `0`; red: `assertion left == right failed: FRAME_COMPRESS`); `config::tests::env_overlay_applies_with_legacy_parse_semantics` (loses the name).
- **Risk reason:** low. Stored data compresses whenever that pays, which is what every deployment ran with the switch on; nothing a client sends or reads changes through this name.
- **Check against commit:** written at landing from the layout 5 drafts (#NN-c); not checked by an independent pass. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call L1), as drafted, before landing.

### #128 6d5d0fb8 — format=frames answers uncompressed version 4 frames on every deployment, so no response length depends on how well a record compresses

- **Program item:** layout 5 (spec §5: `format=frames` re-encrypts each record as a frame for the client); the crypto review of 2026-10-08 (F2: a compressed wire frame is a length side channel, the BREACH condition) and the landing instruction to keep today's default of uncompressed wire frames.
- **Surface:** raw.
- **Endpoint:** `GET /v1/stream/{name}?format=frames` (`render_raw_read` → `read_payload`), and its usage counter `bytes_out`.
- **Condition:** a `format=frames` read of a record of at least 256 bytes that zstd-1 shrinks, on a deployment that set `FRAME_COMPRESS=1` (every deployment in the repository).
- **Before:** such a record was a version 5 frame (compressed before encryption); on deployments without `FRAME_COMPRESS=1` (the code default) a version 4 frame.
- **After:** a version 4 frame on every deployment. Each record is still its own frame under the selector's subkey, in order; a client decoder that reads both versions (the keys CLI does) sees the same content, in more bytes for compressible records. The server spends no zstd pass per record on such a read.
- **Retry semantics:** none.
- **Who is affected:** clients of `format=frames`; none known (the SDK does not request frames).
- **Pinning tests:** `dst::dst_tests::reads_raw::format_frames_reencrypts_each_record_of_a_paged_stream` (a 12-record request absorbed as one history page and an 8-record tail page, read from offset 0 and from offset 5 inside the history page: one version 4 frame per record, in order, the 600 B records zstd would shrink included; red against the compressing wire: left `[(5, 0, "", ...`, right `[(4, 0, "", ...`).
- **Risk reason:** low. The content of every frame is unchanged and both versions were always valid frames; responses of compressible records grow.
- **Check against commit:** written at landing; the drafts' #NN-d proposed compressing on every deployment and named "never compress" as the alternative, which this record takes. Not checked by an independent pass. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call L1), as drafted, before landing.

### #124 a39dfba1 — The fleet tick reads its members by name with conditional GETs, and lists fleet/ and routers/ only on its first pass and every 30th after it

- **Program item:** The owner's request of 2026-10-06 for the K2 cost plan, lever E7 ("fleet reads without LIST"). Every fleet pass listed `fleet/` and `routers/`: 3,467 Class A LISTs an hour per server, 89% of an idle field server's LISTs. Tigris prices a LIST as ten GETs, does not bill a 304 and bills a 404 at the request's class (`docs/TIGRIS-404-COST.md` §3, #94).
- **Surface:** fleet-internal
- **Endpoint:** The fleet tick's two population reads, `FleetRepository::read_heartbeat_set` and `read_router_reports` (`src/fleet/repository.rs`, through the new private module `fleet::repository::members`), of `fleet/desired.json`, `fleet/<instance>.json` and `routers/*.json` in the object store. No HTTP endpoint changes shape. An operator sees the change in `GET /v1/debug/store`'s `totals` (#94: far fewer `list` operations; a revalidated GET that answers 304 counts `unbilled`) and in RUNBOOK §3.5, which `GET /operator/runbook` serves. The drain's poll and the operator view (`peek_heartbeat_set`; `GET /operator/data.json`) still list, and the tick's other reads (the desired state, the URL map, the overrides) are unchanged.
- **Condition:** Every fleet tick pass of a runtime with fleet coordination (`FLEET_PREFIX` set).
- **Before:** Every pass listed `fleet/` (one LIST per 1,000 objects) and `routers/`, then GET every heartbeat and every router report in full, each a billed request. A member's document was in the view of the first pass that started after it landed, whatever the member's name. A listed document that vanished before its GET failed the pass.
- **After:**
  - The heartbeat read GETs `fleet/desired.json`, then the heartbeats of `streams-1..=count` (the count from that same GET; `streams-1` alone when the document is absent or does not validate, which the tick's own desired read then refuses), of every member the last read found, and of this instance (`FleetRepository::join`, called as its heartbeat starts). It lists `fleet/` instead, and reads what the listing finds, on its first pass, on every 30th pass after the last listing (about once a minute at the 2 s period), and on a pass whose count names more than nine ordinals it does not hold. `FLEET_MAX` plays no part, and no count is kept as a high-water mark.
  - The router read lists `routers/` on its first pass and every 30th after it; the passes between GET the reports the last listing found.
  - Every GET of a document read before carries that copy's ETag (`If-None-Match`): an unchanged document answers 304 and its copy is parsed again; ages are still judged on the reader's clock. A 404 is an absent member. Any other error, a corrupt document, a member over 128 KiB or a population over 8 MiB fails the read as before, so the pass keeps its prior view, within the same 10 s deadline. A pass whose read by name would name more than 4,099 documents (the members it holds and the ordinals its count names) lists `fleet/` instead. Only a listing fails on the object bound: one of more than 4,099 objects fails closed, as every pass's listing did before; between listings a read by name sees no object beyond the documents it names. A desired document the read cannot GET (other than a 404) fails the pass at its heartbeat read, where the tick's later desired read failed it before.
  - Requests per pass, with N members whose documents exist, every ordinal within the count among them, A of them beating (their documents change every 2 s) and R router reports: before, 2 LISTs (6 at N = 4,096) and N + R billed GETs; after, N + R + 1 GETs of which A + R answer 200 (billed) and the rest 304 (desired.json included until it changes), and the two LISTs only on every 30th pass. With one server awake (an idle field cell) that is 1 + R billed GETs a pass for 1, 2 or 4 servers, against 2 LISTs and N + R billed GETs; with all four awake, 4 + R. A member that leaves keeps its document (its last beat withdraws it; the server never deletes a heartbeat), so from the pass after that beat its GET answers 304. A document that is deleted costs one billed 404 per pass while the count names it (at most nine a pass; from ten the pass lists) and one 404 once otherwise. The tick's LISTs fall from about 3,467 to about 116 an hour per server.
  - What the fleet sees differently: (a) a member no count names (above the count, or not an ordinal) whose first beat lands after a server's latest listing counts toward that server's `live` and load sums from its next listing (within 30 passes, about 60 s) instead of the next pass; no ring holds such a member. (b) That member's planned drain waits as long for that server's view to read its draining beat: within the 144 s drain budget while passes run at their period; past it the drain reports `TimedOut`, never a handoff. (c) An ordinal that first beats above the count after a server's latest listing, and that a rise published between one pass's heartbeat read and its desired read brings within the count, joins that server's ring one pass later than the listing had it. (d) A router report that first appears after a server's latest listing is read within 30 passes, not at the next pass. (e) A listed document that vanishes before its GET is an absent member instead of failing the pass. (f) A namespace that grows past 4,099 objects between listings fails no pass that reads by name: the passes before the next listing (at most 29 after the last one) GET the documents they name and keep those current. A listing then fails, and from the due listing on every pass lists again and fails until the namespace is back within the bound, as every pass did before (the R09 test below, for the owner, and the members tests). Ordinals within the count, and this instance, keep the next-pass bound: a member that boots or stops changes the ring at the first pass that starts after its document lands.
- **Retry semantics:** None on any client surface.
- **Who is affected:** Fleet peers, only in (a)-(f): a member no count names first beats only at a deploy or when it is started outside the count, and a router report first appears when a router with a new name starts, and (f) needs objects under `fleet/` beyond 4,096 members and the three coordination documents. The drain of such a member. Operators reading `/v1/debug/store` totals or the runbook. Mixed-version fleets: no document or format changes, and an earlier-version server keeps listing.
- **Given up:** Next-pass discovery of members no count names and of new router reports (within 30 passes now). Zero router LISTs would need the servers configured with the router names (not done).
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_reads.rs::the_tick_lists_its_members_only_to_discover_them (new: at `FLEET_MAX` 4096 with four members booted, five passes list `fleet` and `routers` once and ask for no heartbeat but `streams-1..4`, and `streams-2..4` and `routers/router-1.json` answer 200 once and 304 at every later pass. Red on the unfixed tree: left `["fleet", "routers", "fleet", "routers", "fleet", "routers", "fleet", "routers", "fleet", "routers"]`, right `["fleet", "routers"]`)
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_reads.rs::a_member_that_boots_or_stops_is_read_by_the_next_pass, src/dst/tests/fleet_reads.rs::a_count_beyond_fleet_max_names_its_members_in_the_pass_that_reads_it and src/dst/tests/fleet_drain.rs::a_drain_beyond_the_count_is_read_by_its_peer (new controls; each passes on the unfixed tree, the listing's bounds, and after the change)
  - src/fleet/repository/members/tests.rs (new, ten tests: each GET and listing with its ETag and answer for the counted ordinals and this instance, through `Members::join` and `FleetRepository::join`; revalidation, a boot and a deletion; a rise, a fall and an invalid count; nine absent ordinals probed and ten listed, and a fall back to the members that exist read by name; a member no count names found by the 30th read; the router cadence; the 128 KiB, 8 MiB and 4,099-object ceilings at their boundaries, a read that would name a 4,100th document listing instead, and the read after a namespace over the bound shrinks back succeeding; a namespace that passes the bound between listings read by name until the due listing, which fails, and every read after it listing and failing until it shrinks (`a_namespace_over_its_bound_fails_every_read_from_its_next_listing`; control: a failed listing counted as a listing lets the second read after it succeed by name); corruption; the deadline)
  - src/fleet/repository/document_tests.rs::r09_fleet_population_budget_includes_its_coordination_documents (edited and re-pinned in `docs/refactor/review-mechanisms.json`, an R09 mechanism test; the owner accepted the edit and the re-pin on 2026-10-08, "all recommended", item T6: the tick's first read and the listing peek read the 4,096 members beside the three owner documents and both fail closed past 4,099 objects, the tick's through a fresh repository whose first read lists; between listings the tick's read returns the 4,096 it holds and names and does not read `fleet/extra.json`)
  - unchanged and green: the `fleet_controller` (with `fleet_desired`), `fleet_drain` and `runtime_isolation` DSTs and the `fleet::` unit tests.
- **Risk reason:** low: the fleet control plane only, with no status, code or header change; a ring member within the count keeps the next-pass bound, the differences touch only members no count names and new router reports, and fencing, not the view, keeps two writers apart. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the mutation legs of `fleet_repository`, `fleet_members` and `fleet_heartbeat` (selected by CI), Miri, Kani and TLC; no formal receipt is staled (`src/fleet.rs` is unchanged). Made on the owner's request of 2026-10-06 (the K2 cost plan); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. A review of the landing chain (2026-10-08, finding F3) found (f) saying a namespace over the bound fails only the passes that list it; a failed listing never counts as one, so every pass from the due listing on fails, and (f) and the members tests say so. Numbered #96 on its branch (keep/e7-fleet-reads, on 1d93d3e0); renumbered #125 when it was rebased onto slate a2ae3d57, where #96 is the keyed DELETE (77beff8a), and #124 in the landing order of 2026-10-08 (rebased onto cb518e57).

### #122 c12ef0a3 — A graceful stop cuts a usage drain round that cannot finish after min(cadence, 5 s), not one cadence, so no TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS lets the supervisor's 10 s grace abort it

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-08 ("all recommended", item T9(b); README, "Owner decisions", row "The usage drain's bounds"), on finding 5d of the usage-drain step: the terminal round was bounded by one cadence, so `TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS` of 10 or more let the supervisor's 10 s grace abort it (recorded by #25's check, pre-existing), and #117's 8 s default left 2 s of margin.
- **Surface:** process: a graceful stop (SIGTERM, a planned drain's stop) of a server that meters usage.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. The `telemetry-drain` loop (`src/billing/telemetry_loop.rs`) runs one terminal round after its cancellation: it seals the active read window and drains once, under `tokio::time::timeout(bound, ..)` where `bound` is now `min(cadence, 5 s)` (`TERMINAL_ROUND_MAX`), one cadence before.
- **Condition:** A graceful stop whose terminal round cannot finish (a store that does not answer the spool write or the `_usage` append).
- **Before:** The round was cut after one cadence: 8 s at the default (#117), 2 s at the old default, and at `TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS` of 10 or more the supervisor's 10 s grace aborted the task first (`aborted: ["telemetry-drain"]`), so the stop needed a hard abort; nothing validated the setting.
- **After:** The round is cut after min(cadence, 5 s): 5 s at the default 8 s, the cadence when it is shorter, and 5 s whatever larger cadence a server sets, so the stop ends cooperatively inside the grace. A round that finishes in time is unchanged; what the spool accepted is durable, and a cut round leaves its batch under the same custody as an interrupted round (R09). No setting is refused at boot.
- **Retry semantics:** None (process lifecycle).
- **Who is affected:** A server stopping while its store does not answer: it reaches its process exit about 3 s sooner at the default cadence. A server that sets `TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS` of 10 or more no longer needs a hard abort at a stop with a wedged store. A healthy stop is unchanged.
- **Given up:** 3 s of the terminal round's patience at the default cadence: a store that answers between 5 s and 8 s into a stop leaves the window to the spool's custody instead of the ledger.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/billing_drain_bounds.rs::a_graceful_stop_cuts_a_wedged_terminal_round_at_five_seconds (new; red on the one-cadence bound: the stop took about 8 s)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_controller.rs::r09_active_telemetry_cancels_entered_storage_and_preserves_debt (review mechanism `active-telemetry-cancellation`: its stop is given the round's bound, 5 s, instead of one cadence; re-pinned, which the owner approved on 2026-10-08, landing call C6)
- **Risk reason:** low: process timing only, in the direction of a cooperative stop; no request's answer changes and the spool's custody of a cut round is unchanged. RUNBOOK §3.2b and OBSERVABILITY-BILLING §7.4 no longer ask operators to keep the cadence below the grace. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation leg CI runs for `billing_telemetry_loop`, the platform e2e and any field run; no formal receipt is staled. Awaits ratification.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change; not yet reviewed by an independent pass.

### #121 030eea63 — One usage drain round takes up to 256 of each shard's dirty rows, not 64, so a shard that dirties more than 64 segments within one cadence reaches the usage answers in a quarter of the rounds

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-08 ("all recommended", item T9(a); README, "Owner decisions", row "The usage drain's bounds"), on finding 5b of the usage-drain step: #117's 8 s cadence left the per-round page at 64 rows, so the drain's throughput fell from 32 to 8 segments a second per shard.
- **Surface:** product: how far `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage`, `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage/current` and `GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage` may trail the meters on a shard whose writes dirty many segments. Also operator-debug: the dirty rows behind the `usage_outbox_lag` alert drain up to four times faster per shard.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `drain_once` (`src/billing.rs`), one round of the `telemetry-drain` loop, reads each visited shard's dirty index through `ShardEngine::usage_dirty_page(cursor, 256)` (64 before). The round's other bounds are unchanged: at most 4 shards, 32 month-final rows per segment, and at most 1,000 envelopes or 1 MB per `_usage` append, admitted row by row.
- **Condition:** A shard holds more than 64 dirty usage rows (segments whose billing state has versions `_usage` has not acknowledged) when a round visits it.
- **Before:** A round took at most 64 of a shard's dirty rows and continued after the last one at the next round. A shard that dirtied N segments within one cadence had them all in `_usage` after ceil(N / 64) rounds: 1,000 active segments trailed by about 16 rounds, 128 s at the 8 s cadence (#117).
- **After:** A round takes at most 256 of a shard's dirty rows: the same 1,000 segments trail by about 4 rounds, 32 s. With four such shards the 1,000-envelope cap per append binds first (1,024 rows), as it always could. Nothing else changes: the rows stay dirty until acknowledged, the totals are the same, only earlier, and no status, header, body or error code changes. One round's `_usage` append can carry up to four times as many snapshots, inside the unchanged 1,000-envelope and 1 MB caps.
- **Measured:** locally only, by the new DST: 400 streams with one append each over the rig's two shards (288 and 112 dirty rows); the first round emits 368 snapshots (128 before: 64 + 64) and, once its acknowledgements apply, the second the last 32.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** A cell whose shard dirties more than 64 segments within one cadence: the launch shape's one-shard shared cell with many small projects (README, "Launch shape and routing"), and any fleet shard under that many active streams. Below 64 dirty segments per shard per cadence nothing changes.
- **Given up:** Nothing measurable: a round reads up to 192 more dirty-index rows and billing rows per shard and appends one larger `_usage` body.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/billing_drain_bounds.rs::one_drain_round_takes_up_to_256_dirty_rows_of_each_shard (new; red on the unchanged page: ``assertion `left == right` failed: one round takes up to 256 dirty rows of each shard [288, 112] left: Ok(128) right: Ok(368)``; controls: a page of 255 gives `Ok(367)`, one of 257 `Ok(369)`)
  - src/shard/bounded_outbox_tests.rs (unchanged: `usage_dirty_page` is bounded by its caller's limit)
- **Risk reason:** low: timing only, in the direction of fresher answers; no request that succeeded fails and none that failed succeeds, and the per-append caps that bound the ledger body are unchanged. OBSERVABILITY-BILLING §10.5, WIRE-MATRIX §2.19 and the R09 storage evidence say 256. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation leg CI runs for `billing`, the platform e2e and any field run; no formal receipt is staled. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change; not yet reviewed by an independent pass.

### #118 d6fe34d3 — The per-stream usage answer's possibleReadLossWindowSeconds states the read flush interval plus one drain cadence, 18 at the defaults, not the flush interval alone

- **Program item:** The owner's approval of 2026-10-08 ("all recommended", item T1: "fix the loss window with the growth row"), on finding 5a of the usage-drain step: the field already understated the window before #117 (12 s against 10) and #117's 8 s cadence widened the gap (18 s against 10).
- **Surface:** product: the body of `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage` and `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage/current`.
- **Endpoint:** `product_usage` (`src/product/usage.rs`) builds the answer's `metering` object. `metering.possibleReadLossWindowSeconds` now comes from `BillingConfig::read_loss_window_secs()` (`src/config/model.rs`): the read flush interval (`READ_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS / 1000`, 10) plus the drain cadence (`TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS`, floored at 1 s as the loop floors it), saturating. `metering.readFlushIntervalSeconds` is unchanged. The project answer (`GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage`) has no `metering` object and is unchanged.
- **Condition:** Every successful per-stream usage answer.
- **Before:** `possibleReadLossWindowSeconds` was the read flush interval, 10, whatever the cadence. A metered read's window seals at the first drain round after it is 10 s old and that round spools it, so a hard process loss could drop up to the interval plus one cadence: 12 s at the 2 s cadence, 18 s at #117's 8 s. OBSERVABILITY-BILLING §7.4 and RELEASE-PRODUCT-SURFACE already stated the interval plus one cadence.
- **After:** `possibleReadLossWindowSeconds` is the interval plus the server's cadence: 18 at the default 8 s, 12 on a server that sets `TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS=2`, 11 on one that sets 0 or 1. `readFlushIntervalSeconds` stays 10. No status, header, other field or error code changes; no billed total changes.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** A client that reads `metering.possibleReadLossWindowSeconds` (none in the repository: the SDK does not type the field, and no rig asserts it); a dashboard that shows it now shows 18 instead of 10.
- **Given up:** Nothing. The field now says what OBSERVABILITY-BILLING §7.4 says.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/billing_usage.rs::the_usage_answer_states_the_read_loss_window_of_the_drain_cadence (new; red on the unchanged answer: `assertion left == right failed left: Number(10) right: 18`; it also pins `readFlushIntervalSeconds` 10 and that `metering` has exactly these two members)
  - src/config/tests.rs::the_read_loss_window_is_the_flush_interval_plus_one_drain_cadence (new: cadences 0, 1, 2, 8 and `u64::MAX` give 11, 11, 12, 18 and `u64::MAX`, and the default configuration 18)
- **Exception growth:** the field sits inside `product_usage`'s `json_ok(&json!({...}))` call, whose `#[expect(clippy::unwrap_used)]` contract fingerprints every ordinary call site with its argument text, so the changed argument is one new call-site fingerprint (and one fewer of the old). Its row in `docs/quality/exception-growth.json` is owner-approved (2026-10-08, T1). No unwrap is added.
- **Risk reason:** low: one value inside a successful answer becomes correct; nothing fails or succeeds differently. WIRE-MATRIX §2.19 now names the two `metering` members and what each says; OBSERVABILITY-BILLING §7.4 and the §10.2 example say 18. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation leg CI runs for `product_usage`, the platform e2e and any field run; no formal receipt is staled. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change; not yet reviewed by an independent pass.

### #117 0a211002 — A server that meters usage and does not set TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS runs its usage drain every 8 s, not every 2 s, so the usage answers trail an acknowledged write by up to about 10 s and a read by up to about 20 s

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-07 (README, "Owner decisions", row "Write tier": "the WAL failsafe at 60 s and the usage drain at 8 s approved, each with an edge record"; NEXT-WORK §14.2, item 2; the drain half of lever E6 of the K2 cost plan). The 100 ms gap is #97 and the failsafe #98. Performance acceptance stays with the owner (AGENTS.md §1).
- **Surface:** product: how far `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage`, `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage/current` and `GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage` may trail the meters. Also process (a graceful stop's terminal round) and operator-debug (`/operator/billing.json` `drain.lastOkAgeSecs`, and when the `_ops_events`, `_audit_events` and fleet-event journals are written).
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `BillingConfig::default()` (`src/config/model.rs`) gives `telemetry_drain_secs`, the cadence of the `telemetry-drain` loop (`src/billing/telemetry_loop.rs`), which a server runs when `USAGE_STREAM_KEY` is set. Each round seals the read window if it is 10 s old, spools the sealed read batches and appends them, with the dirty segment snapshots and month finals, to `_usage` in one append, acknowledges those rows, then appends the queued ops, audit and fleet events to their journals and emits `_ops_metrics` once 15 s have passed. The usage answers read the rollup, which applies new `_usage` records within its 2 s idle poll. The same cadence bounds a graceful stop's terminal round (#25).
- **Condition:** The server meters usage (`USAGE_STREAM_KEY` set, as `BILLING_MODE=required` requires) and does not set `TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS`.
- **Before:** One round every 2 s.
  - A write's ingest, records, append request and storage reached `_usage` up to 2 s after its acknowledgement, and the usage answers up to about 4 s after it.
  - A read window sealed at the first round after it was 10 s old, so windows lasted 10-12 s, and a read reached the answers up to about 14 s after its window opened. A hard process loss could lose up to 12 s of metered reads.
  - A graceful stop whose terminal round could not finish (a store that does not answer) waited 2 s for it.
  - A round takes at most 64 dirty segments from each of at most 4 shards, 1,000 envelopes or 1 MB: up to 32 segments a second per shard.
  - The ops and audit journals were appended every 2 s; each queue holds 4,096 events between rounds.
- **After:** One round every 8 s.
  - A write's usage reaches `_usage` up to 8 s after its acknowledgement, and the usage answers up to about 10 s after it.
  - A read window seals 16 s after it opens in steady state (a window that opens just after a round seals two rounds later) and at most 18 s after; a read reaches the answers up to about 20 s after its window opened. A hard process loss can lose up to 18 s of metered reads. The read flush interval (10 s) is unchanged, and so is the per-stream answer's `metering` object: `readFlushIntervalSeconds` and `possibleReadLossWindowSeconds` both still say 10 (the window it names was already 10 s plus one cadence; OBSERVABILITY-BILLING §7.4 now says so; #118 makes the field state it).
  - A graceful stop whose terminal round cannot finish waits up to 8 s for it (5 s since #122), inside the supervisor's 10 s grace (the 30 s process stop bound is unchanged). A healthy round finishes as before.
  - The per-round bounds are unchanged, so the drain takes up to 8 segments a second per shard (32 since #121 raised the page to 256 rows). A cell that dirties more than 64 segments per shard within one cadence trails further than 10 s (a segment written continuously stays one dirty row; the `usage_outbox_lag` alert's threshold of 1,000 dirty rows is unchanged).
  - The ops and audit journals are appended every 8 s: a burst of more than 4,096 events within one cadence drops the excess, counted in a gap event as before. `drain.lastOkAgeSecs` runs from 0 to 8 in a healthy server, and `GET /v1/debug/usage-reconcile` can see the rollup up to about 10 s behind a shard's rows (about 4 s before).
  - `_ops_metrics` stays every 16 s (15 s rounded up to a round, as before). No status, header, body or error code changes, and the totals are the same, only later.
- **Measured:** locally only, by `dst_tests::billing_controller::an_append_after_a_drain_round_reaches_the_ledger_one_default_cadence_later`: the default configuration, an in-memory store, the dev profile; one raw append acknowledged just after the loop's first round reached `_usage` 8.009-8.020 s after the loop started, in six runs (2.02 s at the former default). Not measured here: the saving. The owner's tier analysis (outside the repository) prices it at 2-22% of a light shard's WAL objects (leaving 2 s costs S0 +22%, S2 +14%, DEV +13%, M1 +8%, L1 +7%, M2 and S1 +2-3%), from a local four-shard light run at the 10 ms gap that wrote 1.51 fewer WAL objects a second; nothing at heavy load.
- **Retry semantics:** None. A client that polls a usage answer until a write or read shows up waits up to about 10 s or 20 s instead of 4 s or 14 s.
- **Who is affected:** Every server that meters usage and leaves the cadence at its default: the fleet families (`BILLING_MODE=required`), the platform e2e and the LiveFeed canary families, and every rig that sets `USAGE_STREAM_KEY`; no deployment profile or family sets `TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS`. Callers of the usage answers (the platform e2e polls `/v1/projects/proj-b/usage` for about 24 s, its comment now names the 8 s cadence; `bench/fleet/ci-fanout.sh` polls for 90 s). `bench/fleet/local-fanout.sh` sets `TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS=1` and is unchanged; the k2cost harness README lists the new default. Operators reading `drain.lastOkAgeSecs` (RUNBOOK §3.2b and the troubleshooting table say 8).
- **Given up:** Freshness: about 6 s for writes and reads alike, and 6 s more of reads at risk on a hard process loss. Saved: up to three of every four `_usage` appends, UsageAck groups and journal appends on a lightly loaded cell (all of them where every 2 s round had something to write), each a WAL object on its shard (the figures under "Measured").
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::default_values_are_pinned (red on the unchanged default: left `2`, right `8`)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_controller.rs::an_append_after_a_drain_round_reaches_the_ledger_one_default_cadence_later (new; red: ``an append acknowledged after the first round reaches `_usage` at the next, one default cadence (8 s) after the loop started; it took 2.024748167s``; it also pins that the round after the first one emits the append exactly once, as one snapshot of 7 payload bytes and one record)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_controller.rs::r09_active_telemetry_cancels_entered_storage_and_preserves_debt (review mechanism `active-telemetry-cancellation`, re-pinned: its stop now runs on production's 10 s grace instead of 5 s, because at the 8 s cadence the 5 s grace aborted the parked terminal round: `active passes must stop cooperatively: ShutdownReport { outcomes: [("telemetry-outbox-sweep", Finished), ("telemetry-drain", Cancelled)], aborted: ["telemetry-drain"] }`; the mechanism's configuration says `shutdown_ms` 10000 and `terminal_round_cadence_secs` 8)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_controller.rs::graceful_stop_seals_and_drains_the_active_read_window (unchanged, green: the terminal round still drains the window at once)
  - Not pinned: the read window's 16-18 s, the per-round bounds' throughput, and anything on a rig that starts the binary.
- **Risk reason:** low: timing only. No request that succeeded fails, none that failed succeeds, the ledger's content and the billed totals are unchanged, and the month close waits 24 h after a boundary, so 6 s more lag never moves a row across a close. The terminal round still finishes inside the 10 s grace, with 2 s to spare (`TELEMETRY_DRAIN_SECS` of 10 or more still lets the supervisor abort it, as #25's check recorded; nothing validates it). WIRE-MATRIX §2.19 and §2.21 now state the freshness. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation leg CI runs for `billing_telemetry_loop` (its doc comment changed), the platform e2e and any field run; no formal receipt is staled. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change; not yet reviewed by an independent pass. Amended by #121's commit (T9(a)): the page of 256 rows. Amended by #122's commit (T9(b)): the terminal round's bound of 5 s.

### #116 0a3e8576 — Deleting a stream retires its incarnation's watch journal, so the journal's flusher task ends with the stream instead of at the next shard fence

- **Program item:** The capacity review of 2026-10-07 (C9); this predates phase A. The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4"); retiring idle journals (C9's other half) is the owner's decision of the same day and follows landing (README, "Shared cells Q10" (a)).
- **Surface:** both (the product and raw `DELETE` go through `http::delete_stream`); the watch waits observe the effect.
- **Endpoint:** `DELETE /v1/streams/{name}` and `DELETE /v1/stream/{name}`; `GET /v1/streams/{name}/watches/{watch}/keys/{key}` waits and the raw touch wait observe it. `TouchRegistry::retire` (`src/touch.rs`), called by `CreationService::delete` (`src/application/creation/deletion.rs`) for the incarnation it deletes and for a tombstone or expired incarnation it finds.
- **Condition:** The deleted (or already dead) incarnation had a touch journal: a watched stream that was appended to or watched.
- **Before:** The journal and its 25 ms flusher task lived until a shard fence or move, which never happens on a single-shard cell, so create/append/delete churn (which `max_streams` does not bound) left one task and one journal per incarnation for the life of the process; its waiters waited out their timeout.
- **After:** The journal is closed and dropped, so its flusher exits; its waiters are answered `stale` at once, with the cursor of its last generation, as after a fence. A poisoned map retires nothing.
- **Retry semantics:** A stale answer means "resynchronise": the client's next observation finds the stream gone (`404`).
- **Who is affected:** Watchers of a stream that is deleted while they wait.
- **Given up:** Nothing.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_capacity.rs::deleted_watched_streams_leave_no_touch_journal_running (C9; red with `retire` a no-op: `32 more tasks alive after 32 watched streams were created, appended to and deleted (35 before, 67 after)`)
  - src/touch/journal_tests.rs::retiring_an_identity_closes_and_forgets_exactly_its_journal (the retired journal's waiter is stale at its epoch, the other still waits, the next journal for the identity is fresh, an unknown identity changes nothing)
- **Risk reason:** low: a waiter of a deleted stream gets the answer a fence already gives it, sooner. Residuals: a stream that expires (TTL) is not deleted through this path, so its journal lives until a delete of its name finds it or a fence, and every live watched stream's flusher ticks at 40 Hz even when idle (`TouchJournal::start` is a frozen scope); both follow landing (NEXT-WORK §14.4). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 0a3e8576, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit; amended in 3091944e (below).
- **Amendment (3091944e, #133):** the TTL residual is closed: the tombstone walk's step for an expired (or deleted) descriptor retires its journal, so an expired stream's watchers are answered `stale` at the walk's close. Idle journals are retired after ten minutes since 3c34f64f (#133). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.

### #114 b023e3cb — A consumer pull reserves the coverage its walk reads at admission, as a page read reserves its page, and takes it again before each walk that follows a wait

- **Program item:** The capacity review of 2026-10-07 (C1). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4"); the dead-letter handoff's unreserved 4 MiB read stays, accepted for design partners (README, "Shared cells Q1", (ii)).
- **Surface:** product.
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:pull`. `product::read_memory` (the product surface's memory backstops) and `application::consumer::pull_wait` (the wait loop, moved unchanged into `PullPark::until_moved`); `PULL_COVERAGE_BYTES` is one constant in `application::consumer::delivery`. `pull` and `dlq_and_settle` (frozen) are untouched.
- **Condition:** At admission, a pull reserves 4 MiB, the records one lineage walk reads from the consumer's cursor before it leases, under its project's line and the instance's read memory; a pull with `waitMs` releases it while parked and takes it again before each walk after a wait. Refusal: the reservation does not fit within 2 s. Empty answer: a woken pull finds no room by its deadline.
- **Before:** A pull passed the write gate (a latch on the project's pressure at that moment) and reserved nothing. Concurrent pulls each read up to 4 MiB of coverage and rendered batches charged unreserved: one project at its in-flight ceiling filled the instance's read memory alone (64 × ~4 MiB against 125 MiB), and its neighbours were refused `503 read_memory_busy`.
- **After:** A pull that does not fit under its project's line waits up to 2 s, then is refused `429 project_memory_pressure` (`Retry-After: 1`); one that does not fit in the instance's read memory, `503 read_memory_busy` (`Retry-After: 1`). A pull with `waitMs` woken without room by its deadline answers `200` with `messages: []` and leases nothing. Its batch replaces the reservation when it renders and is held until its body ends, as before. A pull's body is also buffered under the line (#115).
- **Retry semantics:** A refused pull leased nothing (the refusal precedes the walk), so a retry is safe; an empty answer is an ordinary empty pull, and the message stays deliverable.
- **Who is affected:** Projects at their memory line (shared cells), and every project when the instance's read memory is full.
- **Given up:** Nothing a compliant consumer needs: its pulls wait for room, as reads do.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/read_memory.rs::a_projects_pulls_reserve_their_coverage_inside_its_line_and_its_neighbour_reads (C1; red: `the third pull, after 5.000924375s left: (3, (0, 0), None) right: (2, (8388608, 8388608), Some((429, Some("project_memory_pressure"), Some("1"))))`)
  - src/dst/tests/read_memory.rs::a_woken_pull_without_room_for_its_coverage_answers_empty_and_leases_nothing (red: `left: (..., Some(1)) right: (..., Some(0))`)
- **Risk reason:** low: these are the refusals reads already have, now on pulls, and an empty pull is always a valid answer. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands b023e3cb, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit; amended in 35a1e06d (below).
- **Amendment (35a1e06d, the owner's landing call C1 of 2026-10-08):** a pull buffers its body before it reserves its coverage. Before, its body was buffered under the line after its coverage was reserved, and the body check (#115) counts every byte the project holds, the pull's own 4 MiB reservation included: whenever the project's other pressure was above the line less 4 MiB and the body, the pull was refused `429 project_memory_pressure` at once, with no shed counted, where this record says it waits up to 2 s. Now its own reservation never weighs on its body: the write latch (#109) and the body check (#115) see only its project's other bytes, and past them the pull is judged by its coverage reservation, as "After" says: it waits up to 2 s and is refused, counted, only when its coverage does not fit beside its project's other read bytes (or in the instance's read memory), and is otherwise served or parks. A pull on a reserved `_` name is answered its `403 reserved_stream` before its reservation instead of possibly after a 2 s wait. Pinning tests: src/dst/tests/read_memory.rs::a_pulls_own_coverage_reservation_never_refuses_its_body (two parked waits, 96 KiB and no read bytes, under a line of one coverage and 64 KiB; red: `left: (429, None, false, true) right: (200, Some(1), false, true)`), and ::a_woken_pull_without_room_for_its_coverage_answers_empty_and_leases_nothing, whose line is now one coverage with no room for the body (red on the old order: `left: ((0, 0), (3146583, 3146583), 200, 429, None)`). Owner-approved as part of C1.

### #111 91c5b2af — Design-partner tokens are operator-minted from the cell's one key, for at most 24 h

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 9, the owner's decision P1(c) for design partners (README, "Shared cells Q9, operator-minted tokens"); the minting and key custody are among the cryptography the owner accepted on 2026-10-08 (README, "Shared cells Q8"). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** product: the `Authorization: Bearer` token every product request on a shared cell carries.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `deploy/cell-admin/tokens.ts` (`cell-admin token`) and the credentials `commands.ts` publishes in the grants feed.
- **Condition:** A design partner on a shared cell, with tokens minted by `cell-admin token`.
- **Before:** Field and campaign tooling minted tokens with fixed versions and deterministic credential ids (`bench/k2cost/field/feeds.mjs`, `bench/soak/mtgen.mjs`).
- **After:** One credential per project, with a random id `strcred_<32 hex>` and a `grant_version` that starts at 1 and only increases. Tokens: `aud prisma-streams-data`, `iss https://auth.prisma.io` (or the cell's `--issuer`), RS256 or EdDSA under the cell's one key (`kid streams-<alg>-<16 hex of its fingerprint>`; the key is pinned to `prisma-streams-data` only, so a `cell-admin` cell carries no key that can sign a workload token, #103), every scope the server defines unless the operator narrows them, optional stream prefixes, `exp − iat` at most 86,400 s with `iat` 60 s in the past (so at most 23 h 59 min of use), claims schema-checked before signing. The key is read only from `--key-file` (refused if group or others can read it) and never printed; the token reaches the partner as a mode-0600 file. There is no refresh endpoint.
- **Retry semantics:** An expired token gets `401 expired`; the SDK's `tokenProvider` refresh helps only if the partner wires it to a new token file.
- **Who is affected:** Design partners on shared cells, who need a new token at least daily.
- **Given up:** Self-service refresh, until the platform's Stage 1 issuer exists.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - deploy/cell-admin/cell-admin.test.ts, "token > …" (three: lifetime cap, claims, file mode) and "init > refuses a key file group or others can read…"
  - deploy/cell-admin/e2e.test.ts (opt-in; CI runs it against the release binaries since this push): the real cell verifies the minted tokens.
  - Controls: a lifetime cap of 10 × 24 h and an unchecked key mode each fail their named test.
- **Risk reason:** low: the format is the existing contract (`customer-token-claims.schema.json`, validated before signing); the operational burden of daily re-issue is the price of P1(c) until the platform issuer exists. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 91c5b2af, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit.

### #109 385bd8e7 — A write sees its project's held read bytes and parked waits in the project's memory pressure (pressure model version 2)

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 6, finding H3. Writes latching on held pages and parked waits was the owner's choice: the cost lands on the project itself (README, "Shared cells Q4"). `/v1/debug/load`'s members fold into this record. The owner ratified it as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** product; operator-debug for `GET /v1/debug/load` and the boot log.
- **Endpoint:** Every POST on the product surface the per-project memory gate covers: appends, `:batch`, consumer `:pull` and `:settle`, `:seal`. The model is `src/quota/pressure.rs` (moved there unchanged in 8ed1ddab); `pressure_model_json` (`src/quota.rs`) feeds `GET /v1/debug/load` and the boot log.
- **Condition:** `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES` > 0.
- **Before:** Model version 1: the estimate counted subscriptions, feeds, retained SSE bytes, buffered bodies, queued bytes, unabsorbed frame bytes and dirty streams; a project's reads and parked waits weighed nothing.
- **After:** Model version 2 adds two terms: a project's held read bytes exactly (#108), and 48 KiB for each wait parked in the project's share (a long-poll or a pull, #106; #269's measured ~44 KB per parked connection rounded up). A watch wait is already a live subscription and weighs as one. A project whose clients hold unread pages or park many waits can now be refused writes with `429 project_memory_pressure` (unchanged wire). Reads are not refused by this latch; #108's line applies to them. Operator-debug: `project_pressure_model` reads `version: 2` and adds `parked_weight_bytes: 49152`; the per-project rows (`project_memory.rows`) do not list read bytes or parked waits (`QuotaRegistry::memory_pressure_json` is a frozen scope), though their `estimated_pressure_bytes` includes both.
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged: retryable, `Retry-After: 1`.
- **Who is affected:** Only cells that set `PROJECT_MEMORY_PRESSURE_BYTES`: the shared-cell profile (#110).
- **Given up:** A project that holds unread pages may be refused its own writes until its clients read them.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/quota/pressure_tests.rs::parked_waits_weigh_48_kib_and_read_bytes_enter_exactly, src/dst/tests/read_memory.rs::parked_waits_weigh_in_their_projects_pressure (red with parked weight 0: `left: 0 right: 147456`), src/quota/read_reservation/tests.rs::reservations_fit_under_the_line_and_the_estimate_counts_them
  - Control: parked weight 0 fails both weight tests.
- **Risk reason:** low: inert until a cell sets the line; the weight is a code constant under a version bump, as the round-13 model requires. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 385bd8e7, from the draft the owner ratified with `/v1/debug/load`'s members folded in, and checked against that commit.

### #106 a3839329 — A request waiting for data is parked, not in flight: waiting long-polls, consumer pulls and watch waits no longer shed writes, trip the survival bound or ask the fleet for servers

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 5, finding M1 (cost review 2.3). Parked waits take only what open subscriptions leave, with no new refusal, as the owner chose (README, "Shared cells Q4"). The heartbeat and `/operator/data.json` surfaces fold into this record. The owner ratified it as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** both (instance admission on both stream surfaces); fleet-internal for the heartbeat; operator-debug for `GET /operator/data.json`.
- **Endpoint:** The post-auth write gate of every append (`POST /v1/streams/{name}/records`, `:batch`, raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}`) and the pre-auth survival bound on every `/v1/stream*` path (`track_inflight`, `src/http.rs`; `AdmissionController::park` and `ParkedTicket`, `src/admission.rs`; the request scope, `src/admission/park.rs`; the per-project share, `src/quota/parked.rs`). The park sites: the read long-poll's `wait_tail`, the pull's `PullPark::wait`, the watch wait. The heartbeat (`fleet/<instance>.json`, `src/fleet/heartbeat.rs`) and `GET /operator/data.json` (`src/operator.rs`).
- **Condition:** `ADMIT_MAX_INFLIGHT` > 0 and some requests in flight are waiting for data: a read long-poll (`records:long-poll`, raw `live=long-poll`) in its tail wait, a consumer pull with `waitMs` between walks, or a watch wait.
- **Before:** Every request in flight counted, waiting or not. An append was refused `429 rate_limited` (25 ms tarpit, `Retry-After`) once in-flight requests exceeded the cap, so 512 waiting long-polls or SDK consumer pulls shed every tenant's appends on the instance; above four times the cap every `/v1/stream*` request was refused `503 overloaded` before authentication, waits included. The heartbeat's `inflight` and `inflight_peak` counted waits, and the scaler wanted ⌈inflight ÷ 105⌉ servers.
- **After:** Both gates count only active requests, in flight minus parked.
  - A wait is parked while the live-connection pool (the effective SSE cap) has room after the open subscriptions and, for a verified project, while its open subscriptions, watch waits and parked waits stay within its live-subscription quota (on a cell shared k ways #100's ceiling makes that the pool ÷ k). A wait that finds no room is never refused: it waits as an active request and counts against both gates, as every wait did before. Subscriptions are never refused because of parked waits. A parked wait keeps its request slot in the project's `max_inflight_requests`.
  - Fleet-internal: the heartbeat's `inflight` and `inflight_peak` count active requests only, so waits no longer drive the scaler's edge-slot dimension (in the isolated run 400 parked pulls kept the desired count at 1, where counting them wanted 4).
  - Operator-debug: `GET /operator/data.json` adds `local.parked`, the requests in flight that are parked now. `GET /v1/debug/load`'s `inflight_now` and `inflight_peak` still count every request.
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged: the same 429 and 503, with the same `Retry-After`, for the requests that still meet them.
- **Who is affected:** Every cell: writers on a cell with waiting clients see fewer 429s and 503s, and nothing admitted before is refused now. A project whose waits exceed its live share keeps the former accounting for the excess. Not done: the router's parked-time correction of its latency EWMA (single-server cells have no router).
- **Given up:** Nothing.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::a_parked_crowd_never_sheds_the_victims_writes (A8, un-ignored. Red: `the victim among parked waits left: ((429, Some("rate_limited")), 200, true) right: ((200, None), 200, true)`)
  - src/dst/tests/parked_waits.rs::waiting_reads_pulls_and_watches_are_parked_and_leave_the_write_gate, ::a_projects_waits_park_only_within_its_share_and_the_pool, ::parked_pulls_never_reach_the_heartbeat (new)
  - src/admission.rs::tests::parked_requests_leave_the_write_gate_survival_and_the_active_count and ::parked_waits_take_the_live_pool_after_subscriptions; src/quota/parked.rs::tests::parked_waits_and_subscriptions_share_the_live_quota
  - Controls (waits never park): `((3, 0), 429)` against `((3, 3), 200)`; the heartbeat `(0, 400)` against `(400, 0)`; the share `(4, 0)` against `(4, 2)`; a heartbeat counting every request wanted 4 servers for 400 parked pulls.
- **Risk reason:** low: it only removes refusals. The waits that leave the gates are bounded by the pool (1,200 on `compute-1g`) and each project's share, so the survival bound still sees every wait beyond the pool; a parked wait holds a connection, about 26 KiB (the round-12 model), so 1,200 are about 31 MiB. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands a3839329, from the draft the owner ratified with the heartbeat and `local.parked` folded in, and checked against that commit.

### #104 26807d56 — Under enforce, a watch observation that carries a capability must verify it: a stream key observes only beside a verified token, never in place of a failed capability

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 8, finding M5. Closing it under enforce only, not in off and shadow, was the owner's choice (README, "Shared cells Q4"). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing.
- **Surface:** product.
- **Endpoint:** `GET /v1/streams/{name}/watches/{watch}/keys/{keyHex16}` with a capability carried as `?cap=` or `Authorization: Prisma-Watch <cap>`. `WatchService::authenticate` (`src/application/watch.rs`) now delegates the proof to `observation_proof`, outside its two frozen scopes, which only shrank.
- **Condition:** `STREAMS_AUTH_MODE=enforce`; a capability that does not verify (garbage, expired, over-lifetime, for another stream, watch, key or epoch); a matching `Prisma-Encryption-Key`; and no verified token. A carrier's bearer is not consulted: the gate classifies any `cap=` or `Prisma-Watch` request as a carrier.
- **Before:** `200` observation, charged to the project the capability names, and still served after every credential of that project was revoked.
- **After:** `403 watch_unauthorized` "a valid observation capability or Prisma-Encryption-Key is required", `retryable: false`, journaled as `watch_unauthorized` with no project attributed. Unchanged: a verified capability, with or without a key (`200`); a token plus a key and no capability (`200`); a token whose project or prefix does not cover the stream (`403`); no credential (`403`); off and shadow modes, where a carrier with only a matching key still observes.
- **Retry semantics:** Not retryable as sent: the client presents a capability that verifies, or drops the capability and sends its token with the key.
- **Who is affected:** Raw HTTP clients on enforce cells that send a stale or foreign capability with the stream key and no token. The SDK: none (`Watch.wait` mints a fresh capability per request from the stream key it holds and sends no key header, `sdk/src/index.ts`). An attacker who learned project B's stream key could observe B's watches with no credential, past revocation, charged to B's quotas; that is refused now.
- **Given up:** Nothing a legitimate client sends.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::a_capability_carrier_with_only_a_key_is_refused (A10 M5, un-ignored: exactly `(403, watch_unauthorized)`, and on the same cell the key beside B's token observes (`200`) and B's verified capability with no key and no token observes (`200`). Red on the unfixed tree: `key-only carrier: {"cursor":"01f56451db1eaf18:0","invalidated":false,...} left: 200 right: 401` (the leg's first oracle guessed 401))
  - unchanged and green: the `watch_observation` and `watch_admission` DSTs, `security_routes::only_the_exact_signed_watch_route_skips_the_token`, `review_security::r16_unauthorized_watch_shapes_are_independent_of_existence`
  - Controls: a matching key admitting any request again fails the leg exactly as its red; a key never admitting under enforce, even beside a token, fails the token-plus-key control and `watch_admission::account_watch_wait_charges_one_request_and_releases_its_subscription`.
- **Risk reason:** low: one predicate, enforce only, and the refused shape has no legitimate SDK producer. It does refuse a request that was served (a key-only carrier), which is the point. The 403's message still says the key alone suffices; its rewording lands later in phase A and amends this record. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 26807d56, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit; amended in 8c9582d5 (below).
- **Amendment (8c9582d5, the refusal's message; the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, README "Shared cells Q4"):** the uniform `403 watch_unauthorized` says "a valid observation capability or Prisma-Encryption-Key is required; under enforce the key observes only beside a verified token" in every mode (it was "... is required", which told an enforce client the key alone suffices). Status, code, `retryable: false`, audit tag and journal entry are unchanged, and the text stays the same for every unproven carrier, so it reveals nothing about the stream. Pinning: src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::a_capability_carrier_with_only_a_key_is_refused now asserts the message (red: the former text). Ratified with the record; written in the records commit of the push that lands 8c9582d5 and checked against it.

### #102 b6ed99eb — A stale-feed refusal wakes the auth refresher, so a cell that slept serves again after one out-of-cadence pass instead of at its next tick

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 3, finding R2 (field finding F5). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4"), and accepted the SDK's retry as the answer for design partners, with `Retry-After: 1` on the stale `503`s (README, "Shared cells Q10" (c)), which amends this record when it lands.
- **Surface:** both: every authenticated request a cell answers in `shadow` or `enforce` mode (the product surface; the raw surface under a workload JWT; live subscriptions through the lease; watch waits under a capability or key).
- **Endpoint:** Every customer-token route on `/v1/streams/**` (`verify_customer`); raw and fleet-internal routes under a workload token (`verify_internal`, which shares `verify_signature` and so the key-set check); `GET /v1/streams/{name}/records:sse` and every other live subscription (the lease re-check in `sse::auth`); watch waits (`status_and_quotas`). Not `GET /v1/debug/auth`, which reports staleness and never wakes the refresher. Owner: `src/auth/freshness.rs` (`fresh_jwks`, `fresh_policies`, `fresh_grants`, `refuse_stale`).
- **Condition:** At the request's wall-clock second one of the cell's feed snapshots is past its window: policies or grants older than `POLICY_STALENESS_MAX_SECS` (300 s), keys older than `JWKS_STALENESS_MAX_SECS` (21,600 s). The usual cause is a cell that slept longer than the window: Compute pauses the VM, the wall clock moves on and the refresher's monotonic tick does not. A refresher whose source fails for longer than the window gets here too.
- **Before:** The request was refused (503 and the feed's code; a live subscription's lease ended with `policy_stale` or `grants_stale`) and nothing else happened. The refresher fetched at its next tick (`STREAMS_AUTH_REFRESH_SECS`, monotonic); only an unknown `kid` could bring a pass forward. Field (F5): 90-130 s of `503 policy_stale` per wake, 8,857 to 29,586 refusals, 30-90% of client requests. In the new DST, with an hour's cadence and tokio time held still: after a wake 0 of 128 projects were served and the refresher ran no pass.
- **After:**
  - **The refusal is byte-identical:** the same status, `code` and `retryable: true`, and no `Retry-After` header (until the amendment below). A live subscription ends with the same lease reason.
  - **The refusal also wakes the refresher** through the existing nudge (`request_kid_refresh`): at most one per 30 s per instance, shared with the unknown-kid sighting. The refresher runs a full pass of keys, policies and grants at once, and requests after that pass lands are served. The pass takes as long as the source fetch.
  - **The window does not move:** a request is refused while a feed is past its window and served once it is not.
  - In the DST, for each feed: the first request is `(503, <feed>_stale)`; every concurrent request is that refusal or served; after the one extra pass all 128 projects are served (1,000 with `MT_CERT_PROJECTS=1000`), and the refresher ran exactly 2 passes (boot and the wake).
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged in class: a retryable 503. The SDK retries it after `Retry-After`, 1 s without the header, up to three times (`sdk/src/index.ts`), so an SDK client sees about 1 s of extra latency on its first request after a wake, where it used to run out of retries inside the 90-130 s window. A raw client still sees `503` for every request before the pass lands: the source fetch time, not the tick.
- **Who is affected:** Every tenant of a cell that sleeps longer than 300 s (shared cells, which are cheap because they sleep; dedicated cells and scaled-out spares, where F5 found the same window). Feed sources: at most one extra pass per 30 s per instance, only while a feed is stale. Abuse: none new; the policy and grant refusals need a token whose signature verifies, and the key-set refusal shares the unknown-kid branch's limiter, so an anonymous caller gets at most one pass per 30 s either way, and a nudge always runs a full pass.
- **Given up:** Nothing.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_lifecycle.rs::a_woken_cell_refreshes_on_its_first_stale_refusal (new. Red on the unfixed tree: `(stale feed, (refresh passes, projects served)) after each wake left: [("policy_stale", (1, 0)), ("grants_stale", (1, 0)), ("keys_stale", (1, 0))] right: [("policy_stale", (2, 128)), ("grants_stale", (2, 128)), ("keys_stale", (2, 128))]`)
  - src/auth/freshness.rs::tests::every_stale_refusal_wakes_the_refresher_once and ::a_fresh_read_does_not_wake_the_refresher (new)
  - unchanged and green: src/auth.rs::tests::every_freshness_reader_shares_one_boundary, the `security_modes` DSTs (the stale `503 policy_stale`), `security_policy::unknown_kid_nudge_is_rate_limited` and `::kid_nudge_fired_before_the_waiter_is_not_lost`, the `security_freshness::v01_*` lease-boundary DSTs.
  - Controls: `refuse_stale` without the nudge fails the leg exactly as its red; an unconditional nudge fails the unit test's fifth row (a second refusal inside 30 s woke it again).
- **Risk reason:** low: no response changes and the staleness window and its boundary are untouched; the only new effect is a refresh pass brought forward, bounded by a limiter callers could already trigger. Residual: the limiter compares wall-clock milliseconds, so a wall clock stepped back by more than 30 s suppresses nudges until it catches up and the tick is the fallback (true for unknown kids before, now for stale feeds too). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands b6ed99eb, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit; amended in 332673b5 (below).
- **Amendment (332673b5, `Retry-After: 1`; the owner's decision of 2026-10-08, README "Shared cells Q10" (c)):** the stale class's `503` (`policy_stale`, `grants_stale`, `keys_stale`) built by `auth_failure_response` (`src/product.rs`) carries `retry-after: 1`, so a raw client waits a second before its retry as the SDK does; status, code, body, `retryable` and journal scope are unchanged, the `421 wrong_cell` keeps `prisma-error-code`, and denials get no header. Every caller of that function answers it (the product wrapper's gate, project admission, the seal, the usage route, the entry's re-checks); a live subscription ended by a stale lease and a watch wait's own `503 policy_stale` answer separately and carry none. Who is affected: raw product clients during a stale window; the SDK already waited 1 s. Pinning: src/product/tests.rs::every_auth_refusal_keeps_its_response (red: `policy_stale left: None right: Some([49])`). Ratified with the record; written in the records commit of the push that lands 332673b5 and checked against it.

### #101 f7de42c7 — A cell never serves a policy that names the system project, its deployment's PROJECT_ID or a workspace equal to its ACCOUNT_ID, and reports how many it dropped

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 2, finding L2. Which identities a dedicated cell reserves was the owner's question: `PROJECT_ID` on every cell, `ACCOUNT_ID` only when k > 1, since on a dedicated cell `ACCOUNT_ID` may be the customer's own workspace (README, "Shared cells Q2" (a)). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4").
- **Surface:** product (customer tokens of a dropped project, on every product route); operator-debug for `GET /v1/debug/auth`.
- **Endpoint:** Every product route, for the dropped project's tokens; `GET /v1/debug/auth` (`feed_json`, `src/auth.rs`). The drop runs in `CellCeiling::apply` (`src/auth/ceiling.rs`) before `publish_policies` commits a snapshot.
- **Condition:** A policy whose `project_id` is the system project (`system`), or the deployment's `PROJECT_ID`, or whose `workspace_id` equals the deployment's `ACCOUNT_ID`. The last two apply once boot installs the cell's deployment identity (the owner-approved `bootstrap::run` wiring, #100).
- **Before:** Such a policy was served. A token for the deployment's `PROJECT_ID` created a stream (`201` in A12), in the raw surface's tenant, which fleet credentials reach; a project in the `ACCOUNT_ID` workspace was billed every unowned meter event. A system-project token was refused by verification (`is_system`), but its policy was published.
- **After:**
  - The policy is dropped before publication and logged with `tracing::warn` on each pass. Its tokens get `421 wrong_cell`, as for any project the cell does not serve.
  - `GET /v1/debug/auth` gains `feeds.policies.reservedDropped`: how many policies the last published snapshot dropped.
  - Live in production today: the system-project drop and the new member (0 unless a feed names `system`). The `PROJECT_ID` and `ACCOUNT_ID` rules wait for the wiring.
- **Retry semantics:** `421 wrong_cell` is not retryable at this cell (the SDK raises `WrongCellError`); the fix is a different project id or workspace in the feed.
- **Who is affected:** A feed that names `system` (none in the tree); once wired, any customer project placed under the deployment's own `PROJECT_ID`, or (k > 1) in a workspace equal to `ACCOUNT_ID`. `cell-admin` (#110) refuses all three at `admit`.
- **Given up:** Nothing: every dropped case crossed a trust or billing boundary.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/shared_cell_hostile.rs::reserved_ids_are_dropped_from_the_feed (A12, un-ignored: `(served, dropped)` = `(2, 3)` on `/v1/debug/auth`, and both tokens `421 wrong_cell`. Red on the unfixed tree: `a policy for proj-test left: (201, None) right: (421, Some("wrong_cell"))`)
  - src/auth/ceiling.rs::tests::reserved_identities_are_dropped_and_counted (the dedicated ceiling drops only the system project; a shared one drops all three and publishes the rest at the share)
  - Control: with `reserves()` always false, A12 fails `(served, dropped as reserved) left: (5, 0) right: (2, 3)`.
- **Risk reason:** low: every dropped case was a misconfiguration that crossed a trust or billing boundary, and the debug member is additive. Unlike most low records it refuses requests that were served (a dropped project's tokens), which is the point. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands f7de42c7, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit; amended in 75d28313 (below).
- **Amendment (75d28313, #130):** live in production. Boot gives the ceiling its deployment identity: the `PROJECT_ID` rule applies on every cell, the `ACCOUNT_ID` rule only when `PROJECT_SHARE_K` > 1. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08 (landing call C3), as drafted.

### #99 06000c6b — A policy feed whose project omits its quotas, or names a quota key the server does not know, is refused whole, where it published that project without limits

- **Program item:** Shared cells phase A, step 2, finding R3 (the owner's scope of 2026-10-07, README "Shared cells, first scope"; NEXT-WORK §14.4). The owner ratified this record as drafted on 2026-10-08, before landing (README, "Shared cells Q4").
- **Surface:** process (the project-policy feed the cell reads: `STREAMS_AUTH_POLICY_FILE` today, the platform policy feed later). Clients see it only through the cell as a whole.
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. The parse runs in `auth_feed::parse_policies` (`src/auth_feed.rs`) on every refresher pass; the wire type is `ProjectQuotas` (`src/project_policy.rs`: `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`, and `PolicyDoc.quotas` loses its serde default).
- **Condition:** A policy entry has no `quotas` object, or its `quotas` object has a key that is not one of the eight contract fields.
- **Before:** The entry parsed. A missing object, or a misspelled key (`request_per_sec`), left those fields at 0, and a 0 means "no project limit", so the project ran unlimited.
- **After:** The whole fetch is refused, as for any other strict-parse failure:
  - The previous snapshot stays published and keeps ageing; after `POLICY_STALENESS_MAX_SECS` (300 s) product requests on the cell get `503 policy_stale`. A cell that never published policies stays not ready.
  - Individual quota fields may still be absent. An absent field is 0, which on a shared cell takes the ceiling (#100).
- **Retry semantics:** `503 policy_stale` is retryable. The operator fixes the feed, and the next pass publishes it.
- **Who is affected:** Only a feed writer that violates the published contract: `contracts/streams-platform/v1/project-policies.schema.json` already requires `quotas` and sets `additionalProperties: false`.
