Every in-tree writer (`bench/k2cost/field/feeds.mjs`, `bench/soak/mtgen.mjs`, `scripts/mt-noisy-campaign.mjs`, `platform-demo/src/emulator.mjs`, `scripts/platform-e2e.mjs`) and the contract golden already conform.
- **Given up:** Nothing: the parser now enforces the contract it claimed.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/auth_feed.rs::tests::policies_parse_strictly (flipped: it refuses a misspelled quota key and a policy without quotas. Red on the unfixed tree: `a misspelled quota key parsed as Ok("ProjectQuotas { requests_per_sec: 0, ... }")`)
  - src/auth_feed.rs::tests::refresh_once_publishes_from_files_and_survives_a_broken_one (its fixture gains `"quotas": {}`; it published a contract-invalid policy)
- **Risk reason:** low: it moves the parser onto the contract it claims; the failure mode is the feed's existing loud, fail-closed path, and the alternative is a silently unlimited project. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-08, as drafted, before landing.
- **Check against commit:** written in the records commit of the push that lands 06000c6b, from the draft the owner ratified, and checked against that commit (the two attributes, the flipped test).

### #98 this record's commit — Under the group-commit pump a shard log's SlateDB flush timer runs every 60-90 s, not every 1-1.5 s, so a busy shard writes one WAL object per pump flush and no more

- **Program item:** The owner's decision of 2026-10-07 (README, "Owner decisions", row "Write tier": the WAL failsafe at 60 s and the usage drain at 8 s, "each with an edge record"; NEXT-WORK §14.2, item 2; lever E6 of the K2 cost plan). The 100 ms gap is #97; the usage drain at 8 s has a record of its own. Performance acceptance stays with the owner (AGENTS.md §1).
- **Surface:** process (operator-debug for the object store's request count, a cost the operator sees in `GET /v1/debug/store`: a busy shard writes fewer WAL objects, while the `pump` block of `GET /v1/debug/timings` counts the same flushes). What a client can observe is time (see "After").
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. `shard_settings` (`src/config/validation.rs`) gives every shard log's SlateDB its `flush_interval`, the period of SlateDB's own WAL flush timer. Under the pump that period is now the constant `EngineConfig::WAL_FAILSAFE_INTERVAL` (`src/config/model.rs`), and `bootstrap::run` adds its per-shard offset as before. The timer decides when a commit that is waiting out the pump's gap can be written early, and with it the acknowledgement of that append (raw `POST /v1/stream/{name}`, product `POST /v1/streams/{name}/records` and `records:batch`, a seal with a final record, the server's own appends to its system streams) and the wake of live tails that follows it. The history, telemetry, rollup and read-spool databases have settings of their own and are not affected.
- **Condition:** The process runs the pump (`WAL_GROUP_COMMIT=1`, the default since #77), whatever `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS` says.
- **Before:** `max(FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS, 1000)` ms, 1 s at the default 25, plus the offset `FNV-1a(prefix) mod (period / 2)` that `bootstrap::run` adds (O14a): 1.0-1.5 s per shard; 1.261 s for a single shard (prefix `""`), 1.421, 1.302, 1.112 and 1.231 s for the shards `00`, `01`, `10` and `11`. Each tick that found the WAL buffer non-empty froze it and wrote it as one WAL object.
  - Every commit wakes the pump, so no acknowledgement waited for a tick. At the 100 ms gap (#97), though, a commit waits in the buffer for up to the rest of the gap before the pump flushes it; a tick in that window wrote it early. That commit was acknowledged up to the rest of the gap sooner, and the shard wrote one more WAL object. On a busy shard about 0.8 ticks a second did so.
  - A deployment that set `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS` above 1000 had that value as the period (and the offset of half of it).
- **After:** 60 s, plus the same offset, now below 30 s: 60-90 s per shard; 76.3 s for a single shard (prefix `""`), 79.4, 71.8, 66.6 and 74.2 s for the shards `00`, `01`, `10` and `11`.
  - `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS` no longer sets it. Under the pump that setting is read only as the gap when `WAL_FLUSH_GAP_MS=0`. No setting restores the former period.
  - A busy shard's WAL objects are its pump flushes, plus at most one per tick, about one a minute.
  - A commit waiting out the gap waits for the pump. On a busy shard the mean acknowledgement time rises by about 1 ms (the owner's tier analysis, outside the repository, interpolated and not measured: at the 100 ms gap the 1 s timer split about 7% of saturated flush cycles). The commits a tick used to write early now wait for the rest of the gap, at most about 100 ms, as the other commits of the same cycle do. A quiet shard's append is unchanged.
  - The timer still fires once when the log opens (SlateDB's first tick is immediate). It still writes what the pump never flushes: the maintenance row that a shard's first open rebuilds without a commit, and the write of a commit group whose incarnation retired before publishing it (answered `Moved`, outcome unknown). Each becomes durable at the next pump flush, memtable flush (the engine's 5 s ticker, when records were appended) or clean close, or at the next tick, within 60-90 s instead of 1-1.5 s. Neither is acknowledged to anyone, and a crash before then loses nothing that a reopen does not rebuild or that its client did not already treat as unknown.
  - `WAL_GROUP_COMMIT=0` still runs SlateDB's tick at `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS`. No status, header, body or error code changes.
- **Measured:** locally only, by `bootstrap::tests::write_tier::a_busy_shard_writes_one_wal_object_per_pump_flush`: one shard engine built by `shard_config` and `shard_settings` from the default command line (no per-shard offset: `run` adds it), an in-memory store whose every WAL write takes exactly 50 ms, the dev profile, an append offered every 5 ms for 2 s, none waiting for another. With this change: 400 appends acknowledged, 21 WAL objects and 21 pump flushes in each of ten runs. At the former 1 s timer (six runs): 23 WAL objects and 21 pump flushes in each, one more object per tick (the rig has no per-shard offset, so its timer ticks every second). The slowest acknowledgement was 157-160 ms either way (a commit that arrives just after a flush starts: the gap, the settle, the write and the rig's overhead). Not measured: anything on Tigris, and the acknowledgement-time effect.
- **Retry semantics:** None (no client surface).
- **Who is affected:** Every deployment that runs the pump: the binary's default, every family of `scripts/effective-config/families` except `fra-ab-server` (`WAL_GROUP_COMMIT=0`, unchanged), and every rig that starts the binary.
  - Their `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS=25` (the costab, fleet, soak and docker scripts, the k2cost harness, the STAGING and GUIDE-COMPOSER recipes) restates the default and gave a 1 s period; they now get 60 s and need no edit. The rigs that pass `--flush-interval-ms 1 --wal-flush-gap-ms 2` (conformance, CI's field gate, the platform e2e and its negative twin, the LiveFeed canary and certification, the mt-noisy campaign) also got 1 s and now get 60 s.
  - The k2cost field harness's recorded cells ran the 1-1.5 s timer; every posture, `P-exp` included, now runs 60 s, so a rerun of a recorded cell writes fewer WAL objects than it did (about 4-5% on a busy fra shard at the 10 ms gap, the tier analysis). `bench/k2cost/field/deploy.py` and its README say so.
  - Experiments outside the repository that set `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS` to 600000 or more to switch the timer off now run it at 60 s.
- **Given up:** Nothing a client relied on: the early writes went to the commits that happened to wait when a tick fired. Saved: about 0.7-0.8 WAL PUTs a second per busy shard at the 100 ms gap, 10.8 to about 10.1 (−7%, about $7.6 per busy shard-month at Tigris's request price; the owner's tier analysis, interpolated from local measurements).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::the_default_commit_pipeline_is_the_pump_and_the_switch_still_selects_the_tick (red on the unchanged code: left `Some(1s)`, right `Some(60s)`; it also pins that `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS=120000` under the pump still gives 60 s, and that the tick still runs at `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests/provider_contract.rs::in_memory_store_meets_the_slatedb_contract_under_each_commit_pipeline and ::s3lite_meets_the_slatedb_and_http_contract_under_each_commit_pipeline (red: `in-memory` and `s3lite`, left `Some(1s)`, right `Some(60s)`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests/write_tier.rs::a_busy_shard_writes_one_wal_object_per_pump_flush (new; red: `every WAL object a busy shard writes is a pump flush; SlateDB's failsafe timer wrote 2 of them`, left `23`, right `21`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests/write_tier.rs::an_isolated_append_waits_for_one_wal_write_and_the_rest_of_the_gap (#97's test, which set `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS=60000` to keep the timer out of its one-second run; it now runs on the default command line unmodified)
  - The DST fixtures that state production's timer under the pump (`open_engine_cfg` in src/dst/tests/fixture_storage.rs, which the four durability_gather tests use, and runtime_open_gate::idle_engine_store_traffic_is_bounded_by_the_poll_cadence) now read the constant; all green.
  - Not pinned: the per-shard offset (in `bootstrap::run`, which no test can call), the acknowledgement-time effect, and anything on a rig that starts the binary.
- **Risk reason:** low: timing and the object-store request count only. No request that succeeded fails, none that failed succeeds, and no acknowledgement waits for the timer; the guarantees G1-G10 do not depend on it (a WAL write failure is fatal to the log, so the timer never retried what the pump could not write). WIRE-MATRIX is unchanged: it states no acknowledgement time. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the conformance suite, the platform e2e, the LiveFeed certification, the SDK smoke and any field run; no formal receipt is staled (`src/config/{validation,model,cli,tests}.rs`, `src/bootstrap/tests*` and `src/dst/tests/*` feed none). Awaits ratification.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change; not yet reviewed by an independent pass.

### #96 77beff8a — The server deletes each object with a keyed DELETE request, not a one-key DeleteObjects POST, so GET /v1/debug/store files the provider's timing and region counts for a delete in the delete cell of its key's class, not in mpu:other

- **Program item:** The owner's request of 2026-10-06 for the K2 cost plan, lever E1 ("Plain DELETE for GC"). Tigris's pricing bills no DELETE and does not name `DeleteObjects` (`POST /<bucket>?delete`, an XML list of keys), which object_store 0.14.1 sent for every delete, one key or many. Every delete the server issues is of one key, so the requests are as many as before.
- **Surface:** operator-debug
- **Endpoint:** No route changes. The object-store request a delete sends changes (`S3Store::build`, `src/bootstrap/s3_store.rs`: `with_disable_bulk_delete(true)` on both of the store's clients), and `GET /v1/debug/store[?window=][&swap=]` and `GET /operator/data.json` (`local.store`, the same snapshot) report it differently. No product, raw or fleet-internal answer changes.
- **Condition:** The server deletes an object: SlateDB's garbage collector (WAL SSTs, compacted SSTs, manifests, compaction files), the registry's replaced-incarnation and fork-debt deletes, the boot canary's probe. The debug members change only where the provider answers with `Server-Timing` and `x-tigris-served-from` (Tigris); the physical sniffer records nothing for a response without them (s3lite, InMemory).
- **Before:**
  - Each delete was one `POST /<bucket>?delete` whose XML body named the key, with `Content-MD5` and the body's SHA-256. The physical sniffer (`SniffService`, `src/store_timing/observations.rs`) maps a POST to op `mpu`, and classifies by the URL path, which names only the bucket: class `other`.
  - So Tigris's own time for every delete went into `ops["mpu:other"]` (`sn`, `sp50_ms`, `sp99_ms`), a cell whose client-side `n` is 0 unless a multipart upload of an `other`-class key ran in the window, and `served_from_by_class.<region>["mpu:other"]` counted every delete. The `delete:<class>` cells held only the client-side members (`n`, `err`, `p50_ms`, `p90_ms`, `p99_ms`, `max_ms`), without `sn`, `sp50_ms` or `sp99_ms`.
  - A key that held nothing was reported deleted by `DeleteObjects`, so the caller got `Ok`.
  - Tigris's request log and invoice show `DeleteObjects` requests.
- **After:**
  - Each delete is one `DELETE /<bucket>/<key>` without a body. A delete stream of several keys sends one DELETE per key, at most 20 at a time, where it sent one POST per 1,000 keys; only the provider contract's cleanup deletes more than one key per call.
  - The sniffer maps a DELETE to op `delete` and classifies the key's path, so Tigris's time for a delete goes into `ops["delete:wal"]`, `["delete:manifest"]`, `["delete:sst"]`, `["delete:fleet"]` or `["delete:other"]`, which gain `sn`, `sp50_ms` and `sp99_ms` beside their unchanged client-side members, and `served_from_by_class.<region>` counts it under the same `delete:<class>`. `ops["mpu:other"]` holds only multipart requests of `other`-class keys, and is absent from a window without one.
  - Unchanged: `totals` (one count per deleted path, at the store wrapper), the window's client-side members of every cell, `served_from` (one response per delete, as before), `wal_read_storm`, `slow`, `out_inflight_now` and `out_inflight_peak`.
  - A key that holds nothing: S3 and s3lite answer the keyed DELETE with 204, so the caller gets `Ok` as before. A provider that answered 404 would give the caller `NotFound`, counted in `totals` `delete:other.not_found` and the `delete:other` window's `err` whatever the key's class, because the store wrapper sees no path for a failed delete (`TimedDeleteStream`, `src/store_timing.rs`, classifies the empty path as `other`), while the physical sniffer files that 404's own timing under `delete:<class>` of the key; the provider contract now asserts `Ok`, and its real-provider run has not been made against Tigris.
  - The s3lite emulator's ledger (`GET /_s3lite/stats2`, which the cost harness reads) files a delete under its key's `<tier>/<kind>/delete`, where every delete was under `other/meta/delete`. `bench/k2cost/k2cells.py` prices the keyed cells free and `other/meta/delete` as Class A in the headline column, so a run of this binary prices its deletes free there; the `alt` column priced them free before.
- **Retry semantics:** None. object_store retries both requests after a 5xx, 429 or 408 and after a connect error, and neither after a transport timeout (a POST is not safe, and the DELETE request is not marked idempotent).
- **Who is affected:** Operators who read Tigris's delete latency or region counts from `ops["mpu:other"]` or `served_from_by_class`, and the cost tools that read s3lite's cells (`bench/k2cost`; its README's smoke finding "Every delete is a `POST ?delete`" describes the binaries before this change). No client.
- **Given up:** Nothing measurable: the same number of requests, each without the XML body and its digests. A delete stream of many keys, which nothing in production issues, sends one request per key instead of one per 1,000.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/bootstrap/tests/provider_contract.rs::s3lite_receives_every_delete_as_one_keyed_delete_request (new: through the production client on s3lite, a one-key delete, a delete of a key that holds nothing and a two-key delete stream, each `Ok`; the exact ledger cells `shard/wal/put` 1, `shard/sst/put` 2, `shard/wal/delete` 2 and `shard/sst/delete` 2, all 2xx, and no live object. Red on the unfixed tree: `each delete is one DELETE of its own key`, left `{"other/meta/delete": {"2xx": 3}, "shard/sst/put": {"2xx": 2}, "shard/wal/put": {"2xx": 1}}`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests/provider_contract/store_cases.rs, case `deletes` (new; run by `in_memory_store_meets_the_provider_contract`, `s3lite_through_the_production_client_meets_the_provider_contract` and the `#[ignore]`d `real_provider_meets_the_provider_contract`: a delete removes its object, and a delete of a key that holds nothing answers `Ok`. Green before and after on InMemory and s3lite, so not red-first: it is the qualification case for a provider's missing-key answer)
  - unchanged and green: src/store_timing/tests.rs::http_op_mapping (`DELETE` is op 4, `delete`) and the other provider-contract runners, the s3lite runner now cleaning up with keyed DELETEs.
  - Control: removing `.with_disable_bulk_delete(true)` is the unfixed tree, and fails the first test as above.
- **Risk reason:** low: no client answer changes, and the operator-debug members keep their names and types; only which cell Tigris's own delete timings and region counts fall in changes. The residual risk is a provider that answers a missing key's DELETE with 404, where `DeleteObjects` answered success: SlateDB's collector logs the error and does not retry it, the fork-debt settle and the spurious-debt drop already accept `NotFound`, and the closure-debt settle (`src/registry/replaced.rs`) returns it, which its caller (`src/billing/replaced.rs`) logs as a warning before the next sweep finds the debt gone. The real-provider contract run answers that. WIRE-MATRIX: the line of `GET /v1/debug/store` says where delete timings fall. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation leg (owner `bootstrap_s3_store`), Miri and the real-provider contract; TLA-001's receipt is stale (it digests `src/bootstrap/s3_store.rs`) and not re-recorded. Made on the owner's request of 2026-10-06 (the K2 cost plan); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-07.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change (77beff8a). An independent review of 2026-10-07 corrected one sentence, in the ratification's commit: a failed delete is counted under `delete:other` in `totals` and the window, not under its key's class.

### #94 f23ffc9d — GET /v1/debug/store reports a cumulative count of every object-store operation, by op, class and billed outcome, with the bytes put and got

- **Program item:** The owner's request of 2026-10-02 for a cost experiment on Prisma Compute and Tigris Standard that compares the cost per GB produced, consumed and retained with Cloudflare K2's prices. Tigris bills a Class A request (PUT, multipart create and parts, COPY, LIST) and a Class B request (GET, HEAD); a DELETE is free, and so are the answers its pricing page lists (301, 307, 400, 403, 405, 409, 411, 412, 416, 304, 500, 501). A 404 is not among them: it is billed at the request's class. The experiment needs exact cumulative request counts, and the server had none.
- **Surface:** operator-debug
- **Endpoint:** `GET /v1/debug/store[?window=][&swap=]` (behind the one `/v1/debug` gate, #39; the 401 path is unchanged) and `GET /operator/data.json`, whose `local.store` is the same snapshot (`src/operator.rs`). No product, raw or fleet-internal route, and no object-store request, changes.
- **Condition:** Every answer.
- **Before:** The snapshot's only request accounting was `ops`, per `<op>:<class>` over the trailing `window` (60 s by default), read from a ring that keeps the latest 16,384 events (`RING_CAP`, `src/store_timing/observations.rs`). Above about 16,000 operations a minute the window silently undercounts, and consecutive samples overlap, so they cannot be summed into a total. A 304, 404 and 412 were not told apart from other failures except that a 304 counted as a success.
- **After:**
  - The snapshot gains `totals`: `{"since_ms": <ms>, "bytes_put": <n>, "bytes_got": <n>, "ops": {"<op>:<class>": {"ok": <n>, "not_found": <n>, "unbilled": <n>, "err": <n>}, ...}}`. Each operation the store wrapper finishes (`TimingStore`, `src/store_timing.rs`) is counted once, where its ring event is recorded (`StoreStats::observe`), in its cell of the `OPS` and `CLASSES` that `ops` uses, under the outcome the provider bills (`Outcome`): `ok` a success; `not_found` a 404 (object_store's `NotFound`), which Tigris bills at the request's class; `unbilled` a 304 (`NotModified`) or 412 (`Precondition`, and the `AlreadyExists` that object_store answers for a conditional create's 412 and for a 409); `err` every other failure: a status left after the client's retries, a transport error, an aborted multipart upload, or a call or list stream dropped before it finished. A cell no operation reached is absent. The counters are atomics in the process-wide `StoreStats`; they never drop an event and nothing resets them, `swap=1` included, so the difference of two samples is exact.
  - `bytes_put` is the payload of every successful PUT and completed multipart upload; `bytes_got` is the existing cumulative GET byte count (`GET_BYTES`: the body bytes received, counted as they stream). `since_ms` is when the counters started: the process's first store operation or diagnostic read, so no operation precedes it.
  - One count is one object_store call, not one HTTP request: a retried request counts once, with its final outcome; a `list` counts once however many pages it fetched; a multipart upload counts once, as `mpu`, though its create, parts and complete are each a billed request; `get_ranges` counts once though object_store may send a GET per coalesced range; a delete counts once per deleted path. A list stream that yields an error and then ends counts as `ok`, as the window always did.
  - Every existing member keeps its name, value and type. The window's `ok` is unchanged (a success, or a 304), so `ops`' `err` is as before; the peak swap moved into `StoreStats::gauge` unchanged. serde_json's map is sorted (no `preserve_order`), so `totals` sorts among the old members.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators and the cost experiment, which reads the member. A reader that rejects unknown members fails; in the repository the bench scripts (`bench/soak/poll.py`, `bench/costab/*.sh`) save the snapshot whole, and the DST reads `shard_opens` and `out_inflight_peak` by name (`git grep`).
- **Given up:** Nothing. Each store operation gains one relaxed atomic increment, and the process holds 140 more counters (7 ops, 5 classes, 4 outcomes).
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/store_timing/tests.rs::debug_store_totals_count_every_wrapped_operation_by_billed_outcome (new: a `TimingStore` over `InMemory` puts a 1 MiB object, is refused twice (`AlreadyExists`, `Precondition`), completes a 2 MiB multipart upload, reads, revalidates (304), misses (404), reads beyond the end (an error), heads, copies, lists twice (one stream abandoned) and deletes; each `totals` cell and both byte totals grow at least by those operations, since other tests of the process may add their own, and `since_ms` holds. Red-first on the unfixed tree: `GET /v1/debug/store carries no cumulative totals: {"bulk_gate":…`; for the amendment, with the `not_found` count present but a 404 still counted `unbilled`: `ops/get:other/not_found grew 0, under 1`)
  - src/store_timing/observations/tests.rs::totals_count_every_operation_once_by_billed_outcome (new: ten operations and five PUT byte counts on a private `StoreStats`: the exact `totals`, a 404 in `not_found` and a 304 and 412 in `unbilled`, and the window's `(cell, n, err)` over the same events, the 304 counted `ok` and the 404 and 412 `err` as before)
  - src/store_timing/observations/tests.rs::outcomes_follow_the_provider_bill_and_keep_the_ring_ok (new: each object_store answer's outcome and window `ok`; amendment red: `left: Refused` / `right: NotFound` for `Err(NotFound { .. })`)
  - unchanged and green: the 22 other `store_timing::` tests, src/dst/tests/runtime_open_gate.rs::debug_store_reports_this_runtimes_shard_opens (the peak swap) and the 11 `security_routes` DSTs.
  - Controls: counting a refusal as `err` fails the observation test (`get:other` left `err` 1, `unbilled` 1; right `unbilled` 2) and the wrapper test (`ops/put:other/unbilled grew 0, under 2`); dropping a multipart upload's part bytes fails the wrapper test (`bytes_put grew 1048600, under 3145728`); restored. Rerun on the amendment: counting a refusal as `err` fails both tests (the exact `totals` at observations/tests.rs:299; `ops/put:other/unbilled grew 0, under 2`), and counting a 404 as `unbilled` fails both (observations/tests.rs:299; `ops/get:other/not_found grew 0, under 1`); restored.
- **Risk reason:** low: one member is added to two operator-debug answers and every existing member keeps its value; the store path gains one relaxed increment per operation. The risk is a tool outside the repository that requires the exact member set. WIRE-MATRIX: the line of `GET /v1/debug/store` describes the member. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile and Miri; no mutation leg is selected (`src/store_timing` is not a critical prefix). Made on the owner's request of 2026-10-02 (the K2 cost experiment); RATIFIED by the owner, as amended, on 2026-10-07. AMENDED before ratification on 2026-10-02: the record and f23ffc9d counted a 404 as `unbilled` on the belief that Tigris does not bill it; Tigris's pricing page (read 2026-10-02) leaves 404 out of its free answers, as docs/TIGRIS-404-COST.md had found, so the amendment's commit counts a 404 in its own `not_found` and leaves `unbilled` to the 304 and 412 (and 409).
- **Check against commit:** written with the change (f23ffc9d); amended with its correction (the 404 count) in fcd16d84.

### #95 2aaa4282 — The settle, the watch definitions and the watch wait answer their 200s with Cache-Control: no-store, as WIRE-MATRIX §2.16 to §2.18 list

- **Program item:** the owner's decisions of 2026-10-02 (NEXT-WORK, "Owner decisions (2026-10-02)": "the settle and watch answers gain the `no-store` WIRE-MATRIX lists"; README.md, the decisions row "Other answers missing their listed `no-store`"). #92's record named these answers as still lacking the header WIRE-MATRIX lists for them.
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:settle` (`settle_authorized`), `GET /v1/streams/{name}/watches` (`product_watches_list`), `GET /v1/streams/{name}/watches/{watch}` (`product_watch_get`) and `GET /v1/streams/{name}/watches/{watch}/keys/{keyHex16}` (`product_watch_wait`), all in `src/product.rs`. The answers are built by `json_ok_no_store` (the settle and the definitions) and `watch_observation_response` (the wait), a new module `src/product/answers.rs`.
- **Condition:** The request succeeds with 200: a settle the consumer accepted (an all-stale or empty settle included), a list of the collection's watch definitions or one definition, and a watch wait whose observation is a change, a resync or a timeout.
- **Before:** 200, `Content-Type: application/json`, built by `json_ok`, which sets no `Cache-Control`; the watch wait added `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. WIRE-MATRIX §2.16 to §2.18 listed `Cache-Control: no-store` for all four, and §2.19 said the usage answer lacked `Cache-Control` "unlike every other product 200", which these four contradicted.
- **After:** The same status, body and headers, plus `Cache-Control: no-store`; the watch wait keeps `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. `json_ok` is unchanged and now builds only the two usage answers (WIRE-MATRIX §2.19 and §2.21, which list no `Cache-Control`) and the fleet-internal sweep, queue-position and scan-page receivers. WIRE-MATRIX §2.19 now says which answers carry `no-store` and that the SSE subscription (§2.11) answers `no-cache`. src/product.rs shrinks by two lines (3,942); `product_watch_wait`'s exception contract (too_many_arguments) shrinks.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Product clients that settle, read watch definitions or wait on a watch (a browser holding a signed watch URL included), and any cache between them and the server: the answers now forbid storing them. The settle is a POST, which a shared cache could not store without explicit freshness information before either (RFC 9110 §9.3.3). The three GETs carried no freshness information, so a cache applying heuristic freshness (RFC 9111 §4.2.2) could have stored one and replayed a stale definition list or a stale observation; no such cache in front of the service is known. The SDK does not read `Cache-Control`.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/consumer_product.rs::product_consumer_per_key_fifo (extended: the ack's settle 200 asserts `cache-control` is exactly `no-store` beside its status. Red on the unfixed tree: left `(200, None)`, right `(200, Some("no-store"))`)
  - src/dst/tests/product_lifecycle.rs::product_create_metadata_roundtrip (extended, through the new helper `assert_watch_answer`: the definitions list, the single definition and a wait that times out each answer 200 with `content-type` `application/json`, `cache-control` `no-store` and `referrer-policy` `no-referrer` for the wait and absent otherwise, with exact bodies but for the wait's two opaque cursors. Red on the unfixed tree, at the list: left `(200, Some("application/json"), None, None)`, right `(200, Some("application/json"), Some("no-store"), None)`. Reverting only the single definition's call fails it at that path the same way; reverting only the wait's tail fails it at the wait: left `(200, Some("application/json"), None, Some("no-referrer"))`, right `(200, Some("application/json"), Some("no-store"), Some("no-referrer"))`)
  - unchanged and green: the `product::` unit tests and the `product_descriptor_reads`, `reads_product`, `security_operations`, `security_routes`, `security_modes`, `watch_observation`, `watch_admission`, `consumer_*`, `billing_operation_counts` and `runtime_isolation` DSTs.
- **Risk reason:** low: one header is added to four success answers; status, code and body are unchanged, and the answers now match WIRE-MATRIX §2.16 to §2.18 and the product's other JSON 200s. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation legs of the `product` and new `product_answers` owners (selected by CI for this change) and the re-record of TLA-003, whose receipt this change stales. Made on the owner's decision of 2026-10-02 ("So I am accepting/ratifying all of these"); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-07.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

### #90 89011daa — The platform contract names the workload operations segment-close and seal-fence, and the reference emulator mints both into every cell's workload token

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §5 F1-a, the follow-ups the owner approved on 2026-10-01 ("I approve all of these"; README.md, the decisions row "F1-a follow-ups"): "the platform contract names `seal-fence` and the `segment-close` it omitted". This is commit 5 of the F1-a plan. It closes the gap #88's "Who is affected" named.
- **Surface:** fleet-internal
- **Endpoint:** the platform contract, not the binary. The `operations` enum of `contracts/streams-platform/v1/workload-token-claims.schema.json` and the token shape in `docs/CONTROL-PLANE-INTEGRATION.md` §8.1; the reference emulator's default workload token (`platform-demo/src/emulator.mjs`, `--workload-ops`). The routes the two operations open: `POST /v1/internal/segment-close/{*name}` (`internal_segment_close`, `src/http.rs`, op `segment-close`) and `POST /v1/internal/seal-fence/{*name}` (#88, op `seal-fence`). No Rust source changes.
- **Condition:** A fleet in the release posture (`FLEET_AUTH_MODE=workload`, no static fleet token) whose platform mints workload tokens as the contract describes, and a relay of either operation: a seal, split or merge closing a segment another instance owns (`relay_segment_close`, `src/application/topology.rs`), or a seal takeover fencing the old final at its owner (`relay_seal_fence`, #89).
- **Before:** The enum listed eight operations, not the cell's ten (`InternalOperation`, `src/http/internal_routes.rs`): a token valid under the contract could not name `segment-close` or `seal-fence`, and the emulator minted `telemetry-append,segment-read`. The owner answered such a relay 401 `unauthorized`. A seal stayed resumable (product and raw 503 `seal_incomplete`, retryable; for the fence, the sender's Resumable "answered 401"), and a split or merge stayed pending, until a request or the transition ran on the owner itself.
- **After:** The enum lists the cell's ten operations; §8.1 names both in the token shape, states the vocabulary, and says what a cell's token without them cannot do. The emulator's default token is `telemetry-append,segment-read,segment-close,seal-fence`. A platform that mints them lets both relays authenticate, and their answers are the receivers' (#88; WIRE-MATRIX §3, segment-close). Deployments on the static fleet token are unchanged: it keeps full authority (SR3-1).
- **Retry semantics:** Unchanged in the binary. In a fleet whose tokens name both operations, the 401-driven retryable answers above become the relays' own outcomes.
- **Who is affected:** The Prisma platform's workload-token minting (Prisma Auth, Compute identity), which must add both strings to each cell's token; fleets running without the static token; users of the reference emulator (the platform e2e, the bench rigs), whose cells' tokens now carry two more operations. Not changed: `docs/MULTITENANCY.md` §14.1's r4 status block still lists r4's eight operations; that document is a frozen contract, changed only by a contract-revision commit (the owner's).
- **Given up:** Nothing. Each operation still opens exactly one route under its target binding; a token naming neither keeps today's answers.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - scripts/platform-e2e.mjs, "Operation-scoped workload identity": the cell's own token, as the emulator mints it by default, validates against the claims schema and names `segment-close` and `seal-fence`; a token naming exactly `segment-close` gets 400 `invalid_target` from `POST /v1/internal/segment-close/e2e/orders?seg_id=0&seal_gen=1` (authenticated; the request carries no target headers, so it stops before any registry or engine work), and a `segment-read` token gets 401 there; the same pair for `seal-fence` on `POST /v1/internal/seal-fence/e2e/orders?fence_to=1`. Red on the unfixed contract (the battery's validator, the emulator's new default claims against the old schema): `$.operations[2]: "segment-close" not in enum`, `$.operations[3]: "seal-fence" not in enum`.
  - unchanged and green, the cell side: src/dst/tests/seal_fence_receiver.rs::seal_fence_receiver_demands_its_exact_operation, src/dst/tests/seal_convergence.rs::segment_close_receiver_scopes_authorizes_and_is_idempotent, src/dst/tests/security_workload.rs::workload_jwt_operations_scope_the_internal_surface.
- **Risk reason:** low: no binary or wire change. A contract document and the reference emulator name two operations the cell already defined and enforced; this widens what a contract-conformant platform may mint, and each operation authorizes one route under its target binding. Not run for this record: the platform e2e (a release build on fixed ports).

### #93 be2907b4 — The rollup readiness report counts the pending artifacts that cannot publish: pendingArtifactsBlockedCorrupt and pendingArtifactsTotal join pendingArtifacts, which keeps its value

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §7 (bug #7), commit C1 of `plans/plans20/rollup-rekey-migration.md`. The external reviewer's condition that the owner adopted on 2026-09-24 asks for the operator's `pendingArtifacts` to be split into publishable, blocked-corrupt and total counts; the plan's edge item E1 is marked "approved: the reviewer's split", and the owner said on 2026-10-01 "proceed with the next work". C2-C4 (the re-keying itself) are not part of this record.
- **Surface:** operator-debug
- **Endpoint:** `GET /operator/billing.json` (bearer-gated; the 401 path is unchanged): the `rollup` member on an instance whose rollup is installed (`ROLLUP=1`). No product, raw or fleet-internal route. The hourly artifact publisher (`publish_artifacts`, `pending_artifacts(64)`) publishes exactly what it published before.
- **Condition:** Every answer while the rollup is installed. The values a reader already had differ in no case; the new members are non-zero only when `artifact-pending/` holds a row whose key does not split into month, account, project and stream (a non-UTF-8 key included) or whose body does not decode as a month row.
- **Before:** `rollup` had six members: `running`, `lastApplyMs`, `lastApplyAgeSecs`, `oldestUnclosedMonth`, `pendingArtifacts` (the decodable rows, the scan stopping after the 1,000th) and `pendingCorrectionArtifacts`. A row that does not decode was logged on every scan (#51) but counted nowhere, so the report could show `pendingArtifacts: 0` while rows stayed in the outbox forever and the month's invoice inputs were incomplete.
- **After:**
  - `rollup` gains `pendingArtifactsBlockedCorrupt` (the rows that do not decode, which never publish) and `pendingArtifactsTotal` (both). One scan counts all three and stops where the publisher's scan of the same cap stops: after the row that brings the publishable count to 1,000. The two scans share one row classifier (`pending_artifact_row` in `src/rollup.rs`), so a row is publishable in the report exactly when the publisher publishes it.
  - `pendingArtifacts` keeps its exact value and cap, and the five other members keep their names, values and types. serde_json's map is sorted (no `preserve_order`), so the new members sort among the old ones and every old member is serialized as before. The member is now the typed `RollupReadiness` of `src/rollup/readiness.rs`, which `billing_readiness_axum` serializes; `{"running": false}` without a rollup is unchanged. No status, code or header changes.
  - A failed scan reports 0 for all three counts, as `pendingArtifacts` did. Each GET still logs every blocked row it scans, with the same fields and messages (#51).
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators and dashboards that read the report. A reader that reads members by name sees the same `pendingArtifacts`; a reader that rejects unknown members fails. In the repository nothing reads the `rollup` member by name except the RUNBOOK table, which now names the three counts and adds a row for blocked rows (operator repair): no script under `bench/`, `scripts/`, `sdk/` or `deploy/` (`git grep`).
- **Given up:** Nothing.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/billing_readiness.rs::the_rollup_report_splits_pending_artifacts_by_publishability (new: four pending rows over the wire, two publishable, one whose body is not JSON and one whose key is short: `pendingArtifacts` 2, `pendingArtifactsBlockedCorrupt` 2, `pendingArtifactsTotal` 4, the five other members at `true`, 0, -1, null and 0, and exactly the eight member names. Red-first on the unfixed tree: `assertion `left == right` failed: rows the publisher skips because they do not decode`, left `Null`, right `Number(2)`)
  - src/rollup/accounting_failure_tests.rs::the_outbox_count_stops_where_the_publisher_scan_stops (new: five rows in key order, publishable, blocked, publishable, publishable, blocked; at cap 2 the count is 2 publishable, 1 blocked, 3 in total, and the publisher's scan at cap 2 returns s1 and s3, so both stop at s3; at cap 64 the count is 3, 2 and 5. Red-first: it does not compile, `error[E0599]: no method named `artifact_outbox` found for struct `UsageRollup` in the current scope`)
  - src/rollup/readiness.rs::tests::the_report_member_reads_the_rollup_and_ages_the_last_apply (new: the eight values for a rollup with an oldest-unclosed marker, a pending correction and one blocked row, and the age in whole seconds, 7 at 10,999 ms after an apply at 3,000 ms and -1 before any apply)
  - unchanged and green: the four other `billing_readiness` DSTs, `rollup::accounting_failure_tests::an_undecodable_pending_artifact_is_logged_and_stays_pending` and both pinned `r14_` tests.
  - Control (the plan's K8): `artifact_outbox` counting only publishable rows into `total` fails the DST with `pendingArtifactsTotal counts every pending row`, left `Number(2)`, right `Number(4)`, and the two unit tests with it; restored, hash verified.
- **Risk reason:** low: one operator route gains two members and every existing member keeps its value; nothing in the repository requires the member set. The risk is a tool outside the repository that requires the exact set. WIRE-MATRIX: the line of the route lists the `rollup` members and their owner. Not run for this record: the mutation legs of the `http`, `rollup`, `rollup_close` and new `rollup_readiness` owners (selected by CI for this change) and Miri. Made on the owner's decision E1 and instruction of 2026-10-01; RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-02.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

### #91 24493671 — A billing close or retention flag a shard drops unapplied is refused, logged and counted, and the tombstone walk and the closure-debt pass replay it at the next sweep

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §2, the lost close's exact fix, which the owner approved on 2026-10-01 ("I approve all of these"; README.md, "Lost close, the exact fix"): a reply on the committer's billing close and retention flag, answered with a retryable refusal at the three sites that dropped them, with a warning and a counter, and the walk and the debt pass stopping their page on a refusal. #87 (9f25cf96, ratified) stopped the two passes' own hand-back from dropping their op; this makes every drop answered.
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** No status, code, header or body changes. What changes: (1) a hard delete, `DELETE /v1/stream/{name}` and `DELETE /v1/streams/{name}` (`submit_billing_closes`, `src/application/creation/deletion.rs`): it answers after each segment's billing close is applied and durable, or refused, where it answered once the close entered the committer queue; (2) the storage billed for an incarnation whose close a shard dropped: `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage[?month=][&streamId=]` and `GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage[?month=]`, the `_usage` ledger's closing snapshot and the rollup objects built from it, as in #87; (3) operator: a new warning, `the shard dropped a billing op without applying it: refused, for its submitter to retry` (fields `segment`, `op`, `waiting`); the walk's and the debt pass's warnings on a refusal say that the page or the debt replays; `tombstoneWalkCloseSubmits` in `GET /operator/billing.json` counts the closes the walk and the debt pass had applied, where it counted those enqueued; and a process counter, `BILLING_OPS_REFUSED` (`src/shard/billing_ops.rs`), which tests read and no debug surface reports: `src/http.rs` is at its line ceiling and `ops::collect_snapshot` is a frozen exception scope.
- **Condition:** A shard drops a billing close (`CommitOp::BillingClose`) or retention flag (`CommitOp::BillingRetained`) without applying it. Its engine retired (an ownership move, a sweep eviction, a failed write, a shutdown) while the op waited in the queue (the drain arm of `committer_loop`), the committer took it after the engine closed (`reject_op`), or it rode a group that `CommitTransaction::run` refused on a closed engine; or the group's accounting read or the stream's load failed before the op was staged; or the queue was already closed. The submitters are the tombstone walk, the closure-debt pass, the usage drain (its closes of dead and replaced rows and its retention flags) and the hard delete.
- **Before:** `submit_billing_close` and `submit_billing_retained` returned `Ok` once the op entered the queue. A dropped op was answered by nothing, logged nowhere and counted nowhere, and every submitter went on as if it had landed. The walk moved past the segment and came back only after a whole catalog pass (ceil(N/256) sweeps of 300 s); the debt pass went on to the next debt and came back when its cursor did; the drain emitted the incarnation's lifecycle event and found the row still dirty on its next round; `tombstoneWalkCloseSubmits` counted the close. The row stayed as it was, its gauge open, and every month the rollup closed carried the dead gauge onto that month's invoice until a later visit landed the close, which the late-close path (#66) then corrected. After #87 the two passes no longer dropped their own op by handing their shard back, but a retirement from anywhere else still dropped it silently.
- **After:** Each billing op carries its reply (`BillingReply`, `src/shard/billing_ops.rs`). Staged in a group (`src/shard/transaction/billing.rs`), it joins the group's replies: `Ok` once the group is durable, the group's refusal when its write fails or its engine retires before it is durable. Dropped before it was staged, it answers as it drops: a retryable refusal (`Moved`), the warning above and one count. The submits await the answer. The walk and the debt pass stop on a refusal: the walk replays its page and the debt pass resumes at that debt at the next sweep, and a shard the step cold-opened is settled as after any step that applied nothing. The drain leaves the row dirty for its next round, as it did for a failed submit, and emits the lifecycle event only once the close is durable. The hard delete logs its existing warning, and the tombstone's debt and the walk close the segment later. A refused op changed nothing, and its retry closes at the same persisted instant (a close that would change nothing is skipped, #72).
- **Retry semantics:** None at the HTTP level: a hard delete answers the same, later. Internally a refused close is retried at the next sweep, at the same persisted instant, instead of after a catalog pass.
- **Who is affected:** Customers whose dead incarnations' closes a retiring shard dropped: their storage stops billing at the next sweep, with fewer late-close corrections (#66). Clients of a hard delete: the answer waits, per segment and in turn, for one committer group and its durable flush, which is what an append's acknowledgement waits for. Operators: one warning per dropped op; `tombstoneWalkCloseSubmits` no longer counts a close that was dropped; a walk or debt pass waits for each close it submits to be durable before it goes on.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/shard/billing_read_tests.rs::a_billing_op_is_refused_once_when_a_retiring_committer_drops_it_and_answered_once_applied (new, red-first in 5 of 5 runs: `the committer answered a billing op it dropped (retained: false): Ok(())`; green: a close and a flag dropped in a group refused on a closed engine are each refused, counted once and leave the durable row as it was; an applied close and flag answer `Ok` with the row closed and flagged, and count nothing)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_walk_custody.rs::a_close_the_shard_refuses_stops_the_walk_and_the_next_walk_applies_it (new, red-first in 5 of 5 runs: `the walk moved past a close the shard refused`, left 2, right 0)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_walk_custody.rs::a_close_the_shard_refuses_stops_the_debt_pass_and_the_next_pass_applies_it (new, red-first in 5 of 5 runs: `the debt pass moved past a close the shard refused`, left 2, right 0)
  - unchanged and green: #87's three DSTs and the hand-back DST in `billing_walk_custody`, the other `shard::billing_read_tests` (R13 repinned: its three accounting ops are built by a helper, with detached replies), the `billing_*` DSTs, `runtime_sweep`, the `billing::` and `shard::` unit tests, and the DSTs that hard-delete streams.
- **Risk reason:** low: no status, code, header or body shape changes and no stored format changes; the billed figures that change are storage past a close instant that a dropped op left open, as in #87, and a hard delete waits for one durable group per segment more. WIRE-MATRIX §1.5 now says the delete's billing close is applied before the answer. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation legs CI selects (`shard`, `shard_billing_ops`, `transaction_group`, `transaction_billing`, `billing_walk`, `billing_replaced`), the six formal receipts the change stales and the platform e2e rigs. Made on the owner's decision of 2026-10-01 ("I approve all of these"); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-02.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. Afterwards the mutation leg over the batch found the walk-side guard of #87 (`if ours && !applied` in `src/billing/walk.rs` and `src/billing/replaced.rs`) equivalent: since this change the op's answer waits for its group, so the op is durable before the settle, and the settle's own debt probe keeps a shard that holds an applied close's dirty row. The next commit removes the guard; the shard a step applied a retention flag on is now handed back at once instead of staying scheduler-held, which changes residency only.

### #92 1f03aef2 — A seal with a final record answers its 200 with Cache-Control: no-store, as a plain seal does

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §6, F2's observations: the composition test of 445955e9 (`src/dst/tests/retiring_written_group.rs`) observed that the seal-with-final 200 lacks the `Cache-Control: no-store` WIRE-MATRIX §2.5 lists. The owner decided on 2026-10-01 that the code follows the document (README.md, the "F2 observations" row).
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** `POST /v1/streams/{name}:seal` with a body carrying `final`, present even as `null` (`product_seal`, `src/product.rs`). The answer is the constant `SEALED` (`src/product/seal_request.rs`).
- **Condition:** The seal with a final record succeeds (`application::lifecycle::seal_final` returns `Ok`): the request that appends the final and seals, an exact replay of the operation that sealed, and the exact retry that completes an owed final.
- **Before:** 200, `Content-Type: application/json`, body `{"sealed":true}`, built by `json_ok`, which sets no `Cache-Control`. The plain seal's 200 (`product_seal_only`) and every product error answer (`perr`) already carried `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- **After:** The same status, body and headers, plus `Cache-Control: no-store`. The other answers `json_ok` builds are unchanged: the settle, the watch definitions and the watch wait (WIRE-MATRIX §2.16 to §2.18 list `no-store` for them as well, and they still lack it), the two usage routes (§2.19 and §2.21 say they carry no `Cache-Control`) and the fleet-internal sweep, queue-position and scan-page receivers.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Product clients that seal with a final record, and any cache between them and the server: the answer now forbids storing it, as the plain seal's already did. A POST answer without explicit freshness information was not storable by a shared cache before either (RFC 9110 §9.3.3), so beyond the header itself no client should observe a difference. The SDK does not read `Cache-Control`.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/retiring_written_group.rs::a_stranded_product_seal_final_stays_owed_and_its_exact_retry_seals_on_the_successor (extended: the exact retry's 200 asserts `cache-control` is exactly `no-store` beside its status, content type and body. Red on the unfixed tree: left `(200, Some("application/json"), None, Object {"sealed": Bool(true)})`, right `(200, Some("application/json"), Some("no-store"), Object {"sealed": Bool(true)})`; reverting `src/product.rs` and `src/product/seal_request.rs` alone fails it the same way)
  - unchanged and green: the `product::` unit tests and the `product_lifecycle`, `product_descriptor_reads`, `security_seal`, `security_operations`, `security_routes` and `seal_*` DSTs.
- **Risk reason:** low: one header is added to a success answer; status, code and body are unchanged, and the answer now matches the plain seal's and WIRE-MATRIX §2.5. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation legs of the `product` and `product_seal_request` owners (selected by CI for this change) and the re-record of TLA-003, whose receipt this change stales. Made on the owner's decision of 2026-10-01 ("I approve all of these"); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-02, who also accepted the hard DELETE's added latency (one durable commit per segment, in turn), the re-pinned R13 test and the new meaning of `tombstoneWalkCloseSubmits` (applied closes).
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

### #88 433b60e5 — A fleet peer raises a segment's seal fence at the segment's owner: POST /v1/internal/seal-fence answers the owner committer's closed-report only after the fence is durable

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §5 F1-a, the owner's decision to implement "a typed, authenticated fleet-internal seal-fence operation" (README.md, the F1 row). This is the receiver, step 2 of the F1-a plan; #89 is the sender that relays to it. The plan's defaults are taken on the owner's instruction: the claim string is `seal-fence`, a new `InternalOperation::SealFence`, and the receiver makes the three choices listed under "After".
- **Surface:** fleet-internal
- **Endpoint:** new `POST /v1/internal/seal-fence/{*name}?fence_to=<u64>` (`internal_seal_fence`, `src/http/internal_routes.rs`). Credential: the static fleet token, or a workload JWT whose `operations` claim names exactly `seal-fence`. Headers: the four target headers `InternalTarget::headers` builds (project, epoch as 32 hex, segment, and identity as 32 hex). `fence_to` is the takeover's reserved generation. Empty body.
- **Condition:** Any request to the path.
- **Before:** No route: 404 with an empty body.
- **After:** The receiver answers in this order:
  1. 401 `unauthorized` "fleet-internal credential required" for any other credential, before any registry or engine work. A missing or non-numeric `fence_to` is axum's Query rejection (400, plain text), decided before authentication, as a bad `seg_id` is on segment-close.
  2. 400 `invalid_target` (perr envelope, `retryable: false`) for a missing or malformed project header or a non-canonical name.
  3. The receiver drops its cached descriptor and re-reads it: 404 `not_found` only for a positive "no descriptor", 503 `temporarily_unavailable` when the registry could not be read.
  4. `verify_internal_target`: 400 `invalid_target` for a missing or malformed epoch or segment header; 409 `stale_target` for the epoch, project, segment or identity. The segment and identity come only from here: there is no `seg_id` query.
  5. 409 `fence_unreserved` when `fence_to` exceeds the descriptor's `seal_gen_counter`.
  6. 400 `unknown_segment` (no route for the segment; unreachable after step 4).
  7. The shard directory's answer, verbatim: 409 `not_ring_owner` "shard <prefix> belongs to <owner>" + `Streams-Replay-To: <owner>`, decided before any open, so a non-owner opens no writer; 503 with the opening code + `retry-after`; 500 `shard_open`.
  8. 503 `fence_unconfirmed` when the committer queue is full or closed, when the committer refused the fence (a retiring engine's `Moved`, an unverified fence row, a rejected group), or when the reply was dropped.
  9. 200 `application/json` `{"closed": <bool>}`, sent only after the owner's committer released the fence's group as durable. The fence travels the queue appends and closes use, so it is staged behind every one queued before it on that engine; `closed` is the segment's state then. `false` means no close or claim-tagged append below the fence can close the segment afterwards, on this engine or any replacement (the row `<identity>G` is written in the fence's own group); `true` means an earlier close committed.
  - The three deliberate differences from segment-close: (a) the registry invalidate before the read, so a cached older incarnation cannot answer `stale_target` for the live one (the coordinator re-reads before it reserves); (b) no liveness gate, so a soft-deleted or expired descriptor of the same incarnation stays fenceable, as it is for the local fence, whose binding is the epoch alone (the Item 30 remainder in `report/decisions.json`, decided for this route only; segment-close keeps its gate); (c) the generation bound `fence_to <= seal_gen_counter`: the fence is monotone, and a fence above every reservation would refuse every claim the incarnation could still install. A fence at or below the counter is no stronger than a real reservation.
  - Idempotent: the fence is max(current, `fence_to`), so a repeat at the same or a lower generation answers the same `closed` and never lowers the fence. The receiver never relays.
  - Error envelope: `err_resp` (no `retryable` member), as on segment-close, except the perr answers of steps 2 and 4.
- **Retry semantics:** 401, 400, 404 and 409 are decided without touching the engine; 409 `not_ring_owner` names the owner in `Streams-Replay-To`. Every 503 is retryable, and a retry is idempotent. A 200 is final for its generation.
- **Who is affected:** Fleet peers only. Nothing sends to the route until #89. A JWT-only fleet also needs `seal-fence` in the platform's workload tokens; the platform contract (`contracts/streams-platform/v1/workload-token-claims.schema.json`) does not list it yet, nor `segment-close` (the plan's commit 5, awaiting the owner).
- **Given up:** Nothing. The static bridge token keeps full authority (SR3-1).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/seal_fence_receiver.rs::seal_fence_receiver_demands_its_exact_operation (wrong ingress, credentials: tokens with the operations [], ["segment-read"], ["segment-close"] and ["everything"] get 401 `unauthorized` and leave the engine's enqueue count and the fence row untouched; a `seal-fence` token at `fence_to=7` gets 200 exactly `{"closed":false}` and the row holds 7; the same token on segment-close gets 401). Red on the unfixed tree: `seal-fence must demand its exact operation ([])`, left 404, right 401.
  - src/dst/tests/seal_fence_receiver.rs::seal_fence_receiver_binds_target_and_generation (wrong ingress, target: another incarnation's epoch, segment 7 and a zeroed identity get 409 `stale_target`, no epoch header 400 `invalid_target`, another project 404 `not_found`, `fence_to=8` over a counter of 7 409 `fence_unreserved`, no or a non-numeric `fence_to` 400; none reaches the engine or writes the row; then the bound request at 7 gets `{"closed":false}` and the row holds 7). Red: `another incarnation`, left 404, right 409.
  - src/dst/tests/seal_fence_receiver.rs::seal_fence_receiver_on_a_non_owner_redirects_without_opening_a_writer (wrong ingress, owner: with the ring moving shard 00 to a live peer, two requests each get 409 `not_ring_owner` + `streams-replay-to: elsewhere`, and the instance holds no engine for 00). Red: `attempt 0`, left 404, right 409.
  - src/dst/tests/seal_fence_receiver.rs::seal_fence_receiver_answers_after_durability_and_is_idempotent (crash around durability, the receiver's half: with the owner's dispatch held, the request has reached the engine and is still pending after 150 ms; after the release it gets `{"closed":false}` and the row holds 7; a close at generation 6 is then `SealSuperseded` and one at 7 closes; a repeat at 7 and one at 3 get `{"closed":true}` and the row stays 7). Red: `the fence: the request never reached the engine`.
  - src/dst/tests/seal_fence_receiver.rs::seal_fence_receiver_never_answers_closed_for_a_failed_group (crash around durability, a failed write: an append of the same stream and the fence share a held group that is made to fail; the fence gets 503 `fence_unconfirmed`, the failpoint fired once, the reopened engine holds no row, and a bounded retry gets `{"closed":false}` with the row at 7). Red: `the fence: the request never reached the engine`.
  - unchanged and green: src/dst/tests/security_workload.rs::workload_jwt_operations_scope_the_internal_surface and src/dst/tests/seal_convergence.rs::segment_close_receiver_scopes_authorizes_and_is_idempotent.
- **Risk reason:** low: a new authenticated route with segment-close's target binding and a generation bound; nothing existing changes. Its only authority is placing a fence, which is monotone, idempotent and bounded by a reservation the registry already holds. WIRE-MATRIX §3 has the route's row. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile and the mutation leg of the `http_internal_routes` owner (selected by CI for this change).
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. Made on the owner's instruction of 2026-09-30 ("Do them all in that order! use your own judgement"); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-01.

### #3 1994fc25 — Consumer pull honours its deadline under a blocked dead-letter handoff

- **Program item:** bug #4
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:pull (application::consumer::delivery::pull). The shared dlq_and_settle is also reached from POST /v1/streams/{name}/consumers/{consumer}:settle, where the response fields dlq and dlqBlocked do not change.
- **Condition:** A Receive reports poisoned leases: max-delivery reached on an expired lease. Two cases. (a) The dead-letter handoff settles nothing: the target was deleted or recreated as another incarnation, the target lookup failed, a by_off miss, a definitive append rejection, a transient append error, or engine re-resolution failed (the last one is removed). (b) The same Receive also granted leases to OTHER routing keys.
- **Before:** (a) The branch ran `let _ = dlq_and_settle(..)` and then an unconditional `continue 'outer` that jumped above the only deadline check and sleep. The pull never answered, even with waitMs=0. Every pass cost a read_coverage and a durable cursor put. The HTTP request hung until the client or proxy timed out. (b) The poison branch came before delivery, so leases the Receive had durably granted to other keys were dropped undelivered. Each lost an attempt and a visibility window, and at maxAttempts=1 a record was dead-lettered without ever reaching a client. The response then came from a later Receive.
- **After:** (a) 200 {"messages":[],"backlog":n} by the pull's waitMs deadline (at most 25 s, 50 ms poll loop). The poisoned lease stays retained, so its key stays blocked, and a failed dead-letter append is logged at warn. (b) 200 with the co-granted leases in messages (id, routingKey, attempts, leaseToken, value), delivered whether or not the handoff settled anything. The pull loops back only when the handoff settled more than 0 leases, which is bounded by the poisoned count. Status and error codes are unchanged.
- **Retry semantics:** None at the status/code level. A request that hung forever now returns a 200 empty page at its deadline. The number of attempts consumed per record drops, because leases are no longer dropped.
- **Who is affected:** Product consumer (queue) users, SDK pull loops, where poison and dead-lettering occur. The hang needs a dead-letter target whose handoff cannot settle, for example a DLQ stream that was deleted and recreated. Dropped leases affected any consumer whose Receive mixed poison with healthy keys.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - a_blocked_dead_letter_handoff_neither_wedges_the_pull_nor_drops_other_keys_leases (src/dst/tests/consumer_dlq.rs; 5 s bound; waitMs default and 200)
  - a_settled_dead_letter_handoff_still_delivers_the_same_receives_leases (src/dst/tests/consumer_dlq.rs)
- **Risk reason:** No status, code or body-shape change. One case could not succeed before at all (an infinite hang now answers 200). The other now returns records that used to be silently dropped. Clients see more complete 200 pages and lower attempt counts.
- **Check against commit:** None material. The settle path also changed internally: it passes the held engine instead of re-resolving per record, and it logs transient dead-letter append failures. Its dlq and dlqBlocked counts are unchanged, except that a failed per-record engine re-resolution can no longer skip a settlement.

### #4 d2e87129 + 3dfb970f — Postings cache no longer over-claims or publishes stale coverage

- **Program item:** bug #1
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** GET /v1/streams/{name}/records?routingKey=... and its live :long-poll/:sse forms. Every product read carries a selector (the default key is ""), so this covers any product read served from the absorbed history partition via read_history2_keyed_cached. Fleet-internal /v1/internal/segment-read relays that carry a key use the same path. No raw-surface impact: raw reads are keyless.
- **Condition:** Default configuration (POSTINGS_CACHE_BYTES 64 MiB). Two triggers. (1) Over-claim: a cold or extension postings load (spawn_load/prefetch) published provable_to as the bucket end (a multiple of 65,536) instead of clamping it to its absorbed target, claiming postings that did not exist yet. (2) Stale publish: spawn_load built its slice from its lead-time snapshot and overwrote a write-through install_chunk that landed mid-load. Either way, a later catch-up read over the hole got a cache Hit proving 'no matches'.
- **Before:** 200 read pages silently omitted matching durable records; the reproduction returned [] where [4,5,6,7] was expected. The page/cursor advanced past those records, so the reader lost them permanently with no error.
- **After:** 200 pages contain every matching durable record. load_runs is the single owner of the coverage claim: .min(target_offset), with runs clipped to that claim. publish_load merges against the entry resident at publish time and never lowers indexed_to_offset or drops resident runs. A byte-capped load returns an honest short provable_to and the reader resumes, which is the existing partial-page contract. Response shape and status are unchanged. Documented trade-off (performance only): a cold load that stops short of a chunk-only entry publishes nothing, and such reads are served by direct loads until they reach the seam.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Every product reader of records from the history partition (catch-up, replay, live resume), including SDK read()/subscribe() and consumers of peer-relayed reads. Records they used to lose silently are now delivered. Readers that already advanced past a hole before the fix do not get those records back.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - keyed_catch_up_after_a_cold_index_load_sees_later_absorbed_records (src/dst/tests/reads_history.rs)
  - cold_load_claims_only_to_its_absorbed_target (src/postings_cache/tests.rs)
  - cold_load_clips_runs_the_store_holds_past_its_target (src/postings_cache/tests.rs)
  - load_publish_joins_an_install_that_landed_mid_load (src/postings_cache/tests.rs)
  - load_publish_never_regresses_an_entry_extended_mid_load (src/postings_cache/tests.rs)
  - quality_a_cold_load_never_hides_a_later_install (src/postings_cache/tests.rs, proptest 1,024 cases)
  - publish_load_merges_into_the_resident_entry_or_publishes_nothing (src/postings_cache/tests.rs)
  - publish_load_joins_a_chunk_only_entry_only_across_a_proven_seam (src/postings_cache/tests.rs)
  - load_publish_evicts_least_recent_others_only_over_budget (src/postings_cache/tests.rs)
  - byte_capped_load_claims_through_its_last_decoded_run (src/postings_cache/tests.rs, added by 3dfb970f to kill the 2 surviving mutants)
  - clipped_to_cuts_at_the_boundary_and_keeps_whole_run_weight (src/postings/validated/tests.rs)
  - quality_clipped_runs_are_exactly_the_offsets_below_the_cut (src/postings/validated/tests.rs, proptest 1,024 cases)
- **Risk reason:** No wire change: same statuses, codes, headers and body shapes. It is a data-completeness fix inside 200 responses, and the only other effect is a documented performance trade-off. 3dfb970f adds tests only.
- **Check against commit:** None. Scope note: because product reads always carry a selector (default ""), the fix covers all product history reads, not only reads with an explicit routingKey.

### #5 84f80b75 — Billing tombstone walk covers every project in the cell

- **Program item:** bug #5
- **Surface:** product
- **Endpoint:** No request/response contract change. This is the background billing::tombstone_walk. The effect shows up in the billing rollup/invoices and in the product usage month rows for terminal incarnations: GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage?month=&streamId=, field storageByteSeconds. Internally, Registry::list_page_raw is replaced by the cell-wide Registry::reconciliation_page; the public list_page/catalog goes through the private project_page.
- **Condition:** A stream in any project OTHER than the deployment tenant becomes terminal while its usage row is CLEAN. In enforce mode that is nearly every customer stream. Terminal means deleted, expired or fork-retained. Typical cases: an idle stream expires by TTL (nothing closes at expiry), or a DELETE's close is lost to a foreign owner, a failed committer submit, or a crash after the tombstone write.
- **Before:** The walk paged only the deployment tenant's catalog prefix, so these rows were never closed. owned_frame_bytes_current stayed above 0, and the rollup carried that storage into every month close indefinitely, surviving deletion. Dirty rows were reconciled correctly in every project.
- **After:** The walk pages registry/v4/projects/ cell-wide: 256 descriptors per pass, terminal descriptors included, cursor = last consumed object key. For each terminal descriptor whose segments this instance owns, it resubmits the close at the persisted logical time. The gauge is zeroed and storage stops accruing. Log lines print the project-qualified ref. The walk fails CLOSED on any key it cannot bind (sref_of_key must round-trip through desc_path), on an undecodable descriptor, or on one over 4 MiB anywhere in the cell: it logs 'tombstone walk paused (registry list)' and does not advance. System-project streams (system/v1/cells/...) are still not walked, which is unchanged.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Customers of non-deployment projects, whose invoices and usage rows for deleted or expired streams stop carrying storage (billed less; the correction is toward truth). Billing and finance, whose totals change at month close. Operators: one unbindable object under any project now stalls reconciliation for the whole cell, where before only a bad object in the deployment tenant could.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - tombstone_walk_closes_clean_terminal_rows_of_every_project (src/dst/tests/billing_controller.rs)
  - r09_tombstone_walk_advances_one_catalog_page_per_pass (src/dst/tests/billing_controller.rs; pinned test re-pinned: expected cursor is now the descriptor key, progression 256/256/1 unchanged)
  - reconciliation_page_reaches_terminals_of_every_project_in_key_order (src/registry/catalog/tests.rs)
  - reconciliation_page_fails_closed_on_a_key_it_cannot_bind (src/registry/catalog/tests.rs)
  - a_minted_key_binds_exactly_its_own_identity (src/registry/catalog/tests.rs, proptest 1,024)
  - no_damaged_key_binds (src/registry/catalog/tests.rs, proptest 1,024)
- **Risk reason:** No request fails and no status or code changes. The only customer-visible effect is lower (corrected) billed storage for terminal incarnations. The residual risk is operational: the cell-wide walk's fail-closed pause on one bad descriptor key in any project.
- **Check against commit:** The commit matches the record. Additional effects not in the record: (1) the cell-wide fail-closed pause described above; (2) the walk cursor changed from a stream name to an object key (in memory only, so no upgrade migration); (3) the product catalog's key binding is stricter. A non-canonical key under a project prefix, for example uppercase hex, which unhex used to accept, now fails the list page ('non-canonical catalog key') instead of being listed. desc_path never mints such keys; (4) a pinned test body (r09) was re-pinned.

### #6 c19cc2f0 — Absorber never waits for pool bytes while holding some

- **Program item:** bug #8
- **Surface:** operator-debug
- **Endpoint:** No wire change. This is the internal history absorber's v2 gather: AbsorbReservation::grow is replaced by try_grow, and stage_chunk is no longer async. The only direct observable is a new info log line: 'v2 gather {prefix}: pool refused +{short} B at a {batch} B batch; deferring'.
- **Condition:** Two or more shard absorbers in one process AND ABSORB_GLOBAL_GATHERS >= 2. The binary default is 2 per RUNBOOK; the certified deploy/profiles/compute-1g.env sets 1 and is unaffected. The trigger is two concurrent gathers that each outgrow their adaptive reservation on the shared ABSORB_GLOBAL_BUDGET_BYTES pool.
- **Before:** grow() awaited acquire_many with no timeout while keeping its existing grant. Two gathers each held part of the pool and waited forever for the rest (hold-and-wait), keeping both gather slots. Every absorber in the process then parked, and nothing was logged. Absorption stalled process-wide until an engine closed. The only thing clients would eventually see is the existing unabsorbed-backlog/maintenance-backpressure behaviour on the affected shards.
- **After:** try_grow refuses without waiting. The batch is closed, the refused chunk goes to the existing deferred_budget outcome (kept pending), and no further read_wave is issued for that gather. The demand is recorded so the next tick's reserve() asks for the full size up front, and reserve() is now the only wait on the pool, made while holding no bytes. One info log per refused gather. Deferred work lands one tick later. An oversized single frame on an empty batch is covered the same way.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators running binary defaults, or any profile with ABSORB_GLOBAL_GATHERS >= 2 and several shards per process. Indirectly, clients of those processes, who no longer risk the backlog/backpressure that follows an absorber wedge.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - two_gathers_outgrowing_a_shared_pool_defer_instead_of_deadlocking (src/dst/tests/history_gather.rs)
  - a_refused_oversized_chunk_defers_and_sizes_the_next_reservation (src/dst/tests/history_gather.rs)
  - reservation_try_grow_accounts_clamps_and_never_waits (src/history.rs unit test; replaces the deleted reservation_grow_accounts_clamps_and_waits and reservation_grow_cancel_leaves_grant_intact)
  - an_oversized_chunk_gathers_alone (src/dst/tests/history_gather.rs, unchanged, still green)
  - v2_gather_packs_to_the_aggregate_budget (src/dst/tests/history_gather.rs, unchanged, still green)
- **Risk reason:** Internal only, with no wire, status or code change. It removes a process-wide deadlock, a case in which no absorption could succeed. The only behavioural cost is one tick of added latency for chunks the pool refuses.
- **Check against commit:** None. Note for the reviewer: the two old tests that pinned the in-grow wait as intentional cross-shard backpressure were deleted, not adapted. That backpressure now lives only in reserve().

### #7 5292e3e3 — Feed retry task no longer reads and publishes records

- **Program item:** bug #6
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** Product GET /v1/streams/{name}/records:sse and raw keyed GET /v1/stream/{name}?live=sse (LiveFeed sessions, src/sse/session.rs). Raw keyless SSE sessions are unchanged, because they already disconnect on any source swap (raw_keyless && generation != join_gen).
- **Condition:** The feed's single transition-retry task is armed during a split or merge: a session saw a closed source tail whose successor could not be resolved yet (RetryLater, a refresh error, or the 4-swap budget ran out). On a later 250 ms tick the successor can be installed and already holds records past the feed head. (A) One subscriber is parked at the head and has reached live. (B) After a 2->1 leave, the survivor still has an unread retained shared batch.
- **Before:** The tick called the subscriber drive (drive_once -> drive_under_permit) under the permit. It installed the successor, read its records as a Solo drive, and threw them away. (A) The solo read moved head and floor past the subscriber's cursor. The session's next take_visible returned Lagged, and because it had reached live, it ended the SSE body with no terminal control (a 'lag disconnect below feed floor', counted in FEED_LAG_DISCONNECTS). The client had to reconnect and resume from its cursor; nothing was lost durably. (B) The retained ring held the floor, so no lag fired. The survivor drained the ring and found the head already past the offsets the tick had read. Its next drive was Idle, and later drives sent only newer records, so the offsets the tick read were never sent on that connection. The cursor and nextCursor moved past them: a silent gap, with no disconnect.
- **After:** The tick only settles the tail (Tail enum, no variant that carries records). It installs the successor, or retires the feed as Closed or Gone (incarnation cutoff or incompatible topology), and never reads. When the tail is readable, the tick bumps the feed version after releasing the permit, so parked sessions wake and read those records through their own drive. On a readable but closed tail, the bump repeats on every 250 ms tick until a subscriber reads it. The result: no lag disconnect and no gap, and the session delivers the successor's records in order. Statuses, headers, frame formats, terminal controls and cutoff behaviour are unchanged. Operators see fewer sse lag disconnects and more feed version bumps.
- **Retry semantics:** No wire change. A spurious mid-stream disconnect (a forced client reconnect) and a silent skip of live records are removed. The client's resume contract is unchanged.
- **Who is affected:** SSE subscribers: SDK subscribe on records:sse, and raw keyed live=sse clients. Only collections that split or merge while a single subscriber (A) or a surviving subscriber (B) is attached. Operators watching the /v1/debug/load sse_livefeed counters.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_install_leaves_the_successor_read_to_the_singleton
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_never_skips_the_survivor_past_a_retained_ring
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_tick_on_a_readable_tail_wakes_the_session_and_reads_nothing
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_keeps_ticking_while_a_closed_tail_is_still_unread
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_keeps_ticking_until_the_transition_settles
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_cuts_the_feed_off_on_an_incompatible_successor
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::a_swap_storm_is_continued_by_the_next_attempt
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_ends_when_the_last_subscriber_leaves
  - src/sse/feed/tests/retry.rs::retry_ends_at_once_when_the_feed_is_cancelled
  - (These are feed-level unit tests only. This commit adds no end-to-end SSE wire test.)
- **Risk reason:** The surface is public, but no status, code, header or frame changes. The change removes a spurious disconnect and a silent delivery gap, and nothing that worked before now fails. The remaining risk is liveness: parked sessions now depend on the tick's version bump to wake. The wake tests cover this.
- **Check against commit:** The recorded text is correct but understates the BEFORE behaviour. Besides the solo lag-cut (scenario A), the commit's scenario B (retry_never_skips_the_survivor_past_a_retained_ring) shows a silent record skip: a survivor with a retained ring received no disconnect, and the records the tick read were never sent. That is a live-delivery gap, not just a reconnect. Also not recorded: the tick now re-bumps the feed version every 250 ms while a closed tail stays unread. This is deliberate and documented.

### #11 063a674d — Registry read that raced a write no longer re-installs what it read

- **Surface:** both
