  - src/bootstrap/tests/provider_contract.rs::in_memory_store_meets_the_slatedb_contract_under_each_commit_pipeline and ::s3lite_meets_the_slatedb_and_http_contract_under_each_commit_pipeline (new; the writer flushes as the pump does, red-first without that flush: `nothing flushed the WAL within 10s of the second write (pump: true)`; under both pipelines a fenced writer's write is `Fenced`, a lost WAL reply without put-id metadata is `Fenced`, both batches are durable after a reopen, and the eleven armed HTTP faults fire)
  - Not pinned by any test, and asserted by no rig: that the three values reach the engine. `bootstrap::run` cannot be called by a test and is a frozen scope. Pinned since the cleanup of 2026-09-30: `bootstrap::tests::the_parsed_shard_settings_reach_every_shard_engine` reads the three values (pump on, 10 ms gap, 6 ms gather) from `shard_config`, the function `run` now takes every engine's configuration from. The rigs that start the binary read no pump counter and no log line: none of them requests `GET /v1/debug/timings` (the scripts that do are `bench/costab/run-soak.sh` and `run-wide.sh`, which set all three names themselves, and `scripts/bench-fra-ab.sh`, which selects the tick), and none reads the shard's start line `WAL group-commit pump on`, which carries `gap_ms` and `gather_ms`. A rig's timing budget shows only that a pump runs, because without one every append would wait for the 1 s failsafe. The rigs that pass `--wal-flush-gap-ms 2` never run the default gap; of the rigs named in the risk reason only the SDK smoke starts the binary with none of the three names, and `sdk/smoke.mjs` requests no debug endpoint. The gap and the gather of a running server can be read by hand, in that start line and in the `pump` block of `/v1/debug/timings`. The rigs were not run for this record.
- **Risk reason:** low: timing and the object-store request count only. No request that succeeded fails, and none that failed succeeds. The costs are the ones measured: about 2.2 times the WAL writes at the measured load and 6 to 9% more CPU per unit at saturation, for a lower acknowledgement latency and a higher rate with clients that wait for each acknowledgement. The default gap of 10 ms permits more WAL objects per second than the 25 ms that the `FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS` text argues for against WAL garbage collection (5 ms outran it); 10 ms is what the soak and fleet deployments run, and the pump does not flush an idle shard. The switch stays, so the tick is one setting away. `ShardConfig::default` (src/shard.rs), which only tests read, is not changed and stays tick mode. Not run for this record: the conformance suite, the field gate, the platform e2e, the LiveFeed certification, the SDK smoke and the noisy-neighbour campaign under the new default; their timing thresholds are the open risk. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change; amended on 2026-09-30, after its ratification, with the field measurement under "Measured" (the record had said no field measurement on Tigris existed). An independent review of 2026-09-29 corrected this record in three places: "Who is affected" gains the six `bench/sse-probes` scripts and says why `bench/livefeed-perf/run-one.sh` is not changed; "After" states the 1 ms herd-settle, which makes the spacing of two flushes 11 ms and not 10; and the pinning note said that the rigs show the three values through the `pump` counters, which no rig reads. Corrected with it, outside this record: the help of `--wal-flush-gap-ms` ("churn ceiling unchanged") and of `--wal-post-ack-gather-ms` ("never delays an idle shard's first write"), SPEC.md D22 and its write path, which still described the 25 ms tick as what the binary runs, and the comment of CI's conformance step, which stated as fact that the 2 ms gap keeps the suite inside its budget (CONFORMANCE.md says that this has not been measured). Left as it is: the same two phrases in the field comments of `ShardConfig` (`src/shard.rs` has no line headroom and feeds six formal receipts). For the owner: an assertion on the start line or on `pump.flushes` in CI's SDK smoke step would pin the default end to end; it changes what a CI job checks, so it was not added.

### #78 683c1bc3 — A server that does not set ABSORB_AGE_SECS absorbs a stream's tail once it is 60 seconds old

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 3 of `config-simplification.md` (the table "Also in this package", first row). Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this change; it was made on that delegation. Performance acceptance stays with the owner (AGENTS.md §1).
- **Surface:** process (operator-debug for the absorber's gauges: `absorb_backlog` and the per-stream `absorb_lag_secs` of `GET /v1/debug/usage`, `absorb_lag_max_secs` of `GET /v1/debug/load` and of the fleet heartbeat; the object store's request count is a cost the operator sees)
- **Endpoint:** None on the wire. The startup setting `ABSORB_AGE_SECS` (`--absorb-age-secs`), which `bootstrap::absorber_config` gives the absorber of every shard as `AbsorberConfig::threshold_age`. A stream's unabsorbed tail is due for absorption into the history tier when it holds `ABSORB_BYTES` (4 MiB, unchanged) or when its oldest unabsorbed write is this old, whichever comes first (`due_streams`, `src/history.rs`). The absorber looks every 5 s.
- **Condition:** The process sets neither the variable nor the argument, and either a stream's unabsorbed tail stays below 4 MiB for longer than 60 s (a sparse or slow stream), or the absorber seeds a stream from the durable dirty index: when a shard opens, on the rescan every 120 ticks (about 10 minutes), or while a scan continues, and finds a stream with unabsorbed records that is not already pending. A tail that reaches 4 MiB sooner is absorbed by size, as before.
- **Before:** Default 300. A tail below 4 MiB stayed in the shard's log until its oldest unabsorbed write was 300 s old. The lag gauges of such a stream rose to about 300 s before each absorption.
- **After:** Default 60. The same tail is due at 60 s. A read does not show in which tier a record is stored, so no status, header, body or error code changes. What changes is when the data moves, and how often: a stream that receives little but steadily is absorbed up to five times as often, each absorption a history commit and its objects. The lag gauges of such a stream now rise to about 60 s. The former behaviour is one setting: `ABSORB_AGE_SECS=300`.
  - Seeded work. A stream seeded from the dirty index is backdated by the threshold (`seed_from_dirty_index`, `src/history/gather.rs`). The tick takes its time before it seeds, so by age the stream is due on the absorber's next tick, 5 s later, under either value (at once only if it is due by size). The backdating also sets the gauges, whatever the real age of the stream's bytes: the tick publishes the age of every pending stream before it gathers (`src/history/worker.rs`), so a seeded stream is published at the threshold itself, 60 s where it was 300 s on the seeding tick and 65 s where it was 305 s on the next, until a gather settles it. The gauges are `absorb_lag_secs`, `absorb_backlog.max_secs` and `oldest_eligible_secs` of `/v1/debug/usage`, and `absorb_lag_max_secs` of `/v1/debug/load` and of the heartbeat.
  - The quota tracker. Read from the code, not measured: when a stream comes due, the absorber touches its handle, and the handle is evicted `HANDLE_IDLE_EVICT_SECS` (600) after its last touch; while the handle is resident its binding holds its project's entry in the quota tracker, which never evicts an entry a holder still has. On a server that sets neither name a small last append therefore holds the entry for at least about 665 s (60 + 600 + 5) where it held it for about 905 s. The tracker has 32,768 entries and refuses a project it cannot track with 503 `project_tracker_capacity`; the estimated rate of first-seen appending projects at which it is full rises from about 36 to about 49 per second (`docs/reviews/2026-09-hardening/plans/plans18/tracker-live-eviction.md`). The direction is fewer refusals. A deployment that sets 60 is unchanged. The comment above `MAX_TRACKED_PROJECTS` in `src/quota.rs` stated the 905 s figures as the defaults' and now states both.
- **Not measured:** 60 is the value the deployments run. It is not a measured improvement. The one measurement that names the setting (`bench/WORKLOAD-CERT-PLAN.md`, rung L1d4: 5.18% of requests shed at 300 s against 1.56%) is one run that changed two variables, the age and the pass size (`PASS 16 MB`); the same document reports 0.85% and 1.56% for two runs of the better posture and says of another single run, at 5.38%, that it cannot be distinguished from harm at N=1. The cost side, more history commits and objects for sparse streams, has not been measured at all.
- **In a fleet:** the rebalancer reads the age of the oldest unabsorbed bytes (`absorb_lag_max_secs`) and moves a shard when it stays above `REBALANCE_LAG_SECS` (60 by default) for two fleet ticks (`src/fleet.rs`). With the old default a sparse stream held that gauge between 60 and 300 s for minutes at a time on a fleet member that set neither name; with 60 the gauge reaches the threshold about when the tail is absorbed. This is read from the code. It was not run, and whether the gauge of a sparse stream stays above 60 for two ticks under the new default is not determined. Seeded streams: on a member that sets neither name, streams seeded at a shard open or a rescan held the gauge at 300 s and then 305 s for at least one absorber tick (5 s, more than two fleet ticks of 2 s), above `REBALANCE_LAG_SECS` from the first tick; with 60 the gauge reads 60 on the seeding tick, which is not above the threshold (the test is `my_lag > rebalance_lag_secs`), and 65 from the next tick until a gather settles the stream. Also read from the code, not run. The fleet families set both names to 60, so the fleets that ran are this pair, and the LiveFeed rigs set `REBALANCE_LAG_SECS=1000000`. One fleet in the repository runs the pair of defaults: `bench/docker/compose.yml`, the three-instance docker ladder, sets neither name and moves from 300/60 to 60/60; its D3 rung (`compose.d3.yml`) pauses absorption on one instance so that the rebalancer moves a shard on absorb lag.
- **Retry semantics:** None (no client surface).
- **Who is affected:** A deployment that does not set the name. Eight of the nine families that set it set 60 and are unchanged (`region-server`, `region-server-scale`, `fleet-server-1`, `fleet-server-n`, `mt-tenants-off`, `mt-tenants-enforce`, `wc-ladder`, `wc-ladder-diet`; the audit's table counted seven of eight), as are `bench/docker/harness/cluster-deploy.sh` and the `bench/costab` and `bench/fleet/local-fanout.sh` scripts, which set 15, 30 or 60. `scripts/bench-fra-ab.sh` and its family set 300 themselves and keep it. Changed: a bare binary (the `defaults` family; the SDK smoke of CI), and the rigs that start the binary without the name: conformance, CI's product field gate, `scripts/platform-e2e.mjs` and its negative twin, the LiveFeed canary and certification (`platform-e2e` and `livefeed-canary` families); `scripts/mt-noisy-campaign.mjs`; `bench/docker/compose.yml` (see "In a fleet"); and the six `bench/sse-probes` scripts, which start the binary with `--listen` alone. A rig sees the difference only if it runs a stream for longer than 60 s without filling 4 MiB. The noisy-neighbour campaign does at the scale of its contract: it has locked thresholds (victim p99 under load at most the larger of twice the solo p99 and 25 ms), and in `scripts/promote-rc.sh` it runs with its default `WINDOW_SECS=30`, a solo window of 30 s, 48 creates and a loaded window of 30 s, so the victim's tail, first written when the solo window opens, turns 60 s old near the end of the loaded window: an age-triggered absorption can fall in the loaded window and cannot fall in the solo window it is compared with. Under 300 it could fall in neither. The nightly CI run (`WINDOW_SECS=20`, two windows of 20 s and 24 creates) ends before the tail is 60 s old. Not changed: `bench/livefeed-perf/run-one.sh`, which runs the pinned arm binaries of 1834b726 and 3a8016e6, whose compiled default is 300. No deployment script or family is edited for this record: `docs/STAGING.md` already prescribes 60. RUNBOOK.md states the new default since this commit; SPEC.md (D23 and §3.6, the two places the help of the setting cites) said "~5 min" until the correction that followed the review of 2026-09-29. Whether a Compute project lacks the name is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11), which has not been run; on Compute the environment is merged per project (RUNBOOK §7.3), so a project that once set the name keeps its value.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned (the default; red-first on the unchanged code: left `("absorb-age-secs", "ABSORB_AGE_SECS", "300")`, right `"60"`)
  - src/bootstrap/tests.rs::a_server_that_sets_nothing_absorbs_by_age_after_sixty_seconds (new, red-first: left `(300s, 4194304)`, right `(60s, 4194304)`; it calls `absorber_config`, the boundary `bootstrap::run` calls, with the configuration of a server that sets nothing)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_fixture_matches_scrubbed_parse (the fixture equals a parse in a cleared environment)
  - src/bootstrap/tests.rs::active_absorber_options_reach_the_absorber_configuration (unchanged: a value that is set reaches the absorber)
  - Not pinned: the absorption itself at 60 s. No test other than the two above reads this default: a DST builds its own `AbsorberConfig` (1 ms where it sets the age, 1,000,000 s for a cold absorber) or takes `AbsorberConfig::default` in `src/history.rs`, which only tests read because `absorber_config` always sets the field. That second default still says 300: the file has no line headroom and an edit stales three receipts (TLA-016, TLA-018, TLA-019).
- **Risk reason:** low: no answer on the wire changes, and no request that succeeded fails. The change moves data to the history tier sooner and, for sparse streams, more often. The risks are the unmeasured ones: the object-store cost of up to five times as many age-triggered absorptions on a deployment with many sparse streams, and the load those absorptions add where the old default spread them over 300 s. Against them stands that the soak, fleet, multi-tenant and workload-ladder deployments ran 60. Not run for this record: the rigs that start the binary, the noisy-neighbour campaign at its default `WINDOW_SECS=30` (as `scripts/promote-rc.sh` runs it) and the docker ladder among them. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent review of 2026-09-29 corrected this record in four places: "Condition" and "After" gain the streams seeded from the dirty index, which are published at the threshold itself (the record said only that recovered work is due at once; by age it is due on the next tick); "After" gains the shorter hold of a stream's handle and of its project's tracker entry, which the record did not name among the effects of the value; "In a fleet" gains the seeded streams and `bench/docker/compose.yml`, a fleet in the repository that runs the pair of defaults; "Who is affected" gains `scripts/mt-noisy-campaign.mjs`, `bench/docker/compose.yml` and the `bench/sse-probes` scripts, says why `bench/livefeed-perf/run-one.sh` is not changed, and no longer says that no document is edited. Corrected with it, outside this record: SPEC.md D23 and §3.6, which still gave the age as about 5 minutes, and the comment above `MAX_TRACKED_PROJECTS` in `src/quota.rs`. Not mentioned by the record and not added: the rebalancer's choice of a move target (lag below half the threshold) and its return-home rule (lag 0) read the same gauge.

### #80 180e348c — Fourteen settings that nothing sets are constants: their arguments are refused and their environment names are ignored

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 4 of `config-simplification.md` (settings nothing sets become constants). Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this package; the change was made on that delegation. Of the package's twenty-four names, fourteen are done here. `ABSORB_READ_PAR` stays a setting on purpose (it is the memory lever of a gather and the lever the documents name for the append-latency dip during a gather); the other nine were not attempted. The plan recommended constants for twelve of the twenty-four, "the GC intervals, the gather skips, the per-key cap and the buckets", and called the others the owner's taste. Two of the fourteen done here, `TAIL_MAX_BYTES` and `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF`, are among those others, as are `ABSORB_READ_PAR` and the nine not attempted; they were retired on the delegation, and they are the two whose effect a client can observe.
- **Surface:** both (recorded as process until the review of 2026-09-29; the refused arguments and the ignored names are process). Two of the fourteen names changed what a client could observe on a deployment that set them, and are recorded here instead of in a record of their own because no deployment is known to set them: `TAIL_MAX_BYTES` (raw and product reads: the size of a page) and `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF` (every HTTP/1.1 request: the size of the request head hyper accepts).
- **Endpoint:** None on the wire for a deployment that set neither `TAIL_MAX_BYTES` nor `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF`; for one that did, the page of a read woken by a wait (raw and product) and the request head of every HTTP/1.1 request (see "After"). Process startup: the command-line arguments `--wal-gc-interval-secs`, `--wal-gc-min-age-secs`, `--compactions-gc-interval-secs`, `--compactions-gc-min-age-secs`, `--gc-quiet-interval-secs`, `--l0-max-ssts-per-key`, `--wal-gather-skip-reqs`, `--wal-gather-skip-bytes`, `--ops-bucket`, `--shard-bucket`, `--data-bucket`; the environment names `WAL_GC_INTERVAL_SECS`, `WAL_GC_MIN_AGE_SECS`, `COMPACTIONS_GC_INTERVAL_SECS`, `COMPACTIONS_GC_MIN_AGE_SECS`, `GC_QUIET_INTERVAL_SECS`, `HISTORY_GC_INTERVAL_SECS`, `L0_MAX_SSTS_PER_KEY`, `WAL_GATHER_SKIP_REQS`, `WAL_GATHER_SKIP_BYTES`, `TAIL_MAX_BYTES`, `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF`; `--help`; the text of one validation error.
- **Condition:** A process passes one of the eleven arguments or holds one of the eleven environment names.
- **Before:** Each value applied, without validation.
  - GC of the shard databases: the WAL sweep ran every `WAL_GC_INTERVAL_SECS` (30) and deleted WAL SSTs older than `WAL_GC_MIN_AGE_SECS` (60); the compactions-log sweep ran every `COMPACTIONS_GC_INTERVAL_SECS` (30) with the floor `COMPACTIONS_GC_MIN_AGE_SECS` (120); the manifest, compacted and WAL-fence sweeps ran every `GC_QUIET_INTERVAL_SECS` (600). A minimum age of 0 was accepted, and `GC_QUIET_INTERVAL_SECS=0` stopped the three quiet sweeps. `HISTORY_GC_INTERVAL_SECS` (600) set the sweep of every history database, and 0 stopped it.
  - `L0_MAX_SSTS_PER_KEY`: a value other than 0 set SlateDB's per-key L0 cap independently of `L0_MAX_SSTS`; 0, the default, followed it.
  - `WAL_GATHER_SKIP_REQS` (32) and `WAL_GATHER_SKIP_BYTES` (1 MiB): the post-acknowledgement gather was skipped when the next WAL already held that much; 0 meant never skip. They matter only under the group-commit pump with a gather window.
  - `--ops-bucket`, `--shard-bucket`, `--data-bucket` (no environment names): that role's store used the named bucket, with the one credential pair the server holds.
  - `TAIL_MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB; 0 or a value that does not parse kept the default): a read woken by a long-poll wait returned a page of up to that many bytes.
  - `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF` (64 KiB): the read buffer of every HTTP/1.1 connection, which is also the largest request head hyper accepts. A value below 8,192 refused the start with `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF=<n> is below hyper's 8192-byte h1 buffer floor` (#32).
- **After:**
  - The eleven arguments are not declared: a process that passes one does not start (clap `UnknownArgument`, exit status 2, before any side effect). `--help` loses their entries; the entry of `--l0-max-ssts` says that it is also the per-key cap, and the entry of `--compactor-poll-ms` loses six lines about WAL GC that were attached to it by mistake.
  - The eleven environment names are not read. A process that holds one starts as a process that holds none, whatever the value; nothing is logged.
  - The server runs the former defaults: WAL GC 30 s / 60 s, compactions-log GC 30 s / 120 s, the quiet sweeps and the history sweep 600 s (constants of `EngineConfig`, and `HistoryConfig`'s default), the per-key L0 cap equal to `L0_MAX_SSTS`, gather skips of 32 requests and 1 MiB, one bucket (`--bucket` / `SLATE_S3_BUCKET`) for every role, a 1 MiB page for a woken read (bulk reads keep 8 MiB), a 64 KiB h1 read buffer.
  - A process that holds `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF` below 8,192 starts, because the name is not read; #32's refusal can no longer be caused from outside. The check stays for a configuration built in code, with the text `the h1 read buffer of <n> bytes is below hyper's 8192-byte floor`.
  - For a deployment that had set `TAIL_MAX_BYTES`: a client that follows the returned offset reads the same records in pages of another size. For a deployment that had raised `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF` above 65,536: a request head larger than 64 KiB, which was accepted, is refused by hyper before any handler runs. The path of #33 (a `Stream-Seq` over 65,535 bytes) is then reachable only by a head that fits 64 KiB.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators. No profile, family, rig or deploy script in the repository sets one of the names to a value other than the constant: `scripts/bench-fra-ab.sh` and its family set `L0_MAX_SSTS_PER_KEY=0` (follow) and `bench/sse-probes/sse-1per.sh` set `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF` to 65,536 by default, with a lever `H1BUF` that would now do nothing; both lines are removed. On Compute the supervisor passes only `--listen`, so no Compute deployment passes one of the arguments. A Compute project keeps every name it was ever given: whether a project holds one of the eleven names at another value is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11), which has not been run. Such a project changes behaviour on its next deploy without a message, for example `GC_QUIET_INTERVAL_SECS=0` starts sweeping. A deployment outside the repository that split its roles over buckets must merge them before it can start this binary; none is known, and `docs/STAGING.md`, which planned three buckets, now plans one.
- **Given up:** the levers that needed no rebuild: stopping the quiet or the history sweeps, a longer WAL retention (OPERATIONS.md specifies a 24 h WAL floor for a backup feature that does not exist in the source; when it is built the floor is a code change), a per-key cap below the L0 cap, a larger h1 buffer, and a bucket per role. The last is a decision of the specification: SPEC.md D6 decided three shared buckets, one per role, and §3.1 laid the storage out over them; with the three arguments gone no configuration reaches that layout, and SPEC.md (D6, §3.1 and §8) now says one bucket, for the owner to confirm or to revise at ratification (the alternative the audit named is to keep the three settings and give them environment names). A rollback to an older binary on the same project reads the names again.
- **Deployment comparison:** two controls of `scripts/effective-config` change, for the owner to see. The pin of HEAD's leaves (K9) moves from 149 to 143: six `cli.*` fields are gone and declared as removed in `rename-map.json`; the five fields that stay for `bootstrap::run` (`wal_gather_skip_reqs`, `wal_gather_skip_bytes` and the three buckets) still print. Control K6, which reproduced a refusal an environment name causes, used `SSE_H1_MAX_BUF=4096`; it now uses `INITIAL_SHARDS=3` and the text `INITIAL_SHARDS=3 must be a power of two (the rendezvous shard layout derives its bit-width from it)`, taken from the built dumper. The pins measured on rc.4 (`PIN_ENV_KNOBS`, `PIN_RC4_NAMES`, `PIN_HELPERS`, `PIN_ARGS_LINES`) are unchanged. The tool itself was not run (it builds rc.4 and HEAD); its unit tests and `check-families` were.

### #81 b4509415 — A gather never parks between read waves: ABSORB_PACE_MS and ABSORB_PACE_WINDOW_MS are gone with the pacing code

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 5 of `config-simplification.md` (switches with one live path), its first row. Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this package; the removal was made on that delegation. The other four rows of the package (`STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT`, `SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY`, `HISTORY_COMPACTOR`, `BILLING_METER`) are not part of this record.
- **Surface:** process
- **Endpoint:** None on the wire. Process startup: the command-line arguments `--absorb-pace-ms` and `--absorb-pace-window-ms`; the environment names `ABSORB_PACE_MS` and `ABSORB_PACE_WINDOW_MS`; `--help`.
- **Condition:** A process passes one of the two arguments or holds one of the two environment names.
- **Before:** Both were parsed (`ABSORB_PACE_MS` default 0, `ABSORB_PACE_WINDOW_MS` default 50). With `ABSORB_PACE_MS` above 0, the shared-history gather slept that many milliseconds after a wave of frame reads whenever at least the window had passed since its last sleep (a window of 0: after every wave), and reported the total as `gather_last_pace_ms` on GET /v1/debug/load and in the `_ops_metrics` gauges and as `absorber.lastPaceMs` on GET /v1/debug/absorb. With the default of 0 it never slept and reported 0. The source recorded that the pacing had been measured harmful (L1d8: stretching the read phase amplified append shed) and that the setting was kept for field experiments only.
- **After:**
  - The two arguments are not declared: a process that passes one does not start (clap `UnknownArgument`, exit status 2, before any side effect). `--help` loses the two entries.
  - The two environment names are not read. A process that holds one starts as a process that holds none, whatever the value; nothing is logged.
  - A gather issues its read waves back to back, as every process with `ABSORB_PACE_MS=0` did. What is absorbed, the batch budget (`ABSORB_GATHER_MAX_BYTES`), the read concurrency (`ABSORB_READ_PAR`) and the commit are unchanged.
  - `gather_last_pace_ms` (both places) and `absorber.lastPaceMs` are still reported and are always 0: nothing writes the counter. They are not removed here because `collect_snapshot` in `src/ops.rs`, one of the three reporters, is frozen by an exception row that only the owner updates.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators. No profile, family, rig or deploy script in the repository sets `ABSORB_PACE_MS` above 0: `bench/soak/wc-ladder.sh` passed both names at their defaults, with the levers `WC_PACE_MS` and `WC_PACE_WINDOW` to re-run the pacing experiment; the line and the levers are removed, as are the lines of the two `wc-ladder` families. On Compute the supervisor passes only `--listen`, so no Compute deployment passes one of the arguments. A Compute project keeps every name it was ever given: a project that still holds `ABSORB_PACE_MS` above 0 from the L1d experiments stops pacing on its next deploy without a message. Whether one exists is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11), which has not been run. The direction is the one the measurement recommends.
- **Given up:** the ability to re-run the pacing experiment without a source change. The L1d8 measurement itself is not in the repository's documents (`bench/WORKLOAD-CERT-PLAN.md` has no row for it); what remains is the statement in the doc comment of `ReadPlan` in `src/history/gather.rs` and the comment above the counter in `src/history.rs` (since #86 removed the counter and its comment on 2026-09-30, the `ReadPlan` doc comment is the one place that states it).
- **Deployment comparison:** the pin of HEAD's leaves in `scripts/effective-config` (K9) moves from 143 to 141; `cli.absorb_pace_ms` and `cli.absorb_pace_window_ms` are declared as removed in `rename-map.json`, with proposals in `proposed.json`. The tool itself was not run (it builds rc.4 and HEAD); its unit tests and `check-families` were.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned (red-first: `("absorb-pace-ms", "ABSORB_PACE_MS", "0")` and `("absorb-pace-window-ms", "ABSORB_PACE_WINDOW_MS", "50")` in left only)
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::a_retired_flag_on_argv_is_refused (the table gains the two arguments; red-first: `("--absorb-pace-ms", Ok(()))`, `("--absorb-pace-window-ms", Ok(()))` on the left)
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::a_retired_name_in_the_environment_changes_nothing (added after the review of 2026-09-29: the child process, whose environment is cleared, also holds `ABSORB_PACE_MS=abc` and `ABSORB_PACE_WINDOW_MS=abc`, a value clap refused while it parsed the names, and its parse succeeds. It has no red on this commit, where the names are already unread. Control, a temporary edit that was reverted: with the two arguments declared as the parent of this commit declared them, the helper fails with `called `Result::unwrap()` on an `Err` value: ErrorInner { kind: ValueValidation, ... [String("--absorb-pace-window-ms <ABSORB_PACE_WINDOW_MS>"), String("abc")] ... source: Some(ParseIntError { kind: InvalidDigit })`)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_fixture_matches_scrubbed_parse
  - src/bootstrap/tests.rs::active_absorber_options_reach_the_absorber_configuration (loses its two pacing fields; still holds the threshold, the age, the batch budget and the read concurrency)
  - unchanged in what it asserts and green: src/dst/tests/history_gather.rs::gather_parallel_reads_preserve_outcomes_across_reopen (a serial and a parallel gather settle the same 96 streams to the same boundaries), with the rest of `dst::dst_tests::history_gather::`
  - removed: src/dst/tests/history_gather.rs::gather_pacing_preserves_outcomes_and_opens_windows and its helper `gather_with_pacing`. It pinned that a configured park happens and changes no outcome; no configuration can express a park any more.
  - Not pinned: that a gather never sleeps between waves. The sleep and the two fields that enabled it are deleted, so no test can configure the old behaviour; there is no timing assertion.
- **Residue:** the static `GATHER_LAST_PACE_MS` in `src/history.rs` and its three reporters (`src/ops.rs` `collect_snapshot`, `src/http.rs` /v1/debug/load and /v1/debug/absorb) stay and report 0 until the owner rewrites the exception row of `collect_snapshot` (`docs/quality/exception-growth.json`, `syntax_facts: 466`); removing them is a second edge change (operator-debug: two fields and a gauge disappear; WIRE-MATRIX lists both routes). The comment in `src/http.rs` above `gather_last_read_ms` still says that the endpoint shows whether pacing fired; it goes with the fields. Removed on 2026-09-30 on the owner's instruction ("Please do the cleanup"): the static, the gauge and the two fields are gone, the comment says the endpoint reports the read phase of the last gather, the row of `collect_snapshot` was updated (`syntax_facts` 466 -> 457), and the removal is edge record #86 (operator-debug, low); the owner RATIFIED it on 2026-09-30.
- **Risk reason:** low: no answer on the wire changes, and no deployment in the repository paced. The risks are a start that is refused for a process that still passes one of the two arguments, and a project that silently stops pacing. Not run for this record: the rigs that start the binary, the effective-configuration comparison, Miri and the mutation leg of the `bootstrap` owner (both selected by CI for this change). WIRE-MATRIX has no entry (no status, code, header or body changes; the debug fields keep their names and types). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent review of 2026-09-29 found no false statement in this record and one gap that the record had disclosed: the two environment names had no test of their own. They are now in the table of the child process of `a_retired_name_in_the_environment_changes_nothing`, and the "Not pinned" note lost its sentence about them. The header of `src/config/retired_tests.rs` named the records #80, #82, #83, #84 and #85 and now names #80 to #85.

### #82 3742d161 — Store operations are not capped by count: STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT is not read and its semaphore is gone

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 5 of `config-simplification.md` (switches with one live path), its second row. Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this package; the removal was made on that delegation. The audit of the setting (`evidence/config-audit-2026-09-29/detail.md`, `STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT`) put two questions to the owner; both are answered here by the implementer and are listed under "For the owner" below.
- **Surface:** process
- **Endpoint:** None on the wire. Process startup: the environment name `STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT` (it never had a command-line argument, so `--help` does not change); the key `storage.store_max_concurrent` of the startup log line "effective configuration (redacted)".
- **Condition:** A process holds `STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT` at a value other than 0.
- **Before:** The name was read from the environment (default 0 = no cap). With a value N above 0 every runtime owned one semaphore of N permits, shared by all its stores, and six kinds of store call took one permit for the duration of the call: a put, the start of a multipart upload, a get or head (until the response, not while the body streamed), a ranged get, a delimited list and a copy. The parts of a multipart upload, the streaming list and deletes were never counted. A call of the sst class took its bytes from the byte gate first and its permit second. With the default of 0 no semaphore existed and no call waited. The startup summary printed the value.
- **After:**
  - The environment name is not read. A process that holds it starts as a process that does not, whatever the value; nothing is logged.
  - No store call waits for a count permit. Transfers of the sst class (flush and compaction output, and their reads) are bounded in bytes by the byte gate as before (`STORE_BULK_INFLIGHT_MAX_BYTES`, default 32 MiB); calls of every other class (WAL, manifest, fleet and the rest) wait for nothing, as they did with the default.
  - The startup summary has no key `storage.store_max_concurrent`.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators. Nothing in the repository sets the name: no profile, family, rig or deploy script (`git grep`; the mentions outside `src` were RUNBOOK §3.3, the example in `docs/PROVIDER-CONTRACT.md` and the dated experiment records). On Compute a project keeps every name it was ever given: a project that still holds `STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT=48` from the v18 experiment of July (EXPERIMENT-PILOT.md, run 12b) has the cap today and loses it on its next deploy without a message. Whether one exists is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11). A parser of the startup summary loses one key.
- **Given up:** the ability to cap the number of concurrent store calls without a source change. Run 12b measured the cap as a negative result (the gauge was capped as designed and the excursions did not improve), and RUNBOOK §3.3 said to leave it off; the record of that run stays in EXPERIMENT-PILOT.md and REPORT.md.
- **Deployment comparison:** the pin of HEAD's leaves in `scripts/effective-config` (K9) moves from 141 to 140; `storage.store_max_concurrent` is declared as removed in `rename-map.json`, with a proposal in `proposed.json`. No family set the name. The tool itself was not run (it builds rc.4 and HEAD); its unit tests and `check-families` were.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::retired_environment_names_change_nothing (new; red-first: `assertion `left == right` failed: STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT`, the two configurations differing in `store_max_concurrent: 48` against `store_max_concurrent: 0`)
  - src/config/tests.rs::default_values_are_pinned (loses the assertion on the removed field)
  - src/store_timing/resources.rs::runtime_store_concurrency_is_shared_locally_and_independent_of_first_access (rewritten, same name; see below)
  - unchanged, byte for byte: src/store_timing/resources.rs::runtime_bulk_capacity_cancellation_and_exempt_paths_keep_their_own_owner (sha256 9e2d6f42...) and the byte-gate tests of `src/store_timing/tests.rs`
  - Not pinned: that no store call waits for a count permit. The semaphore, its accessor and its six call sites are deleted, so no configuration can express the cap and no test can set it.
- **A pinned mechanism test changed its subject:** `runtime_store_concurrency_is_shared_locally_and_independent_of_first_access` is evidence for review obligation R10 (mechanism `runtime-journals-caches-store-budgets` in `docs/refactor/review-mechanisms.json`). It exercised the count semaphore, which no longer exists. It is rewritten on the byte gate under the same name and its sha256 is re-pinned (93fd22b8... to 784e980a...); the number of mechanism tests and their names do not change. It holds what its sibling does not: two runtimes built through `RuntimeCaps::production(..).with_config(..)` with byte caps of 1 and 2; two stores of one runtime share one `StoreResources` (`Arc::ptr_eq`); a byte held in runtime a makes a's sst put wait on its first poll while the same put in runtime b completes (in flight 1 and 1, waits 1 and 0); after the holds are dropped the waiting put completes and both gates are back at their caps (in flight 0, permits 1 and 2); the resources retire with their owners. The rewritten test is green on the code before the removal as well, because the byte gate is not changed.
- **For the owner:**
  1. The rewrite and the re-pin of the R10 mechanism test, which the audit listed as the owner's question. The alternative is to remove the test from `review-mechanisms.json`, which changes what the review-evidence check counts.
  2. Whether a live Compute project still holds `STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT` above 0; the platform export answers it before the binary is deployed.
- **Risk reason:** low: no answer on the wire changes, the default was no cap, and no deployment in the repository set one. The risk is a project that silently loses a cap it still holds; the only measurement of the cap found that it did not help. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the library suite, the release profile, the rigs that start the binary and the effective-configuration comparison. CI selects neither a mutation leg nor Miri nor a formal obligation for the files of this change. WIRE-MATRIX has no entry (no route, status, code, header or body changes). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent review of 2026-09-29 found nothing to correct in the text of this record. It corrected one document the commit had left: `docs/runtime-resources.md`, a live reference, still listed "store-I/O concurrency and bulk gates" as owned by the runtime, and the count gate no longer exists; the document did not contain the name `STORE_MAX_CONCURRENT`, so the list of mentions under "Who is affected", which a search for the name produced, holds. It also corrected the header of `src/config/retired_tests.rs`, which this commit had rewritten to name the records #80 and #82 and which left out #81.

### #83 c5791119 — The fleet's desired count has no assumed-capacity dimension: SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY is not an option

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 5 of `config-simplification.md` (switches with one live path), its third row. Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this package; the removal was made on that delegation. The audit of the setting (`evidence/config-audit-2026-09-29/detail.md`, `SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY`) put two questions to the owner; both are answered here by the implementer and are listed under "For the owner" below.
- **Surface:** process
- **Endpoint:** No product, raw or fleet-internal route. Process startup: the command-line argument `--scale-rps-capacity`, the environment name `SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY`, `--help`. In a fleet (`FLEET_PREFIX` set): the string `reason` of the fleet document `fleet/desired.json`, which the log line "fleet desired A -> B (reason)" repeats. Operator: `GET /operator/data.json` (bearer-gated) returns that document as it is stored, as `fleet.desired`, so its member `reason` carries the changed string; `GET /operator/runbook` serves the RUNBOOK.md that is compiled into the binary, whose §3.5 loses the row of the setting.
- **Condition:** A process passes the argument or holds the environment name; or a fleet instance publishes a desired count.
- **Before:** The option was parsed (default 0 = dimension off). With a value C above 0 the fleet controller computed `need_rps = ceil(total rps / max(1, target utilisation x C))` from the rates the live heartbeats report, and the desired count was at least that; the shrink target had the same floor, so the fleet did not shrink below it. With the default of 0 `need_rps` was 0 and took part in neither maximum. The reason string ended `rps=R (need_rps) live=L`, for example `rps=0 (0) live=2`.
- **After:**
  - The argument is not declared: a process that passes it does not start (clap `UnknownArgument`, exit status 2, before any side effect). `--help` loses the entry.
  - The environment name is not read. A process that holds it starts as a process that does not, whatever the value; nothing is logged.
  - The desired count is the maximum over utilisation, edge slots, the hot instance, ack latency and edge latency, clamped to `FLEET_MAX` and floored by `FLEET_MIN`, as it was for every process with the default. The measured rate is still summed: it gates the edge-latency dimension (at least 5 requests per second) and is printed.
  - The reason string ends `rps=R live=L`: the token `(need_rps)` between the two is gone. Every other token keeps its place and form.
  - Unchanged, and now a constant: the boot line "fleet coordination on (prefix=P, cap=0 rps)" still prints `cap=0 rps`. The startup summary never held the value (it excludes the clap-owned settings). `bootstrap::run` names the field in that line, and the function is frozen by its exception rows, so the field stays in `CliArgs` with `#[arg(skip)]`.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators of a fleet. Nothing in the repository sets the name: no profile, family, rig, test or deploy script (`git grep`; the mentions outside `src` were the RUNBOOK §3.5 row, which said to leave it off, COMPUTE-SPEC §4 and the dated experiment records). On Compute the supervisor passes only `--listen`, so no Compute deployment passes the argument. A Compute project keeps every name it was ever given: a project that still holds `SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY=150` from pilot run 5 has the dimension today and loses it on its next deploy without a message; it then scales on the five measured dimensions only. That run is the incident that retired the dimension (it scaled out at about 5% utilisation on a constant that described a slower engine). Whether such a project exists is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11), which has not been run. A reader of the reason string that splits it by position finds one token fewer at the end. In the repository `bench/fleet/observe-fleet.py` reads the string and copies it verbatim; the server itself relays the whole document, `reason` included, in `/operator/data.json` (`operator_snapshot`, `src/fleet/repository.rs`; `src/operator.rs`), and the compiled-in operator page reads only `fleet.desired.instances`.
- **Given up:** the ability to make the fleet scale on an assumed capacity in requests per second without a source change.
- **Deployment comparison:** the pin of HEAD's leaves in `scripts/effective-config` stays 140 and `rename-map.json` does not change: the leaf `cli.scale_rps_capacity` remains and is 0 in every family, as before. The tool itself was not run (it builds rc.4 and HEAD); its unit tests were.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/fleet_controller.rs::an_unreadable_router_report_defers_only_the_desired_publication (gains one call of the new helper `assert_reason_ends_with_rate_and_live`, which holds that the last two tokens of the published reason are named `live` and `rps`; red-first: `the reason ends with the measured rate and the live count: cores_used=0.03 util->1 inflight=300 slots->3 hot_cpu=0% (0) ack_p50=0ms (0) edge_p50=0ms (0) rps=0 (0) live=2`, left `(Some("live"), Some("(0)"))`, right `(Some("live"), Some("rps"))`)
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::a_retired_flag_on_argv_is_refused (the table gains the argument; red-first: `("--scale-rps-capacity", Ok(()))` on the left)
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::a_retired_name_in_the_environment_changes_nothing with its helper `retired_names_helper` (the child process holds `SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY=150` and the compared values gain `cli.scale_rps_capacity`; red-first: 150 on the left, 0 on the right)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_surface_is_pinned (loses the row; red-first with the option still declared: `("scale-rps-capacity", "SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY", "0")` in left only)
  - src/config/tests.rs::cli_fixture_matches_scrubbed_parse
  - Not pinned: that no rate can raise the desired count or hold the shrink target. The field and the computation are deleted, so no configuration can express the dimension and no test can set it; the pin is the reason string, which can only name the needs that exist. The values of `rps=` and `live=` are not asserted, only the names of the two tokens.
- **Residue:** the field `CliArgs::scale_rps_capacity` (always 0) and `cap=0 rps` in the boot line, for the change in which the owner next updates the exception rows of `bootstrap::run` (with the residue of edge record #80, so that TLA-011 and the bootstrap mutation owner are paid once). Removed on 2026-09-30 on the owner's instruction ("Please do the cleanup"), together with the residue of #80: the field is gone, the boot line reads "fleet coordination on (prefix=P)", and the rows of `bootstrap::run` were updated (`scope_lines` 583 -> 573, `syntax_facts` 977 -> 968); `cli.scale_rps_capacity` is declared as removed in `rename-map.json`. The comment in `src/fleet/heartbeat.rs` that names `SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY` is history and still true; it is not edited, which keeps the `fleet_heartbeat` mutation owner out of this change.
- **For the owner:**
  1. The audit asked whether one option is worth an update of the `bootstrap::run` rows, a TLA-011 re-record and a fleet mutation leg, or should ride with the next change to those files. It was taken here without the rows: `bootstrap.rs` is not edited, TLA-011 goes stale through `src/fleet.rs` (12.1 min to re-record), and CI selects the `fleet` mutation owner and Miri.
  2. The audit asked: remove outright, or accept and ignore with a notice. Removed outright, as edge records #74, #80 and #81 did.
  3. Whether a live Compute project still holds `SCALE_RPS_CAPACITY` above 0; the platform export answers it before the binary is deployed.
- **Risk reason:** low: no route, status, code, header or field of an answer changes; the value of one string in the operator payload, `fleet.desired.reason` of `/operator/data.json`, loses a token. The default was off, and no deployment in the repository set the name. The risks are a start that is refused for a process that still passes the argument, and a fleet that silently loses a scale-out floor it still holds; the only recorded use of that floor is the incident that retired it. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the library suite, the release profile, the rigs that start the binary (the platform e2e among them), the effective-configuration comparison, Miri and the mutation leg of the `fleet` owner (both selected by CI for this change), and the re-record of TLA-011. WIRE-MATRIX needs no edit: its entry for `/operator/data.json` names the member `desired`, not the layout of `reason`, and the fleet document keeps its fields and their types. RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent review of 2026-09-29 corrected this record in three places: "Endpoint" said "None on the wire" and now names `GET /operator/data.json`, which relays the fleet document; "Who is affected" called `bench/fleet/observe-fleet.py` the only reader of the string in the repository and now names the server's relay; "Risk reason" said that no answer on the wire and no body changes. The surface stays process, the primary effect being the refused argument; whether the record belongs under operator-debug is the owner's to decide with the ratification, and the counts would move with it. Corrected with it, outside this record: the `SCALE_LATENCY_MS` row of RUNBOOK §3.5 still explained the ack-latency dimension by an rps signal that scales out, which no configuration has any more; the body of `GET /operator/runbook`, which "Endpoint" now names as #68 and #79 do, changes with that row as well.

### #84 ad4953fa — The history compactor is always on: HISTORY_COMPACTOR is not read

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 5 of `config-simplification.md` (switches with one live path), its fourth row. Owner answer of 2026-09-29: "For other questions, please make your own judgement call." The answer contains no explicit approval of this package; the removal was made on that delegation. The audit of the setting (`evidence/config-audit-2026-09-29/detail.md`, `HISTORY_COMPACTOR`) put one question to the owner and named one thing it could not determine; both are listed under "For the owner" below.
- **Surface:** process
- **Endpoint:** None on the wire. Process startup: the environment name `HISTORY_COMPACTOR` (it never had a command-line argument, so clap's surface and `--help` do not change), and the key `history.compactor_off` of the startup summary (a log line).
- **Condition:** A process holds `HISTORY_COMPACTOR=off`. Any other value, and an absent name, meant "on" before and mean nothing now.
- **Before:**
  - Without `MEMPROFILE_CERT`: the process started without a message. Every history database (the per-stream layout and the shared partitions of layout 2) was opened with no embedded compactor (`compactor_options: None`) and with L0 caps of 1,000,000 (`l0_max_ssts`, `l0_max_ssts_per_key`), so its L0 files were never consolidated and a flush never waited for compaction.
  - With `MEMPROFILE_CERT=compute-1g`, which the 1 GiB profile sets for every Compute family: the process refused to start, with `MEMPROFILE_CERT: history_v1 settings disable the compactor` and the same line for `history_v2`.
  - The startup summary printed `history.compactor_off`, `false` in every deployment of the repository.
- **After:**
  - The environment name is not read. A process that holds it starts as a process that does not, whatever the value; nothing is logged.
  - Every history database is opened with the embedded compactor on the worker options the engine resolved (by default one compaction at a time, polled every 2.5 s, 32 MiB output SSTs) and with L0 caps of 64: what every process without the name already ran.
  - A process that holds `HISTORY_COMPACTOR=off` together with `MEMPROFILE_CERT=compute-1g` starts, where it was refused.
  - The startup summary has no key `history.compactor_off`.
  - Unchanged: the guard of the certificate (`settings disable the compactor`) stays, because `compactor_options` is still an `Option` in SlateDB's `Settings`; no configuration reaches it for the history families now.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Nobody in the repository: no profile, family, rig, bench, test, workflow or deploy script sets the name (`git grep`; outside `src` it appeared only in the review documents). The effective-configuration evidence of 2026-09-24 shows `history.compactor_off` false in all 12 families on both sides. A Compute project keeps every name it was ever given: a certified project that holds `HISTORY_COMPACTOR=off` cannot be running today (its start is refused) and would start on its next deploy; an uncertified process that holds it gets the compactor back without a message. Whether such a project exists is visible only in the platform export (NEXT-WORK §11), which has not been run. A reader of the startup summary that expects the key finds it absent; nothing in the repository reads it.
- **Given up:** the bench hook for discard-mode runs. `s3lite --discard-substr` drops the bodies of matching PUTs; the switch existed so that history SSTs whose bodies were dropped were never read again by a compactor. With this binary such a run compacts history L0 files and fails when it reads a discarded body (read from the code: the emulator answers a GET of a discarded body with 500; no such run was made). No script pairs the two; a discard-mode benchmark of the history path now needs a source change. The help of `s3lite --discard-substr` still offered the mode for "an unbounded history tier" after this commit; it now says that the mode is not for the server's history databases.
- **Deployment comparison:** `history.compactor_off` is declared as a removed leaf in `scripts/effective-config/rename-map.json`, with its proposal in `proposed.json`; the pin of HEAD's leaves goes from 140 to 139. The tool itself was not run (it builds rc.4 and HEAD); its unit tests were.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::the_history_compactor_cannot_be_switched_off (new; with `HISTORY_COMPACTOR=off` loaded, `history_settings` and `history2_settings` both return L0 caps of 64 and compactor options of 2,500 ms, one compaction and 32 MiB output SSTs; red-first: left `((1000000, 1000000, None), (1000000, 1000000, None))`, right `((64, 64, Some((2500, 1, Some(33554432)))), (64, 64, Some((2500, 1, Some(33554432)))))`)
  - src/config/retired_tests.rs::retired_environment_names_change_nothing (the table gains `("HISTORY_COMPACTOR", "off")`; red-first: the two configurations differ in `compactor_off: true` on the left, `compactor_off: false` on the right)
  - src/config/tests.rs::default_values_are_pinned (loses the assertion on the removed field)
  - scripts/effective-config/test_effective_config.py::PinTest::test_new_leaf_pin_is_the_source_field_count (red-first after the field was removed and before the pin was lowered: `AssertionError: 139 != 140`)
  - Not pinned: the absence of the key from the startup summary. The field is deleted, so the summary cannot name it.
- **For the owner:**
  1. The audit asked for approval to delete a bench hook that discard-mode benchmarks may still want. It is deleted here; see "Given up".
  2. The audit asked for approval of the re-record of three receipts. `src/history.rs` feeds TLA-016, TLA-018 and TLA-019 (103.8 min serial). All three were stale on the branch before this change, through the removal of the gather pacing (edge record #81) and the changes under `src/shard`, so this change adds no receipt to the re-record that is already due.
  3. The audit could not determine whether a Compute project still holds the name; the platform export answers it before the binary is deployed.
- **Risk reason:** low: no answer on the wire changes, the compactor was on by default, no deployment in the repository turned it off, and a certified deployment could not. The risk is an uncertified process that relied on the switch and now compacts; nothing in the repository is one. Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the library suite, the release profile, the rigs that start the binary, the effective-configuration comparison, and the re-record of TLA-016, TLA-018 and TLA-019. CI selects neither a mutation leg nor Miri for the files of this change. WIRE-MATRIX has no entry (no route, status, code, header or body changes). RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change. An independent review of 2026-09-29 found no false statement in this record. It found that the commit left the help text of `s3lite --discard-substr` (`src/bin/s3lite.rs`) advertising the use that "Given up" says is gone; the help is corrected and "Given up" says so.

### #86 eb39fab6 — The gather's pace time is not reported: gather_last_pace_ms and absorber.lastPaceMs are gone with the counter nothing wrote

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §13, package 5 of `config-simplification.md` (switches with one live path), its first row: the second commit of that row's plan, the residue of #81. Made on the owner's instruction of 2026-09-30 in chat ("Please do the cleanup"), which authorised updating the exception row of `collect_snapshot` (`docs/quality/exception-growth.json`) to the value the gate measures; the row's `approver` records that instruction.
- **Surface:** operator-debug
- **Endpoint:** `GET /v1/debug/load` (bearer-gated with the rest of `/v1/debug`, #39): the top-level member `gather_last_pace_ms`. `GET /v1/debug/absorb`: the member `absorber.lastPaceMs`. The `_ops_metrics` snapshot (`collect_snapshot`, SPEC §11): the gauge `gather_last_pace_ms`, in the record `emit_metrics_once` appends to the `_ops_metrics` system stream under the usage key and in the snapshot the alert evaluator reads. No product, raw or fleet-internal route.
- **Condition:** Every answer of the two routes; every ops snapshot.
- **Before:** The two members and the gauge were present and always 0 since #81 (b4509415), which removed the pacing and left the counter `GATHER_LAST_PACE_MS` with nothing writing it. Before #81 they carried the milliseconds the last gather had parked between read waves.
- **After:**
  - `GET /v1/debug/load` has no member `gather_last_pace_ms`; `GET /v1/debug/absorb` has no member `absorber.lastPaceMs`; the snapshot has no gauge `gather_last_pace_ms`. The counter is deleted from `src/history.rs`.
  - `gather_last_read_ms`, `absorber.lastReadMs` and every other member and gauge keep their names, types and places; the read phase of the last gather is reported as before. No status, code or header changes.
- **Retry semantics:** None.
- **Who is affected:** Operators and tools that read one of the two routes or the `_ops_metrics` records by member name. A reader that treats an absent member as 0 sees no difference, since the value was 0; a reader that requires the key fails. In the repository nothing reads either name: no script under `bench/` (the wc sampler reads `/v1/debug/load` for other members), `scripts/`, `sdk/` or `deploy/` (`git grep`); the copies under `docs/reviews/2026-09-hardening/evidence/` hold the old shape and are history. On Compute the operator page (`/operator`) reads neither route for these members.
- **Given up:** Nothing beyond what #81 gave up: the value had been a constant 0 since #81, and the pacing experiment already needed a source change.
- **Deployment comparison:** Not applicable: no setting changes (no argument, environment name or field of the configuration; the pin of HEAD's leaves in `scripts/effective-config` stays 132 and `rename-map.json` does not change).
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/history_gather.rs::gather_reporters_carry_the_read_phase_and_no_pace_time (new: over one HTTP rig, `gather_last_read_ms` is an integer on `/v1/debug/load`, `absorber.lastReadMs` on `/v1/debug/absorb` and `gather_last_read_ms` a gauge of `collect_snapshot`, while `gather_last_pace_ms` and `absorber.lastPaceMs` are absent from all three. Red-first on the unfixed tree: `assertion `left == right` failed: the read phase is reported and the pace time is absent`, left `(true, false, true, false, true, true)`, right `(true, true, true, true, true, false)`)
  - unchanged and green: src/dst/tests/security_routes.rs::debug_surface_serves_every_handler_with_the_token (both routes still answer 200 with an object carrying `inflight_now` and `budget`), src/dst/tests/admission_maintenance.rs::debug_load_reports_typed_limiter_and_frame_totals, the `ops::` unit tests, and the DSTs that call `collect_snapshot` (`runtime_journals`, `fork_debt`, `billing_usage`).
- **Risk reason:** low: no answer changes on any product, raw or fleet-internal route; the two debug routes and the snapshot lose a member whose value was 0 for every process since #81, and nothing in the repository reads it. The risk is a tool outside the repository that requires the key. WIRE-MATRIX: the lines of both routes say the members are gone. Not run for this record: the rigs that start the binary, Miri and the mutation legs of the `ops` and `http` owners (selected by CI for this change). Made on the owner's instruction of 2026-09-30; RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-09-30.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

### #87 9f25cf96 — A billing close or retention flag that the tombstone walk or the closure-debt pass enqueues on a shard it opened is applied: the pass keeps the shard until its committer has applied it

- **Program item:** NEXT-WORK §2, the finding recorded there as unconfirmed ("a close enqueued on an engine the walk cold-opened may be dropped when `walk_settle` retires that engine before the committer applies it"). An independent investigation (three analysts, three skeptics, one judge) confirmed it at 88015cc5. Made on the owner's instruction of 2026-09-30 ("Do them all in that order! use your own judgement").
- **Surface:** both
- **Endpoint:** No request changes. What changes: the storage billed for a stream incarnation that ended while its billing row was acknowledged clean (expired or hard-deleted and closed by the tombstone walk, or replaced and closed by the closure-debt pass): `GET /v1/streams/{name}/usage[?month=][&streamId=]` (`storageByteSeconds`, `ownedStoredBytesNow`, `status`, `effective`, `correctionTotals`, `correctionList`) and `GET /v1/projects/{project}/usage[?month=]` (`effective`, `correctionTotals`); the `_usage` ledger's closing snapshot of that row and the rollup's monthly and correction objects built from it. For a soft-deleted source its forks still read, the `retained_by_forks` flag on its row and snapshots. Operator: a shard the walk or the debt pass enqueued an op on stays a scheduler-held resident until the next sweep (`sweep_custody_prefixes`, the sweep's resident budget); `WALK_CLOSE_SUBMITS` still counts enqueues, and each is now applied.
- **Condition:** The walk (`src/billing/walk.rs` `walk_segment`) or the debt pass (`src/billing/replaced.rs` `settle_segment`) cold-opens a shard for a segment, finds its incarnation's row with an open gauge (or, for the walk, a soft-deleted source's row without the retention flag), enqueues the close (or the flag), and the shard holds no durable debt: no dirty row, no month final, no maintenance backlog, no trim debt. These are the rows the two passes exist for: acknowledged clean, then ended with nothing observing it (an idle expiry, a recreation over an idle-expired incarnation, a close lost to a crash or a full queue). Whether the committer applied the op before the retirement depended on whether the probe's scans yielded to it, which depends on the SST layout at the top of the shard's keyspace; on a layout where they do not, the op was lost on every visit. In production a lone op waits in no gather (`pace_min_reqs` 32), so the loss was a race; the pinning tests make it certain with a committer that gathers for 2 s.
- **Before:** Right after the enqueue the pass settled the shard (`walk_settle`). The settle's debt probe reads only the durable dirty index, which a queued op has not written, so it retired the engine, and the retiring committer dropped the op without a reply, a log line or a counter (the drain arm of `committer_loop`, `reject_op`, or `CommitTransaction::run` on a closed engine). The row stayed byte for byte as it was: gauge open, accounted-through at the last observation, no new version, no dirty marker. The drain emitted nothing, and every month the rollup closed carried the dead incarnation's gauge onto that month's invoice: storage billed for a stream that no longer existed. A later visit re-submitted the close at the same persisted instant: the debt pass when its cursor came back to the debt (the next sweep while the debts fit one page), the walk only after its cursor went round the whole cell catalog (ceil(N/256) sweeps of 300 s), and on a layout where the probe never yields every visit lost it again. A close that finally landed closed at the persisted instant, and the late-close path (#66) corrected the months already invoiced: the final amount was exact, but the carried months needed corrections. A lost retention flag left a soft-deleted source's row unflagged until a later visit; its gauge kept billing, but the flag that carries the fork billing contract across restarts and ownership moves was missing from the row.
- **After:** A step that enqueued a close or a retention flag keeps the shard. It stays scheduler-held, its committer applies the op (a close zeroes the gauge at the persisted instant, takes one version and marks the row dirty for the drain), and the next sweep's phase 1 keeps it as an indebted resident while the drain acknowledges the row, or evicts it with the debt durable. A step that enqueued nothing (no row, another incarnation's row, a closed gauge, a failed read or submit) hands the shard back as before. A close bills what it would have billed whenever it landed: the persisted instant, not the time it ran. A walk that closes on many distinct cold shards keeps up to the sweep's resident budget (`sweep_maint_resident`, default 2) of them and defers the rest to the next sweep, the pace it already had whenever the applied close was visible to the probe.
- **Retry semantics:** None at the HTTP level. The passes still re-submit a close they find open, at the same persisted instant, and the committer still skips a close that would change nothing (#72).
- **Who is affected:** Customers whose streams expired, were hard-deleted or were replaced while their rows were clean: storage is no longer billed past the close instant for the months until a revisit, and no correction is needed for months already invoiced. Invoice consumers: fewer negative storage corrections from late closes (#66). Operators: a sweep may keep up to its resident budget of such shards one sweep longer. No status, code, header or body shape changes.
- **Pinning tests:**
  - src/dst/tests/billing_walk_custody.rs::a_close_the_walk_submits_on_a_shard_it_opened_is_applied_before_the_shard_is_handed_back (new, red-first in 5 of 5 runs: `the walk handed back the shard it opened before its committer applied the close it enqueued there`, left `(81, 1790772820470, 0, (2026, 9), 1)`, right `(0, 1790772820529, 4779, (2026, 9), 2)`)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_walk_custody.rs::a_close_the_debt_pass_submits_on_a_shard_it_opened_is_applied_before_the_shard_is_handed_back (new, red-first in 5 of 5 runs: `the debt pass handed back the shard it opened before its committer applied the close it enqueued there`, left `(81, 1790772812887, 0, (2026, 9), 1)`, right `(0, 1790772812950, 5103, (2026, 9), 2)`)
  - src/dst/tests/billing_walk_custody.rs::a_retention_flag_the_walk_submits_on_a_shard_it_opened_is_applied_before_the_shard_is_handed_back (new, red-first in 5 of 5 runs: `the walk handed back the shard it opened before its committer applied the retention flag it enqueued there`, left `(false, 1)`, right `(true, 2)`)
  - unchanged and green: ::a_shard_the_walk_opened_for_nothing_to_close_is_handed_back_before_the_next_segment (30 of 30 runs alone; its CI flake, gauge 81 still open with accounted-through 55 ms before the expiry, matches this race), and the `billing_closure_debts`, `billing_closure_owners`, `billing_controller` and `runtime_sweep` DSTs and the `billing::` unit tests.
- **Risk reason:** low: no status, code, header or body shape changes and no stored format changes; the only billed figures that change are storage past a close instant that a lost op left open, which the late-close corrections reversed once the close landed. WIRE-MATRIX has no entry: no wire surface changes. What remains: phase 1 still decides from the durable index, so an op still unapplied a whole sweep interval later would meet the same drop, and the exact fix (a reply on the committer op) is for the owner (NEXT-WORK §2). Not run for this record: `scripts/gate.sh`, the release profile, the mutation legs of the `billing_walk` and `billing_replaced` owners (selected by CI for this change) and the platform e2e rigs. Made on the owner's instruction of 2026-09-30 ("Do them all in that order! use your own judgement"); RATIFIED by the owner on 2026-10-01.
- **Check against commit:** written with the change.

## Discrepancies

One record has verified=false: its commit did not match the recorded text.

### #45 07db91a7 — Scaler cooldown and merge patience saturate instead of wrapping

- **Risk:** low
- **Surface:** fleet-internal
- **Finding:** The recorded text says inf means 'never re-scale'. In the code, inf means never scale at all: under SCALE_COOLDOWN_SECS=inf, a stream that has never transitioned is also blocked (its sentinel age of about 2^62 ms is below cooldown_ms = i64::MAX), so no first split or merge ever happens. This matches how any cooldown of about 4.6e15 s or more already behaved before. No test pins the fresh-stream case; the new test only seeds streams with a transition at 0. Secondary: the commit's merge test doc says SCALE_HOT_EVALS=inf 'used to wrap that patience to 0'. In release, u32::MAX\*4 wraps to 4,294,967,292, which is effectively never merge; inf only panicked in debug. The always-merge wrap happens for values near multiples of 2^30, such as the 1\<\<30 the test uses. The recorded wording 'a huge SCALE_HOT_EVALS' is correct for those values.

For #45, the Before and After entries in the low-risk section already describe the behaviour found in the code: under `SCALE_COOLDOWN_SECS=inf` no split or merge happens at all, including a stream's first. The mismatch is with the item's recorded wording ("never re-scale") and with the commit's merge-test doc comment about `SCALE_HOT_EVALS=inf`.

Every other record matched its commit. Where the check found precision points, or effects the original record did not state, they appear under "Check against commit" in that record's subsection.
